{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{K}(\mathsf{Vect}^{\nabla})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{K}(\mathsf{Vect})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\widehat{\mathsf{ku}}^{\nabla}=s\big(\mathsf{K}(\mathsf{Vect}^{\nabla})\big)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\widehat{\mathsf{ku}}=s\big(\mathsf{K}(\mathsf{Vect})\big)}}

Here
s:\mathsf{PSh}\to\mathsf{Sh}
denotes the sheafification functor.

The K-theory machine developed in
[
BNV13
]
is basically a composition,

\displaystyle\mathsf{CAlg}(\mathsf{Cat}(W^{-1}))\to\mathsf{CAlg}(\mathsf{Grpd}(W^{-1}))\to\mathsf{ComMon}(s\mathsf{Set}[W^{-1})

\displaystyle\to\mathsf{ComGrp}(s\mathsf{Set}[W^{-1})\simeq\mathsf{Sp}_{\geq 0}\to\mathsf{Sp}

where we

(1)

First take the groupoid underlying
\mathsf{Cat}
.

(2)

Applying nerve to get a commutative monoid in the category of spaces
s\mathsf{Set}[W^{-1}]
, i.e. an
E_{\infty}
-space.

(3)

Then applying the group completion, we get a commutative group in the category of spaces
s\mathsf{Set}[W^{-1}]
, i.e. a grouplike
E_{\infty}
-space.

(4)

Finally, we apply the functor which maps a commutative group in spaces to the corresponding connective spectrum whose
\infty
-group is this group.

For more details about the K-theory machine
\mathsf{K}
, see
[
BNV13
, Definition 6.1, Remark 6.4]
.
Let
L
and
\mathcal{H}^{{\operatorname{flat}}}
denote the sheafification and homotopification functors.

Definition 19.42
.

We define the following sheaves of spectra

\displaystyle\mathbf{K}=

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{{\operatorname{flat}}}(L(\mathsf{K}(\mathsf{Vect}^{{\operatorname{flat}}})))\in\mathsf{Sh}_{{\mathcal{S}}}^{h}(\mathsf{Mfd}^{{{\mathbb{C}}}{\operatorname{SFol}}})

\displaystyle\mathbf{K}^{\nabla}=

\displaystyle L(\mathsf{K}(\mathsf{Vect}^{{\operatorname{flat}}},\nabla))\in\mathsf{Sh}_{{\mathcal{S}}}(\mathsf{Mfd}^{{{\mathbb{C}}}{\operatorname{SFol}}})

and for
i\in{\mathbb{Z}}
, we define the algebraic
K
-theory of a singular foliation
(M,{\mathcal{F}})
by

K^{i}(M,{\mathcal{F}})=\pi_{-i}(\mathbf{K}(M,{\mathcal{F}}))

Note that
\mathsf{K}(\mathsf{Vect}^{{\operatorname{flat}}})
is a homotopy invariant, hence we expect that homotopification will preserve this invariance. Therefore, the homotopification might not be necessary.

20.
Higher groupoids arised in singular foliations

20.1.
Leaf spaces of singular foliations and Čech
\infty
-groupoids

Recall that for a foliated manifold
(M,{\mathcal{F}})
, the leaf space of
{\mathcal{F}}
is a space
{\mathcal{F}}
which is a quotient of
M
by identifying points within the same leaves. We want to construct a smooth model for the leaf space of a singular foliation.

Definition 20.1
.

We define a
transversal basis
for
(M,{\mathcal{F}})
as a family
\mathcal{U}
of slices
U
such that given any slice
V
at
x
, we can find a
U
at
y
and
x,y
lying in the same leaf, and there exists a holonomy embedding
h:V\hookrightarrow U
.

Given a point
x
, we can take a

Definition 20.2
.

Let
(M,{\mathcal{F}})
be a singular foliated manifold and
\mathcal{U}
a transversal basis of for
(M,{\mathcal{F}})
. We define the
Čech
\infty
-groupoid

\operatorname{\check{C}ech}_{\bullet}({\mathcal{F}})
whose
k
-simplices are

\operatorname{\check{C}ech}({\mathcal{F}})_{k}=\coprod_{U_{0}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle h_{1}}}{{\to}}\cdots\stackrel{{\scriptstyle h_{k}}}{{\to}}U_{k}}U_{0}

where
h_{i}:U_{i-1}\to U_{i}
are holonomy embeddings and
U_{i}\in\mathcal{U}
for all
i
.

There structure maps
d_{i}
’s and
s_{i}
’s are defined as

Definition 20.3
.

For a regular foliation, the standard model for the leaf space is the classifying space of the holonomy groupoid. If
{\mathcal{F}}
is regular, then our construction reduces to
[
CM00
]
, and we have the following isomorphism

Theorem 20.4

(
[
CM00
]
)
.

For a regular foliated-manifold
(M,{\mathcal{F}})
. There is a natural isomorphism

\check{H}^{\bullet}_{\mathcal{U}}(M/{\mathcal{F}})\simeq H^{\bullet}(B\operatorname{Hol}(M{\mathcal{F}});{\mathbb{R}})

between the Čech-de Rham cohomology and the cohomology of the classifying space of the holonomy groupoid. The left hand side is independent of the choice of
\mathcal{U}
.

Hence, we can regard
\operatorname{\check{C}ech}({\mathcal{F}})_{\bullet}
as a model for the leaf space of a singular foliated manifold
(M,{\mathcal{F}})
.

20.2.
Holonomy
\infty
-groupoids

In this section, we are going to construct a higher groupoid model which extends the holonomy groupoids in the sense of Androulidakis and Skandalis.

Recall that, when we construct the groupoid associated to an atlas of bisubmersions, we take the quotient of equivalence relations induced by local morphisms of bisubmersions. This is the reason we get a crappy arrow space for the holonomy groupoid. One natural idea is that, instead of brutally quotient the (local) equivalence, we keep the gluing data, and take a ’nerve’ similar to the case when we construct classifying spaces.

Let
(M,{\mathcal{F}})
be a singular-foliated manifold. In this section, we fix the atlas
\mathcal{U}=(U_{i},s_{i},t_{i})_{i\in I}
to be the path holonomy atlas of
(M,{\mathcal{F}})
.

Definition 20.5
.

We say a morphism between bisubmersions
f:(U,s_{U},t_{U})\to(V,s_{V},t_{V})
is an
equivalence
if there exists a morphism
g:(V,s_{V},t_{V})\to(U,s_{U},t_{U})
. We say
f
is an
isomorphism
if
f\circ g=\operatorname{Id},g\circ f=id
.

We say a morphism between bisubmersions
f:(U,s_{U},t_{U})\to(V,s_{V},t_{V})
is a
local equivalence (isomorphism)
if there exists a morphism
f^{\prime}:U^{\prime}\to V^{\prime}
where
U^{\prime}\subset U,V^{\prime}\subset V
which is an equivalence (isomorphism).

First, let’s recall the following lemma about the local morphisms.

Lemma 20.6

(
[
AS06
]
)
.

Let
(U,s_{U},t_{U}),(V,s_{V},t_{V})
be bisubmersions and let
u\in U,v\in V
with
s_{U}(u)=s_{V}(v)
. Then:

(1)

If the identity local diffeomorphism is carried by
U
at
u
and by
V
at
v
, then there exists an open neighborhood
U^{\prime}
of
u
in
U
, and a morphism
f:U^{\prime}\to V
such that
f(u)=v
.

(2)

If there exists a local diffeomorphism is carried by
U
at
u
and by
V
at
v
, then there exists an open neighborhood
U^{\prime}
of
u
in
U
, and a morphism
f:U^{\prime}\to V
such that
f(u)=v
.

(3)

If there exists a morphism of bisubmersions
f:U\to V
such that
f(u)=v
, then there exists an open neighborhood
V^{\prime}
of
v
in
V
, and a morphism
g:V^{\prime}\to U
such that
g(v)=u
.

Lemma 20.7
.

Let
(U,s_{U},t_{U}),(V,s_{V},t_{V})
be bisubmersions and let
u\in U,v\in V
with
s_{U}(u)=s_{V}(v)
, then any local morphisms around
u
are local equivalences.

Proof.

Let
f^{\prime}:U^{\prime}\to V
denote the morphism induced by
f
. Applying the previous lemma, we can restrict to an open neighborhood
V^{\prime}\subset V
with a morphism
g:V^{\prime}\to U^{\prime}
such that
g(v)=u
, which realizes a local equivalence.
∎

Corollary 20.8
.

Local morphisms are local equivalences.

Proof.

Let
f:(U,s_{U},t_{U})\to(V,s_{V},t_{V})
be a local morphism. Pick some
u\in U^{\prime}
where
f:U^{\prime}\to V
is the morphism defined by the local morphism
f
, and let

. Then clearly

and we can apply the previous lemma.
∎

Let

Lemma 20.10

Definition 20.11

We say two bisubmersions

Now let’s construct the
holonomy
\infty
-groupoids
which enhances the holonomy groupoids of singular foliations.

Definition 20.13
.

Let
(M,{\mathcal{F}})
be a singular foliation manifold with an atlas
\mathcal{U}=(U_{i},s_{i},t_{i})_{i\in I}
. We define a simplicial manifold
\operatorname{Hol}^{\infty}_{\bullet}({\mathcal{F}})
by

\displaystyle\operatorname{Hol}^{\infty}_{k}({\mathcal{F}})

\displaystyle=\coprod_{i_{1},\cdots,i_{k}\in I}U_{i_{1}}\times_{s_{U_{i_{1}}},t_{U_{i_{2}}}}U_{i_{2}}\times\cdots\times U_{i_{k-1}}\times_{s_{U_{i_{k-1}}},t_{U_{i_{k}}}}U_{i_{k}}

\displaystyle k\geq 1

\displaystyle\operatorname{Hol}^{\infty}_{1}({\mathcal{F}})

\displaystyle=M

with face maps generated by

\displaystyle d_{l}(u_{1},u_{2}\cdots,u_{k})

\displaystyle=(u_{2}\cdots,u_{k})

\displaystyle l=0

\displaystyle d_{l}(u_{1},u_{2}\cdots,u_{k})

\displaystyle=(u_{1},u_{2}\cdots,u_{l-1},(u_{l},u_{l+1}),\cdots,u_{k})

\displaystyle 1\leq l\leq k-1

\displaystyle d_{l}(u_{1},u_{2}\cdots,u_{k})

\displaystyle=(u_{1},u_{2}\cdots,u_{k-1})

\displaystyle l=k

where
(u_{l},u_{l+1})\in U_{i_{l}}\times_{{s_{U_{i_{l}}}},t_{U_{i_{l+1}}}}U_{i_{l+1}}
.

Proposition 20.14
.

\operatorname{Hol}^{\infty}_{\bullet}({\mathcal{F}})
is a Lie
\infty
-groupoid.

Proof.

Note that given any length
k
fiber product

U_{i_{1}}\times\cdots\times U_{i_{k}}

we can get its inverse simply by

U_{i_{k}}\times\cdots\times U_{i_{1}}

with all
s_{i_{l}}
and
t_{i_{l}}
switched, i.e. we take the inverse of each
(U_{i},s_{i},s_{j})
and then take the fiber product in the reverse order. This implies that all
k
-simplices are invertible. In particular, given a horn

\Lambda^{k}[n]\to\operatorname{Hol}^{\infty}_{\bullet}({\mathcal{F}})

we get a map
f:u_{1}\to u_{k}
as an element of
U_{i_{1}}\times\cdots U_{i_{k}}

, a map
g:u_{k}\to u_{n}
as an element of
U_{i_{k+1}}\times\cdots U_{i_{n}}
, and a map
h:u_{n}\to u_{1}
as an element of
U_{i_{n}}\times U_{i_{1}}
. Now we can simply take
\phi=g^{-1}\circ h\circ f^{-1}
, which gives the desired horn filling. Hence, the induced map

\operatorname{Hol}^{\infty}_{n}({\mathcal{F}})\to M_{\Lambda^{k}[n]}\operatorname{Hol}^{\infty}_{\bullet}({\mathcal{F}})

is clearly a surjective submersion.
∎

We call
\operatorname{Hol}^{\infty}_{\bullet}({\mathcal{F}})
the
holonomy
\infty
-groupoid
of the singular foliation
{\mathcal{F}}
. Next, we shall justify the correctness of our construction.

Proposition 20.15
.

The 1-truncation
\tau^{\leq 1}\operatorname{Hol}^{\infty}_{\bullet}({\mathcal{F}})
of the holonomy
\infty
-groupoid is equivalent to the holonomy groupoid
\operatorname{Hol}({\mathcal{F}})
in the sense of Androulidakis and Skandalis.

Proof.

Recall that the arrow space of the holonomy groupoid is the quotient of path holonomy atlas by the relation such that
u\in U_{i}
is equivalent to
v\in U_{j}
if there exists a local morphism from
U_{i}\to U_{j}
which takes
u
to
v
. Now suppose
u\sim v
for
u\in U_{i},v\in U_{j}
, and there exists a local morphism
f:U_{i}\to U_{j}
send
u
to
v
. Then by Lemma
20.12
,
(v,u)\in U_{j}^{-1}\times_{t_{U_{j}},t_{U_{i}}}U_{i}=W
and there exists a local morphism near
(v,u)
which maps to identity diffeomorphisms near
s_{U_{j}}(v)
. In addition, at
(v,u)
,

s_{W}\big((v,u)\big)=t_{U_{j}}(v)=t_{U_{i}}(u)=t_{W}\big((v,u)\big)

∎

21.
Holomorphic singular foliations

21.1.
L_{\infty}
-algebroids for holomorphic singular foliations

Let
(X,{\mathcal{F}})
be a holomorphic singular foliation, i.e.
{\mathcal{F}}
is a locally finitely generated involutive coherent
{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
-module. Since
T_{X}
has a natural
L_{\infty}
structure (indeed Lie structure),
{\mathcal{F}}
is closed with this
L_{\infty}
structure. Denote the Dolbeault dga
(A^{0,\bullet},\overline{\partial},0)
by
A
. Recall that a sheaf
{\mathcal{S}}
of
{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}(X)
-modules is called
\overline{\partial}
-analytic coherent
if
{\mathcal{S}}
locally admits a resolution by finitely generated free modules and equips with a flat
\overline{\partial}
-connection.

By a simple application of Hilbert’s Syzygy theorem, we have that
{\mathcal{F}}
admits a local resolution.

Proposition 21.1

(
[
LLS20
]
)
.

Any holomorphic singular foliations on a complex manifold
X
of dimension
n
locally admits a finite resolution by finitely generated
free
{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
-modules of length
\leq n
.

Equivalently, we can regard
X
has local resolution by trivial vector bundles.

The
\overline{\partial}
-connection is directly inherited the flat
\overline{\partial}
-connection on the
T_{X}
. Therefore,
{\mathcal{F}}
is
\overline{\partial}
-analytic coherent
. In (3), the bundle
F
is formed by taking all tangent vectors tangent to leaves, whereas in (4) a
k
-forms is in
\mathcal{J}
if it vanishes on any
k
-tuples of vectors tangent to leaves.
Laurent-Gengoux, Lavau, Strob in
[
LLS20
]
considered the case when a singular foliation on a smooth manifold admits a (global) resolution by vector bundles, and they proved that we can always construct an
L_{\infty}
-algebroid associated to that singular foliation:

Theorem 21.2

(
[
LLS20
]
)
.

Give a foliation
{\mathcal{F}}
which admits a resolution by vector bundles
{\mathcal{F}}_{\bullet}
, there exists a universal
L_{\infty}
-algebroid
\mathfrak{g}\in\mathsf{L_{\infty}Algd}^{\operatorname{dg}}_{{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}{M}}
whose linear part is the given resolution
{\mathcal{F}}_{\bullet}
. Here universal means that
\mathfrak{g}
is the terminal object in the category of
\mathsf{L_{\infty}Algd}^{\operatorname{dg}}_{{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}{M}}/{\mathcal{F}}
which consists of
L_{\infty}
-algebroids resolving
{\mathcal{F}}
.

We would like to generalize this to holomorphic singular foliations.
Though we don’t have global resolutions for holomorphic singular foliations, we can glue the local resolutions by higher homotopical information
[
TT76
]
[
TT78
]
[
Blo05
]
[
Wei16
]
. As natural to coherent sheaves on complex manifolds, we will use the Dolbeault enhancement introduced by Block in
[
Blo05
]
:

Theorem 21.3

(
[
Blo05
]
)
.

Let
X
be a complex manifold, and
\mathfrak{g}=T^{0,1}X
be the Dolbeault Lie algebroid. The homotopy category of the dg-category
\mathsf{Mod}^{Coh}_{\operatorname{CE}(\mathfrak{g})}=\mathtt{Rep}_{\mathfrak{g},A}
is equivalent to the bounded derived category of chain complexes of sheaves of
{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
-modules with coherent cohomology on

X

As a corollary, for any coherent analytic sheaf
{\mathcal{F}}
, there exists a cohesive module
(E,\mathbb{E})\in\mathsf{Mod}^{Coh}_{A}
, which is unique up to quasi-isomorphism, corresponds to
{\mathcal{F}}
. In fact, take
{\mathcal{F}}^{\infty}={\mathcal{F}}\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}_{X}}{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}(X)
which is the
\overline{\partial}
-coherent sheaf associated with
{\mathcal{F}}
. we have a projective resolution

0\to E_{-n}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d_{n}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\cdots\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d_{2}}}{{\longrightarrow}}E_{-1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d_{1}}}{{\longrightarrow}}E_{0}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rho}}{{\longrightarrow}}{\mathcal{F}}^{\infty}\longrightarrow 0

Tensoring the above sequence with the Dolbeault dga gives a resolution
E_{\bullet}\otimes_{{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}(X)}A\to{\mathcal{F}}^{\infty}\otimes_{{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}(X)}A
. Denote
{\mathcal{F}}^{\infty}\otimes_{{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}(X)}A
by
F
. We can equip the dg-
A
-module
{\mathcal{E}}_{\bullet}=E_{\bullet}\otimes_{{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}(X)}A
with a
{\mathbb{Z}}
-connection
\nabla
and then get a cohesive module. Denote the associated map
E_{0}\to 0
by
d_{0}
.

21.2.
Lifting dg-
A
-Module structure to
L_{\infty}
-algebroid structure

We shall construct
L_{\infty}
-structure on
{\mathcal{E}}_{\bullet}
following similar methods in
[
Bar+97
]
and
[
LLS20
]
.

For simplicity, sometimes we will denote the
n
-ary bracket
[-,\cdots,-]_{n}
by
l_{n}
. We regard
F\in\mathsf{Mod}^{\operatorname{dg}}_{A}
concentrated in degree zero, then
\rho
naturally extends to a chain map. Let
C_{\bullet}
and
B_{\bullet}
denote the
d
-cycles and
d
-boundaries, respectively. We have
F\simeq H_{0}({\mathcal{E}}_{\bullet})
. The existence of contracting homotopy
s:{\mathcal{E}}_{\bullet}\to{\mathcal{E}}_{\bullet-1}
specifies a homotopy inverse
\delta:F\to{\mathcal{E}}_{\bullet}
.

21.2.1.
Construction of
l_{2}

First, we want to construct
l_{2}
on
{\mathcal{E}}_{0}
.

Lemma 21.4
.

There exists a skew-symmetric
A
-linear map
\tilde{l}_{2}:{\mathcal{E}}_{0}\otimes{\mathcal{E}}_{0}\to{\mathcal{E}}_{0}
satisfying

(1)

\tilde{l}_{2}(c_{1},b_{1})=0

(2)

[c_{1},a\cdot c_{2}]=a[c_{1},c_{2}]+\rho(c_{1})(a)c_{2}

(3)

\sum_{\sigma\in\operatorname{UnSh}(2,1)}(-1)^{\sigma}\tilde{l}_{2}(\tilde{l}_{2}(c_{\sigma(1)},c_{\sigma(2)}),c_{\sigma(3)})\in B_{0}

where
c_{i}\in{\mathcal{E}}_{0}
,
b_{i}\in B_{0}
,
a\in A
.

Proof.

Define
\tilde{l}_{2}=\delta\circ[-,-]\circ(\rho\otimes\rho)
. It is clearly skew-symmetric. Property (1) is satisfied since
\rho(b_{1})=0
.
We claim that
[-,-]=\rho\circ\tilde{l}_{2}\circ(\delta\otimes\delta)
. In fact,

d_{0}\circ\tilde{l}_{2}\circ(\delta\otimes\delta)=d_{0}\circ\delta\circ[-,-]\circ(\rho\otimes\rho)\circ(\delta\otimes\delta)

since
d_{0}\circ\delta=\operatorname{Id}
the result follows.

For (2), we want to construct
\delta
explicitly. In fact, pick an open neighborhood
U\subset X
such that
F|_{U}
is finitely generated by
\{f_{i}\otimes a_{j}\}
. Pick
\{e_{i}\otimes a_{j}\}
such that
\rho{(e_{i}\otimes a_{j})}=f_{i}\otimes a_{j}
. Then we can define
[e_{i},e_{j}]=\sum_{l=1}^{k}c_{ij}^{l}e_{k}
where
c_{ij}^{l}
comes from
[f_{i},f_{j}]=\sum_{l=1}^{k}c_{ij}^{l}f_{k}
. Extend the brackets to all
E_{0}
by Leibniz rules, i.e.

[e_{i},a\cdot e_{j}]=a[e_{i},e_{j}]+\rho(e_{i})(a)e_{j}

. Note that here we regard
\rho(e_{i}\otimes a_{j})\in F={\mathcal{F}}\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}_{X}}A
sits inside
T_{X}\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}_{X}}A\subset\operatorname{Der}_{k}(A)
. Finally, we glue all the local brackets by partition of unity.

Next, we want to show (3) holds. On
{\mathcal{F}}
, the Jacobi identity implies that, for
f_{i}\in{\mathcal{F}}

\sum_{\sigma\in\operatorname{UnSh}(2,1)}(-1)^{\sigma}[[f_{\sigma(1)},f_{\sigma(2)}],f_{\sigma(3)}]=0

Since
[-,-]=\rho\circ\tilde{l}_{2}\circ(\delta\otimes\delta)
, the left hand side becomes

\displaystyle\sum_{\sigma\in\operatorname{UnSh}(2,1)}(-1)^{\sigma}\big(\rho\circ\tilde{l}_{2}\circ(\delta\otimes\delta)\big)\circ\big(\rho\circ(\tilde{l}_{2}\otimes{\mathbb{1}})\circ(\delta\otimes\delta\otimes{\mathbb{1}})\big)(f_{\sigma(1)},f_{\sigma(2)},f_{\sigma(3)})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\sigma\in\operatorname{UnSh}(2,1)}(-1)^{\sigma}\big(\rho\circ\tilde{l}_{2}\circ(\delta\otimes\delta)\big)\circ\big(\rho\circ(\tilde{l}_{2}(\delta f_{\sigma(1)}\otimes\delta f_{\sigma(2)}))\otimes f_{\sigma(3)}\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\sigma\in\operatorname{UnSh}(2,1)}(-1)^{\sigma}\rho\circ\tilde{l}_{2}\Big(\delta\circ\rho\circ(\tilde{l}_{2}(\delta f_{\sigma(1)}\otimes\delta f_{\sigma(2)})),\delta f_{\sigma(3)}\Big)

Note that
d_{0}\circ\delta={\mathbb{1}}_{{\mathcal{F}}}
and there exists a chain homotopy
s:{\mathcal{E}}_{\bullet}\to{\mathcal{E}}_{\bullet-1}
with
\delta\circ d-{\mathbb{1}}_{{\mathcal{E}}_{\bullet}}=s\circ d+d\circ s
. Hence, we get,

\displaystyle\sum_{\sigma\in\operatorname{UnSh}(2,1)}(-1)^{\sigma}\rho\circ\tilde{l}_{2}\Big(({\mathbb{1}}_{{\mathcal{E}}_{0}}+d_{1}\circ s)\circ(\tilde{l}_{2}(\delta f_{\sigma(1)}\otimes\delta f_{\sigma(2)})),\delta f_{\sigma(3)}\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\sigma\in\operatorname{UnSh}(2,1)}(-1)^{\sigma}\rho\circ\tilde{l}_{2}\Big((\tilde{l}_{2}(\delta f_{\sigma(1)}\otimes\delta f_{\sigma(2)})),\delta f_{\sigma(3)}\Big)

\displaystyle+\sum_{\sigma\in\operatorname{UnSh}(2,1)}(-1)^{\sigma}\rho\circ\tilde{l}_{2}\Big(d_{1}\circ s\circ(\tilde{l}_{2}(\delta f_{\sigma(1)}\otimes\delta f_{\sigma(2)})),\delta f_{\sigma(3)}\Big)

By property (1), the second term is 0. Therefore, we have

\rho\Big(\sum_{\sigma\in\operatorname{UnSh}(2,1)}(-1)^{\sigma}\circ\tilde{l}_{2}\Big((\tilde{l}_{2}(\delta f_{\sigma(1)}\otimes\delta f_{\sigma(2)})),\delta f_{\sigma(3)}\Big)\Big)=0

which implies the term inside belongs to
B_{0}
.
∎

Next, we extend
\tilde{l}_{2}
to a chain map
l_{2}:E_{\bullet}\otimes E_{\bullet}\to E_{\bullet}
. Consider
e_{1}\otimes e_{0}\in E_{1}\otimes E_{0}
, then
d(_{1}\otimes e_{0})=de_{1}\otimes e_{0}
. We define
l_{2}\big(d(x_{1}\otimes x_{0})\big)=\tilde{l}_{2}\big(d(x_{1}\otimes x_{0})\big)=\tilde{l}_{2}\big((dx_{1}\otimes x_{0})\big)=0
. Pick some
x_{1}\in B_{1}
such that
dx_{1}=0
, then we define
l_{2}(e_{1}\otimes e_{0})=x_{1}
. Extend this to
E_{0}\otimes E_{1}
skew-symmetrically. Finally, by induction, we can extend
l_{2}
to all
E_{i}\otimes E_{j}
.

It turns out that
l_{1}=d
and
l_{2}
satisfy a higher homotopy identity by introducing a new map
l_{3}
.

Proposition 21.5
.

There exists an almost Lie algebroid structure on
{\mathcal{E}}_{\bullet}
.

Lemma 21.6
.

There exists a degree one skew-symmetric map
l_{3}:\bigotimes_{3}E_{\bullet}\to E_{\bullet}
such that
l_{1}l_{3}+l_{3}l_{1}+l_{2}l_{2}=0
.

Proof.

First let
e=e_{1}\otimes e_{2}\otimes e_{3}
in degree 0, then by previous lemma,
l_{2}l_{2}e=b\in B_{0}
, hence we can find a
z\in E_{1}
with
dz=b
. Now we define
l_{3}(e)=-z
, then
l_{1}l_{3}+l_{3}l_{1}+l_{2}l_{2}=0
since
l_{3}l_{1}(e)=0
.

Next we proceed by induction, suppose the
l_{3}
is constructed up to degree
k
, then
l_{2}l_{2}+l_{3}l_{1}
is then defined for degree
k+1
elements. Compose with
l_{1}
we have
l_{1}(l_{2}l_{2}+l_{3}l_{1})=l_{2}l_{2}l_{1}+l_{1}l_{3}l_{1}
used the fact that
l_{1}
and
l_{2}
commutes. Using the equality on degree
k
, we get
l_{2}l_{2}l_{1}+l_{1}l_{3}l_{1}=-l_{3}l_{1}l_{1}=0
, hence we see
(l_{2}l_{2}+l_{3}l_{1})
on degree
k+1
element is a boundary
b
, then we can define the image of
l_{3}
to be a preimage of
b
under
l_{1}
just as before.
∎

Next, we want to construct
l_{i}
for all
i\geq 3
. The idea is still the same as before. We first consider the degree 0 elements. Suppose we have construct
l_{i}
for
1\leq i<n
which satisfy strong homotopy Jacobi identities. For simplicity, we denote
\sum_{i+j=n+1}(-1)^{i(j-1)}l_{j}l_{i}
which is already summed under appropriate unshuffles. We will use the following lemma:

Lemma 21.7
.

Let
\{l_{i}\}
define an
L_{\infty}
structure, then we have

l_{1}\sum_{i+j=n+1,i,j>1}(-1)^{i(j-1)}l_{j}l_{i}=(-1)^{(n-1)}\sum_{i+j=n+1,i,j,>1}(-1)^{i(j-1)}l_{j}l_{i}l_{1}

Hence on
\bigotimes^{n}X_{0}
, both
l_{1}l_{1}l_{n}
and
l_{1}l_{n}l_{1}
vanishes, therefore we know
l_{1}l_{n}
is a cycle, so we can define
l_{n}
by acyclicity of
E_{\bullet}
.

Next, suppose
l_{n}
has been constructed on all degree
<k
elements.

l_{1}\sum_{i,j>1,i+j=n+1}(-1)^{i(j-1)}l_{j}l_{i}=(-1)^{(n-1)}\sum_{i,j>1,i+j=n+1}(-1)^{i(j-1)}l_{j}l_{i}l_{1}=0

Hence,
\sum_{i,j>1,i+j=n+1}(-1)^{i(j-1)}l_{j}l_{i}
is a cycle
b
in
E_{n-3}
. Let
z\in E_{n-2}
such that
l_{1}z=b
, then with appropriate care of signs from unshuffles, we can define
l_{n}=z
.

Theorem 21.8
.

Given a holomorphic foliation
{\mathcal{F}}
on a compact complex manifold
{\mathcal{F}}
, there exist an
L_{\infty}
-algebroid
\mathfrak{g}
over
A
, where the linear part of
\mathfrak{g}
corresponds to the cohesive module
E^{\bullet}
associated to
{\mathcal{F}}^{\infty}={\mathcal{F}}\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}_{X}}{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}{X}

21.3.
Cofibrant replacement

Since
F\in\mathsf{Mod}^{\operatorname{dg}}_{A}
,
({\mathcal{E}},\nabla)
is essentially a cofibrant replacement in the model category
\mathsf{Mod}^{\operatorname{dg}}_{A}
. Note that we have a Quillen adjunction
\mathsf{L_{\infty}Algd}^{\operatorname{dg}}_{A}\leftrightarrow\mathsf{Mod}^{\operatorname{dg}}_{A}
, a natural question is whether we can lift
(E,\mathbb{E})
to a cofibrant object in
\mathsf{L_{\infty}Algd}^{\operatorname{dg}}_{A}
.

Since every strict morphism between
L_{\infty}
-morphism is an
\infty
-morphism, we have a function
\iota:\mathsf{L_{\infty}Algd}^{\operatorname{dg}}_{A}\to\mathtt{L_{\infty}Algd}_{A}
.

21.4.
Perfect singular foliations

Following the idea of our construction of
L_{\infty}
-algebroid, we refine the notion of singular foliation as follows.

Definition 21.9
.

Let
M
be a
{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}
-manifold, we define a
perfect singular foliation

{\mathcal{F}}
to be a subsheaf
{\mathcal{F}}
of
{\mathcal{O}}_{M}
-module of the tangent sheaf
T_{M}
such that:

(1)

(Perfectness)
{\mathcal{F}}
is a (strict) perfect
{\mathcal{O}}_{M}
-module, i.e. there exists a (global) local resolution by finite projective
{\mathcal{O}}_{M}
-modules

0\to E_{-d}\to E_{d-1}\to\cdots\to E_{-1}\to E_{0}\to{\mathcal{F}}\to 0

(2)

(Involutivity)
{\mathcal{F}}
is closed under brackets.

In this definition, we replace the local finite generativity by local finite presentivity, which allows us to do many operations in homological algebras and homotopical operations as we did in the case of holomorphic singular foliations previously. For perfect singular foliations, we can always endow
L_{\infty}
-algebroid structures by
[
LLS20
]
. Note that what we defined here is using the existing of a global resolution, which we might weaken to exist local resolutions. In that case, we call
{\mathcal{F}}
a
weakly perfect singular foliation
. In the case of compact manifolds, these two definition agrees. For weakly perfect foliations, we cannot construct
L_{\infty}
-structure directly, but we can follow the similar idea of Theorem
21.8
, and use
twisted perfect complex
(c.f.
[
Wei16
]
[
TT76
]
) resolving
{\mathcal{F}}
, and then use similar freely generating method as Theorem
21.8
to construct an
L_{\infty}
-algebroid structure.

We can easily define
perfect complex singular foliation
to be a subsheaf
{\mathcal{F}}
of the complexified tangent sheaf
T^{{\mathbb{C}}}_{M}
which satisfies perfectness and involutivity.

We can also generalize this to derived manifolds:

Definition 21.10
.

Let
(X,{\mathcal{O}}_{X})
be a derived manifold, we define a
perfect singular foliation

{\mathcal{F}}
to be a complex of subsheaf of
{\mathcal{F}}
of
{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
-module of the tangent sheaf
T_{X}
complex such that:

(1)

(Perfectness)
{\mathcal{F}}
is a perfect
{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
-module, i.e. there exists a resolution by a double complex of finite projective
{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
-modules

0\to E_{-d}\to E_{d-1}\to\cdots\to E_{-1}\to E_{0}\to{\mathcal{F}}\to 0

(2)

(Involutivity)
{\mathcal{F}}
is closed under brackets.

For example, in coisotropic reduction (c.f. Example
15.4
), if
{\mathcal{F}}
has constant dimension in each stratum, then
{\mathcal{F}}
is a perfect singular foliation on the derived critical locus.

Following the ideas for perfect singular foliations, we can generalize the elliptic involutive structures

Definition 21.11
.

Let
M
be a
{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}
-manifold, and
{\mathcal{F}}\subset T^{{\mathbb{C}}}_{M}
a complex singular foliation. We say
{\mathcal{F}}
is an
elliptic singular foliation
if

(1)

{\mathcal{F}}
is a complex perfect singular foliation.

(2)

{\mathcal{F}}+\overline{{\mathcal{F}}}=T^{{\mathbb{C}}}_{M}
.

Clear, elliptic involutive structures are elliptic singular foliations, which we can also call
elliptic regular foliations
.

22.
Elliptic involutive structures

22.1.
Elliptic involutive structures and foliations

Let
M
be a compact manifold.

Definition 22.1
.

A complex Lie algebroid
A
is
elliptic
if its associated dga
\operatorname{Sym}A^{\vee}[-1]
is an elliptic complex.

Note that here the ellipticity is equivalent to require
\rho(A)+\overline{\rho(A)}=T_{{\mathbb{C}}}M
.

Definition 22.2
.

Let
M
be a smooth manifold. An
elliptic involutive structure
(EIS) consists of the following data:

(1)

An involutive sub-bundle
V
of the complexified tangent bundle
T_{{\mathbb{C}}}M
.

(2)

V
is an elliptic Lie algebroid.

Example 22.3
.

The complexified tangent Lie algebroid is clearly a trivial EIS.

Example 22.4
.

Take
V=T^{0,1}M
the anti-holomorphic tangent Lie algebroid, then an
EIS
on
V
corresponds to a complex structure on
M
.

Theorem 22.5

(Newlander-Nirenberg)
.

Let
V
be an elliptic involutive structure on
M
. Then, locally, there exist on
M
real coordinates
(t_{1},\cdots,t_{d})
and complex coordinates
(z_{1},\cdots,z_{n})
such that

(22.1)

V=\operatorname{Span}\big\{\frac{\partial}{\partial t_{1}},\cdots,\frac{\partial}{\partial t_{d}},\frac{\partial}{\partial\overline{z}_{1}},\cdots,\frac{\partial}{\partial\overline{z}_{n}}\big\}=\operatorname{Span}\big\{\frac{\partial}{\partial z_{1}},\cdots,\frac{\partial}{\partial z_{n}}^{\perp}\big\}

Thus, locally, V looks like the product distribution
{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\oplus T^{0,1}{\mathbb{C}}^{n}
, where
{\mathbb{C}}^{n}
has its
standard complex structure.

The real part of an EIS is always a foliation on
M
, hence we also call an EIS an elliptic (regular) foliation.

Proposition 22.6
.

Let
{\mathcal{O}}_{V}
denote the structure sheaf of an
EIS
on
M
, then there exists an equivalence of categories

(22.2)

\{\text{locally free sheaves of ${\mathcal{O}}_{V}$ modules}\}\simeq\{\text{ finitely generated projective $V$-modules}\}

Proof.

First let
\mathcal{E}
be a locally free sheaf of
{\mathcal{O}}_{V}
-module. Note that this condition is equivalent to that there exists a trivializing cover
\{U_{i}\}
such that the transition functions of
E
corresponding to this cover take value in
{\mathcal{O}}_{V}
, where
E=\Gamma(E)\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}_{V}}{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}(M)
is the vector bundle corresponding to
\mathcal{E}
. Hence, in order to construct a flat
V
-connection on
E
, we just need to let the frame on
U_{i}\times{\mathbb{C}}^{r}
to be parallel.

Let
E
be finitely generated projective
V
-modules, i.e. a vector bundle with a flat
V
-connection. We want to show that, for any
x\in M
, there exists a parallel local frame on some neighborhood of
x
. Let
\nabla
be the flat connection on
E
, and
\{e_{i}\}
a local frame on some neighborhood
U
of
x
, then
\nabla e_{i}=\omega^{j}_{i}e_{j}
where
\omega
is the connection 1-form. By Newlander-Nirenberg, we can let
U
be small such that
V|_{U}=\operatorname{Span}_{{\mathbb{C}}}\{dz^{1},\cdots,dz^{m}\}^{\perp}
, where
z^{1},\cdots,z^{m}
are some complex coordinates on
E|_{U}
.

Now we have
E|_{U}\simeq U\times{\mathbb{C}}^{r}
. Let
u^{1},\cdots,u^{r}
be complex coordinates on
{\mathbb{C}}^{r}
. Consider a distribution

V^{\prime}=\operatorname{Span}_{{\mathbb{C}}}\bigg\{\frac{\partial}{\partial\overline{u}^{k}},v-\omega^{j}_{i}(v)u^{i}\frac{\partial}{\partial u^{k}}|v\in V\subset T_{{\mathbb{C}}}(U\times{\mathbb{C}}^{r})\bigg\}\subset T_{{\mathbb{C}}}(U\times{\mathbb{C}}^{r})

By flatness of
\nabla
, we can show that this distribution is involutive. By our construction,
V^{\prime}+\overline{V^{\prime}}=T_{{\mathbb{C}}}U\times{\mathbb{C}}^{r}
, therefore, we get an EIS on
U\times{\mathbb{C}}^{r}
.
According to our construction, the space of 1-form annihilate this distribution is

\displaystyle(V^{\prime})^{\perp}

\displaystyle=V^{\perp}+\operatorname{Span}_{{\mathbb{C}}}\{du^{j}+\tilde{\omega}^{j}_{i}u_{i}:j=1,\cdots,r\}

Note that
V^{\perp}=\{dz^{1},\cdots,dz^{m}\}
by Newlander-Nirenberg, and by same reason we also have
{V^{\prime}}^{\perp}=\{d\tilde{z}^{1},\cdots,d\tilde{z}^{m}\}
for some coordinates
\{\tilde{z}^{i}\}
. Hence, we have
d\tilde{z}^{j}=F^{j}_{i}dz^{i}+G^{j}_{i}(du^{i}+\tilde{\omega}^{i}_{k}u^{k})
. With possibly rearranging indices, we have
G^{j}_{i}\in GL_{r}({\mathbb{C}})
in some neighborhood of zero-section. Differentiating previous equation and set
u^{k}=0
for all
k
, we get

0=dF^{j}_{i}\wedge dz_{i}+dG^{j}_{i}\wedge du^{i}-G^{j}_{i}\tilde{\omega}^{i}_{k}\wedge du^{k}

on
U\times\{0\}
. Pulling back to
V^{\vee}\otimes(^{(}T^{1,0}{\mathbb{C}}^{r})^{\vee}
, we get

d_{V}G^{j}_{i}-G^{j}_{k}\tilde{\omega}^{k}_{i}=0

Now let
\tilde{\sigma}_{i}=(G^{i}_{j})^{-1}\sigma_{j}
on
U\times\{0\}
. Next, we want to show that
\tilde{\sigma}_{k}
’s are all parallel.

First, note that

\displaystyle\nabla\sigma_{j}=

\displaystyle\omega^{k}_{j}\otimes\sigma_{k}=d_{V}G^{k}_{j}\otimes\tilde{\sigma}_{k}+G^{k}_{j}\nabla\tilde{\sigma}_{k}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle G^{k}_{i}\omega^{i}_{j}\otimes\tilde{\sigma}_{k}+G^{k}_{j}\nabla\tilde{\sigma}_{k}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\omega^{i}_{j}\otimes{\sigma}_{i}+G^{k}_{j}\nabla\tilde{\sigma}_{k}

Hence
G^{k}_{j}\nabla\tilde{\sigma}_{k}=0
, which implies
\nabla\tilde{\sigma}_{k}=0
for all
k
. Therefore,
\{\tilde{\sigma}_{k}\}
is a parallel local frame for
E
.
∎

22.2.
Sheaf of
{\mathcal{O}}_{V}
-modules

Definition 22.7
.

Let
(X,V)
be a compact manifold with an elliptic involutive structure
V
. An
V
-analytic
sheaf on
X
is a sheaf of
{\mathcal{O}}_{V}
-modules. An
V
-analytic sheaf
{\mathcal{F}}
is called a coherent
V
-analytic sheaf if each point of
X
is contained in a neighborhood
U
such that
{\mathcal{F}}|_{U}
is the cokernel of a morphism
{\mathcal{O}}_{V}^{\oplus m}\to{\mathcal{O}}_{V}^{\oplus n}
between free finite rank
V
-analytic sheaves.

Lemma 22.8
.

Let
k\in{\mathbb{N}}
. If for every open set
U\subset M
and every positive integer
m
, every morphism
{\mathcal{O}}_{V}^{\oplus m}|_{U}\to{\mathcal{O}}_{V}|_{U}
has locally finitely generated kernel, then every morphism
{\mathcal{O}}_{V}^{\oplus m}|_{U}\to{\mathcal{O}}_{V}^{k}|_{U}
also has locally finitely generated kernel.

Proof.

We proceed by induction on
k
. The case
k=1
is just the assumption. Now assume
k>1
and we have already proved the case for all
j<k
, i.e. for every
U\subset M
, every morphism
{\mathcal{O}}_{V}^{\oplus m}|_{U}\to{\mathcal{O}}_{V}^{j}|_{U}
has finitely generated kernel. Now we fix a
U
, and consider a morphism
f:{\mathcal{O}}_{V}^{\oplus m}|_{U}\to{\mathcal{O}}_{V}^{k}|_{U}
. We can regard
f=(g,h)
, where
g:{\mathcal{O}}_{V}^{\oplus m}|_{U}\to{\mathcal{O}}_{V}^{k-1}|_{U}
and
h:{\mathcal{O}}_{V}^{\oplus m}|_{U}\to{\mathcal{O}}_{V}^{1}|_{U}
is constructed by composing
f
with projection on the first
k-1
entries and the last entry respectively. By induction hypothesis,
\ker g
is locally finitely generated. Hence, for any
x\in U
, we can find a neighborhood
V\subset U
such that there exists some
p\in{\mathbb{N}}
and a morphism
\phi:{\mathcal{O}}_{V}^{\oplus p}|_{V}\to{\mathcal{O}}_{V}^{m}|_{V}
, and
g\circ\phi
is an exact sequence. Note that
h\circ\phi:{\mathcal{O}}_{V}^{\oplus p}|_{V}\to{\mathcal{O}}_{V}|_{V}
also has a locally finitely generated kernel, by shrinking
V
is necessary, there exists some
q\in{\mathbb{N}}
and a morphism
\psi:{\mathcal{O}}_{V}^{\oplus q}|_{V}\to{\mathcal{O}}_{V}^{p}|_{V}
which surjects on
\ker(h\circ\phi)
. Now
\ker f=\ker g\cap\ker h=\phi(\ker h\circ\phi)=\operatorname{Im}\phi\circ\psi
, thus
\ker f
is also locally finitely generated.
∎

Lemma 22.9
.

Let
(p_{1},\cdots,p_{m})
be an
m
-tuple of monic polynomials in
{\mathcal{O}}_{n-1}[z_{n}]_{0}
and
d=\max_{i}\deg p_{i}
, and let
\rho:{\mathcal{O}}_{n}[z_{n}]_{0}^{\oplus m}
be the morphism
\rho(f_{1},\cdots,f_{m})=\sum_{i}p_{i}f_{i}
. Let
\mathcal{K}_{d}\subset\ker\rho
be the subspace generated by
m
-tuples of polynomials of degree at most
d
in
{\mathcal{O}}_{n-1}[z_{n}]_{0}
, then
\mathcal{K}_{d}
generated
\ker\rho
as a
(\mathcal{O}_{n})_{0}
-module.

Proposition 22.10

(Oka)
.

Let
U\subset M
be a trivializing open set. Then each
{\mathcal{O}}_{V}|_{U}
-module morphism
f:{\mathcal{O}}_{V}|_{U}^{\oplus m}\to{\mathcal{O}}_{V}|_{U}^{\oplus k}
has locally finitely generated kernel.

Proof.

By previous lemma, it suffices to prove the case for
k=1
. Recall that by Newlander-Nirenberg,
V
locally look like the distribution
{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\oplus T^{0,1}{\mathbb{C}}^{n}
, so
V
is split to a foliated direction spanned by
\frac{\partial}{\partial t_{i}}
’s and a transverse direction spanned by
\frac{\partial}{\partial\overline{z}_{j}}
’s. Hence,
{\mathcal{O}}_{V}|_{U}
consists of
{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}
functions which are constant along
t_{i}
’s directions and holomorphic along
z_{j}
’s directions. Let
d=\dim\operatorname{Span}\{\frac{\partial}{\partial t_{i}}\}
and
n=\dim\operatorname{Span}\{\frac{\partial}{\partial\overline{z}_{j}}\}
. If
n=0
, then the
f
simplifies to a linear map between finite dimensional vector space, hence the kernel is also finite dimensional. We will proceed by induct on
n
. Suppose the proposition is proved for all
n<k
. Without loss of generality,
U\simeq{\mathbb{R}}^{\dim l}
, where
l=\dim M=2(d+n)
, and
x\in U
sits at the origin in
{\mathbb{R}}^{l}
.
f
has the form
f(s_{1},\cdots,s_{m})=\sum f_{i}s_{i}
for an
m
-tuple of functions
\{f_{i}\}\subset{\mathcal{O}}_{V}|_{U}
. By modifying coordinates if necessary, we can assume that the germ at
0
of each
f_{i}
vanishes at finite order. Note that
f_{i}
are constant along
t
’s direction, i.e. we can regard
f(t_{1},\cdots,t_{d},z_{1},\cdots z_{n})=f(z_{1},\cdots z_{n})
near 0. By Weierstrass preparation theorem, we can then write
f_{i}=u_{i}p_{i}
, where
u_{i}
is a unit and
p_{i}
is a Weierstrass polynomial. We can replace these germs by their representatives in some neighborhood
U^{\prime}
of
0
. Shrinking
U^{\prime}
if necessary, we can assume that
u_{i}
’s are non-vanishing in
U^{\prime}
. Now the map
(t_{1},\cdots,t_{d},z_{1},\cdots,z_{n})\to(t_{1},\cdots,t_{d},u_{1}z_{1},\cdots,u_{n}z_{n})
is an automorphism of
{\mathcal{O}}^{\oplus m}|_{U}
which maps the kernel of
f
to the kernel of the map determined by the
m
-tuple
(p_{1},\cdots,p_{m})
. Hence, without loss of generality, we may replace
f_{i}
’s by
p_{i}
’s, i.e.
f=\sum f_{i}g_{i}
.
We may assume
U^{\prime}
has the form
{\mathbb{R}}^{2d}\times U^{\prime\prime}\times U^{\prime\prime\prime}
for some
U^{\prime\prime}\subset{\mathbb{C}}^{n},U^{\prime\prime\prime}\subset{\mathbb{C}}
.
Let
d=\max_{i}\deg p_{i}
, and let
\mathcal{K}_{d}
denote the sheaf on
U^{\prime\prime}
defined as follows: for each open subset
W\subset U^{\prime\prime}
,
\mathcal{K}_{d}(W)\subset\ker f
is the subspace consisting of
m
-tuple of polynomials of degree less than or equal to
d
in
{\mathcal{O}}_{n-1}(U^{\prime\prime})[z_{n}]
, where
{\mathcal{O}}_{n-1}(U^{\prime\prime})
denotes the sheaf of holomorphic functions on
U^{\prime\prime}
. We need to show that
\mathcal{K}_{d}
is locally finitely generated as a
{\mathcal{O}}_{V}(U^{\prime\prime})
-modules. For each neighborhood
W\subset U^{\prime\prime}
, the space of
m
-tuples
(q_{1},\cdots,q_{m})
forms a free module of rank
(d+1)m
over
{\mathcal{O}}_{n-1}(U^{\prime\prime})
, where each
q_{i}
’s is a polynomial of degree less than or equal to
d
. Hence,
f
gives an
{\mathcal{O}}_{n}(U^{\prime\prime})[z_{n}]
-module morphism from a rank
(d+1)m
free module to a rank
(2d+1)
free module which consists of polynomial in
z_{n}
of degree at most
2d
. Note that
\mathcal{K}_{d}
is exactly the kernel of this restricted morphism. By induction hypothesis, we get that
\mathcal{K}_{d}
is locally finitely generated.

Finally, we need to show that
\mathcal{K}_{d}
actually generate the whole
\ker f
as a sheaf of
{\mathcal{O}}_{V}
-modules. It suffices to show that the stalk
(\mathcal{K}_{d})_{0}
at the origin generated the stalk
(\ker f)_{0}
over
({\mathcal{O}}_{n})_{0}
. Note that here we regard
{\mathcal{O}}_{n}
locally constant in the real directions. The
p_{i}
’s may not be Weierstrass polynomials when taking the germs at the origin, but they are still monic polynomials in
z_{n}
. The proof then follows from the previous lemma.

∎

Corollary 22.11
.

Let
U
be a trivializing open neighborhood of
(X,V)
, and
\mathcal{F}
is a locally finitely generated sheaf of submodules of
{\mathcal{O}}_{V}^{\oplus k}
, then
\mathcal{F}
is
V
-coherent. In particular, given a morphism of
V
-analytic sheaves
\phi:{\mathcal{O}}_{V}^{\oplus m}(U)\to{\mathcal{O}}_{V}^{\oplus k}(U)
, then both
\ker\phi
and
\operatorname{Im}\phi
are
V
-coherent.

Proof.

Let
x\in U
be arbitrary, then there exists
W\ni x
such that
{\mathcal{F}}
is the image of a morphism
\phi:{\mathcal{O}}_{V}^{\oplus m}(W)\to{\mathcal{O}}_{V}^{\oplus k}(W)
of sheaves of
{\mathcal{O}}_{V}
-modules. By Oka’s theorem, its kernel is also locally finitely generated. With shrinking
W
if necessary, we can assume there exists a morphism
\psi:{\mathcal{O}}_{V}^{\oplus p}(W)\to{\mathcal{O}}_{V}^{\oplus m}(W)
which surjects on
\ker\phi
on
W
. Therefore,
{\mathcal{F}}
is
V
-coherent.

Next, by assumption,
\operatorname{Im}\phi
is already finitely generated on
U
, and the kernel is also locally finitely generated, which implies
\operatorname{Im}\phi
is also
V
-coherent.
∎

Corollary 22.12
.

Let
U
be a trivializing open neighborhood of
(X,V)
, and
\mathcal{F}
and
\mathcal{G}
are
V
-coherent sheaves of submodules of
{\mathcal{O}}^{\oplus m}_{V}(U)
, then
\mathcal{F}\cap\mathcal{G}
is also
V
-coherent.

Proof.

For every point
x\in U
, there exists an open neighborhood
W\ni x
such that
{\mathcal{F}}|_{W}
and
{\mathcal{G}}|_{W}
are images of some morphisms
\phi:{\mathcal{O}}_{V}^{\oplus p}|_{W}\to{\mathcal{O}}_{V}^{\oplus m}|_{W}
and
\psi:{\mathcal{O}}_{V}^{\oplus q}|_{W}\to{\mathcal{O}}_{V}^{\oplus m}|_{W}
respectively. Consider the map
\theta:{\mathcal{O}}_{V}^{\oplus(p+q)}|_{W}\to{\mathcal{O}}_{V}^{\oplus m}|_{W}
by
\theta(f\oplus g)=\phi(f)-\psi(g)
. By Oka’s theorem,
\ker\theta
is
V
-coherent. Note that
{\mathcal{F}}\cap{\mathcal{G}}
is the image of
\ker\theta
under
\phi
. With shrinking
W
if necessary, we can choose finitely many generators for
\ker\theta
, whose image under
\phi
will then generate
{\mathcal{F}}\cap{\mathcal{G}}
. Hence,
{\mathcal{F}}\cap{\mathcal{G}}
is locally finitely generated over
{\mathcal{O}}_{V}
. By similar reasoning in the previous corollary,
{\mathcal{F}}\cap{\mathcal{G}}
is
V
-coherent.
∎

Proposition 22.13
.

Let
(X,V)
be a manifold equipped with an elliptic involutive structure
V
, and
{\mathcal{F}}
a coherent
V
-analytic sheaf, then locally
{\mathcal{F}}
admits finite resolution of length less than or equal to
n+1
by free sheaves of modules.

Let
M
be a module over
{\mathcal{A}}^{\bullet}
, then it localizes to a sheaf of
{\mathcal{A}}^{\bullet}_{X}
-modules by taking
M_{X}(U)=M\otimes_{{\mathcal{A}}^{\bullet}}{\mathcal{A}}^{\bullet}_{X}(U)
. Let
(E^{\bullet},\nabla)\in\mathcal{P}_{{\mathcal{A}}^{\bullet}}
, define a double complex of sheaves
\mathcal{E}^{p,q}
by
\mathcal{E}^{p,q}(U)=E^{p}\otimes_{{\mathcal{A}}^{\bullet}}{\mathcal{A}}^{q}_{X}(U)
. Define
(\mathcal{E}_{X}^{\bullet},\nabla)=(\sum_{p+q=\bullet}\mathcal{E}^{p,q}_{X},\nabla)
. Note that
\mathcal{E}_{X}^{\bullet}
is a complex of soft sheaves of
{\mathcal{O}}_{V}
-modules.

Analogous to Pali’s definition of
\overline{\partial}
-coherent analytic sheaves, we define
\overline{\partial}_{V}
-coherent analytic sheaves for elliptic involutive structures.

Definition 22.14
.

Let
(X,V)
be an elliptic involutive structure. We define a
\overline{\partial}
-coherent analytic sheaf

{\mathcal{F}}
to be a sheaf of modules over the sheaf of
{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}
-functions
{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}_{X}
with

(1)

Finiteness:
{\mathcal{F}}
has locally finite resolution by finitely generated free modules over
{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}_{X}
.

(2)

V
-analytic:
{\mathcal{F}}
is equipped with a flat
\overline{\partial}_{V}
-connection, i.e. an operator
\overline{\partial}_{V}:{\mathcal{F}}\to{\mathcal{F}}\otimes_{{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}_{X}}A^{1}_{X}
with
\overline{\partial}_{V}^{2}=0
.

Proposition 22.15
.

The functor
\alpha:\mathsf{Ho}\mathcal{P}_{A^{\bullet}}\to\mathsf{D_{Perf}}(X,{\mathcal{O}}_{V})\simeq\mathsf{D^{b}_{Coh}}(X,{\mathcal{O}}_{V})
defined by

(22.3)

\alpha:(E^{\bullet},\nabla)\mapsto(\mathcal{E}_{X}^{\bullet},\nabla)

is fully faithful.

Proof.

Let
U=\{(t_{1},\cdots,t_{d},z_{1},\cdots,z_{n})|\ |t_{i}|<r,|z_{i}|<r\}
be a polydisc in
X
. We want to show that there exists a small polydisc
V
such that there exists a gauge transformation
\phi:\mathcal{E}^{\bullet}|_{V}\to\mathcal{E}^{\bullet}|_{V}
of degree 0 such that

\phi\circ\nabla\circ\phi^{-1}=\tilde{\nabla}^{0}+\overline{\partial}_{V}
. Hence,
\mathcal{E}^{\bullet}_{X}
is gauge equivalent to a complex of finitely generated projective
V
-modules, that is,
H^{p}\big((\mathcal{E}^{\bullet,0}),\nabla^{0}\big)
is
\overline{\partial}_{V}
-coherent with
\overline{\partial}_{V}
-coherent connection
\nabla^{1}
for each
p
. Note that
U=U_{1}\times U_{2}
where
U_{1}
is contractible and
U_{2}
is Stein, so there is no higher cohomology with respect to
\nabla^{1}
, then we are left with
V
-analytic sections over
U
, which are then coherent.

The construction of
\phi
follows from the proof of integrability of holomorphic structures on vector bundles. As we are in a polydisc
U
, we can write the
\mathbb{Z}
-connection
\nabla
as
\nabla=\nabla^{0}+\overline{\partial}_{V}+J
where

J:\mathcal{E}^{p,q}(U)\to\bigoplus_{i\leq p}\mathcal{E}^{i,q+(p-i)+1}(U)

is a
{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}_{X}(U)
-linear map. Decompose
J
as
J=J^{\prime}\wedge d\overline{z}_{1}+J^{\prime\prime}
with
\iota_{\frac{\partial}{\partial\overline{z}_{1}}}J^{\prime}=\iota_{\frac{\partial}{\partial\overline{z}_{1}}}J^{\prime\prime}=0
. For simplicity, write
\overline{\partial}_{i}=d\overline{z}_{i}\wedge\frac{\partial}{\partial\overline{z}_{i}}
. We want to find a
\phi_{1}
with
\phi_{1}(\overline{\partial}_{1}+J^{\prime}\wedge d\overline{z}_{1})\phi_{1}^{-1}=\overline{\partial}_{1}
. It suffices to solve
\phi_{1}^{-1}\overline{\partial}_{1}(\phi_{1})=J^{\prime}\wedge d\overline{z}_{1}
and treat
t_{1},\cdots,t_{d},z_{2},\cdots,z_{n}
as variables. Now we set
\nabla_{1}=\phi_{1}(\nabla^{0}+\overline{\partial}_{V}+J^{\prime}+J^{\prime\prime})\phi_{1}^{-1}
. We can write
\nabla_{1}=\nabla^{0}_{1}+\overline{\partial}_{1}+\overline{\partial}_{\geq 2}+J_{1}
. We claim that:

(1)

\nabla^{0}_{1}\circ\nabla^{0}_{1}=0.

(2)

\nabla^{0}_{1}
and
J_{1}
are holomorphic in
z_{1}
.

(3)

\iota_{\frac{\partial}{\partial\overline{z}_{1}}}J_{1}=0

Notice that

\displaystyle 0=

\displaystyle\iota_{\frac{\partial}{\partial\overline{z}_{1}}}(\nabla_{1}\circ\nabla_{1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\iota_{\frac{\partial}{\partial\overline{z}_{1}}}(\nabla^{0}_{1}\circ\overline{\partial}_{1}+\overline{\partial}_{1}\circ\nabla^{0}_{1}+J_{1}\circ\overline{\partial}_{1}+\overline{\partial}_{1}\circ J_{1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\iota_{\frac{\partial}{\partial\overline{z}_{1}}}(\overline{\partial}_{1}(\nabla^{0}_{1})+\overline{\partial}_{1}(J_{1}))

For degree reason in the
p
-direction, the two summand in the bracket must both be zero. Therefore, we have proved the claim.

Next, we shall iterate this procedure. Write
J_{1}=J_{1}^{\prime}\wedge d\overline{z}_{2}+J_{1}^{\prime\prime}
with
\iota_{\frac{\partial}{\partial\overline{z}_{1}}}J_{1}^{\prime}=\iota_{\frac{\partial}{\partial\overline{z}_{2}}}J_{1}^{\prime}=\iota_{\frac{\partial}{\partial\overline{z}_{1}}}J_{1}^{\prime\prime}=\iota_{\frac{\partial}{\partial\overline{z}_{2}}}J_{1}^{\prime\prime}=0
, and we want to find a
\phi_{2}
with
\phi_{2}(\overline{\partial}_{2}+J_{1}^{\prime}\wedge d\overline{z}_{2})\phi_{2}^{-1}=\overline{\partial}_{2}
and
\phi_{2}(\overline{\partial}_{1})\phi_{2}^{-1}=\overline{\partial}_{1}
. Then it suffices to solve
\phi_{2}^{-1}\overline{\partial}_{2}(\phi_{2})=J_{1}^{\prime}\wedge d\overline{z}_{2}
. Note that
\phi_{2}
is holomorphic in
z_{1}
since
J_{1}^{\prime}
is. Now set
\nabla_{2}=\phi_{2}\circ\nabla_{1}\circ\phi_{2}^{-1}
, and we can write
\nabla_{2}
as
\nabla_{2}=\nabla^{0}_{2}+\overline{\partial}_{1}+\overline{\partial}_{2}+\overline{\partial}_{\geq 3}+J_{2}
with
\iota_{\frac{\partial}{\partial\overline{z}_{1}}}J_{2}=\iota_{\frac{\partial}{\partial\overline{z}_{2}}}J_{2}=0

Continuing this fashion, we will arrive at
\nabla_{n}=\nabla^{0}_{n}+\overline{\partial}+J_{n}
, where
\iota_{\frac{\partial}{\partial\overline{z}_{i}}}J_{n}=0
for all
i=1,\cdots,n
. Hence, now it suffices to deal with the real directions. Again, write
d_{i}=dt_{i}\wedge\frac{d}{d{t}_{i}}
and
J_{n}=J_{n}^{\prime}\wedge dt_{1}+J_{n}^{\prime\prime}
with
\iota_{\frac{\partial}{\partial t_{1}}}J_{n}^{\prime}=\iota_{\frac{\partial}{\partial t_{1}}}J_{n}^{\prime\prime}=0
. We want to find
\psi_{1}
with
\psi_{1}(d_{1}+J_{n}^{\prime}\wedge dt_{1})\psi_{1}^{-1}=d_{1}
and
\psi_{1}(\overline{\partial}_{i})\psi_{1}^{-1}=\overline{\partial}_{i}
, which can be done by solving
\psi_{1}^{-1}d_{2}(\psi_{1})=J_{n}^{\prime\prime}\wedge dt_{1}
. Now set
\nabla_{n+1}=\psi_{1}\circ\nabla_{n}\circ\psi_{1}^{-1}
. We can write
\nabla_{n+1}=\nabla_{n+1}^{0}+\overline{\partial}+d_{1}+d_{\geq 2}+J_{n+1}
. Similar to the previous argument, we can easily show that

(1)

\nabla_{n+1}^{0}\circ\nabla_{n+1}^{0}=0.

(2)

\nabla_{n+1}^{0}
and
J_{n+1}
are flat in
t_{1}
.

(3)

\iota_{\frac{\partial}{\partial t_{1}}}J_{n+1}=0

Iterating this procedure, we will reach
\widetilde{\nabla}=\nabla_{n+d}+\overline{\partial}+d=\nabla_{n+d}+\overline{\partial}_{V}
.

∎

Lemma 22.16
.

On an elliptic involutive structure
(X,V)
,
{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}_{X}
is flat over
{\mathcal{O}}_{V}
.

Theorem 22.17

(Block
[
Blo05
]
)
.

Suppose
(A^{\bullet},d,c)
is a curved dga. Let
X=(X,\nabla)
be a quasi-cohesive module over
A^{\bullet}
, then there is an object
E=(E^{\bullet},\nabla^{\prime})
in
\mathcal{P}_{A^{\bullet}}
such that
\tilde{h}_{X}
is quasi-isomorphic to
h_{E}
, under either of the two following conditions:

(1)

X is a quasi-finite quasi-cohesive module.

(2)

A^{\bullet}
is flat over
A^{0}
and there exists a bounded complex
(E,\nabla^{\prime 0})
of finitely generated projective right
A^{0}
-modules and an
A^{0}
-linear quasi-isomorphisms
e^{0}:(E,\nabla^{\prime 0})\to(X,\nabla^{0})
.

Proposition 22.18
.

Let
(\mathcal{E}^{\bullet}_{X},d)
be a complex of sheaf of
{\mathcal{O}}_{V}
-modules with coherent
V
-analytic cohomology, then there exists a cohesive
A^{\bullet}
-module
E=(E^{\bullet},\nabla)
unique up to quasi-isomorphism, and
\alpha(E)
is quasi-isomorphic to
(\mathcal{E}^{\bullet}_{X},d)
. In addition, for two such complexes
(\mathcal{E}^{\bullet}_{1},d)
and
(\mathcal{E}^{\bullet}_{2},d)
, the corresponding cohesive modules
E_{1}
and
E_{2}
satisfies

(22.4)

\operatorname{Ext}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{V}}^{k}(\mathcal{E}^{\bullet}_{1},\mathcal{E}^{\bullet}_{2})\simeq H^{k}(\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{P}_{A^{\bullet}}}(E_{1},E_{2}))

Proof.

Without loss of generality, we can assume
(\mathcal{E}^{\bullet}_{X},d)
is a perfect complex over
{\mathcal{O}}_{V}
. Define
\mathcal{E}^{\bullet}_{\infty}=\mathcal{E}^{\bullet}_{X}\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}_{V}}{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}_{X}
. By flatness of
{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}_{X}
over
{\mathcal{O}}_{V}
,
(\mathcal{E}^{\bullet}_{X},d)
is a perfect complex of
A_{X}
-modules, and the map
(\mathcal{E}^{\bullet}_{X},d)\to(\mathcal{E}^{\bullet}_{X}\otimes_{{\mathcal{O}}_{V}}A_{X}^{\bullet},d\otimes 1+1\otimes\overline{\partial}_{V})
is a quasi-isomorphism. By proposition, there exists a (strict) perfect complex
(E^{\bullet},\nabla)
of
{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}(X)
-modules and a quasi-isomorphism
e^{0}:(E^{\bullet},\nabla)\to(\Gamma(X,\mathcal{E}^{\bullet}_{\infty}),d)
.
(\Gamma(X,\mathcal{E}^{\bullet}_{\infty}),d)
defines a quasi-cohesive module over
A^{\bullet}
, hence the result follows from Theorem
22.17
.
∎

In summary, we have just proved that

Proposition 22.19
.

Let
(X,V)
be a compact manifold
X
with an elliptic involutive structure
V
, then there exists an equivalence of categories between
\mathsf{D^{b}_{Coh}}(X)
, the bounded derived category of
complexes of sheaves of
{\mathcal{O}}_{V}
-modules with coherent
V
-analytic cohomology, and
\mathsf{Ho}\mathcal{P}_{{\mathcal{A}}^{\bullet}}
, the homotopy category of the dg-category of cohesive modules over
{\mathcal{A}}^{\bullet}=\operatorname{Sym}V^{\vee}[-1]
, i.e.

\mathsf{D^{b}_{Coh}}(X,{\mathcal{O}}_{V})\simeq\mathsf{Ho}\mathcal{P}_{{\mathcal{A}}^{\bullet}}

We can deduct analogous result of Pali for coherent
V
-analytic sheaves and
\overline{\partial}_{V}
-coherent analytic sheaves.

Corollary 22.20
.

The category of
V
-coherent analytic sheaves on
X
is equivalent to the category of coherent
V
-analytic sheaves.

22.3.
Cauchy-Riemann structures

Let
M
be a
2n+1
dimensional smooth manifold. An
almost Cauchy-Riemann structure
on
M
is a sub-bundle
L
of the complexified tangent bundle
T_{{\mathbb{C}}}M
such that
L\cap\overline{L}=0
. We say
L
is a
Cauchy-Riemann structure
on
M
is
L
is involutive.

Definition 22.21
.

Let
(M,L)
be a CR manifold. Let
f\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}_{{\mathbb{C}}}(M)
, then we say
f
is a
Cauchy-Riemann
or simply
CR
function, if for all
Z\in\overline{L}
,
Z(f)=0

Proposition 22.22
.

Let
{\mathcal{O}}_{L}
denote the structure sheaf of a
CR
-structure on
M
, then there exists an equivalence of categories

(22.5)

\{\text{locally free sheaves of ${\mathcal{O}}_{L}$ modules}\}\simeq\{\text{ finitely generated projective $L$-modules}\}

Part VI
Higher monodromy and holonomy

23.
Monodromy

23.1.
Higher monodromy

Let
(M,{\mathcal{F}})
be a regular foliation, we consider the collection
{\mathcal{G}}
of all homotopy classes of paths lying in the same leaf, which form a smooth manifold and gives the
monodromy groupoid
of
{\mathcal{F}}
.

Definition 23.1
.

A map
p:X\to Y
is called
semi-locally simply connected
if given any
x\in X
, there is a basic open neighborhood
V
of
p(x)
and a basic open neighborhood of
x
such that
p(U)\subset V
and the following diagram commutes and the lift exists.
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\partial I^{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V\times_{Y}X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I^{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V}

Proposition 23.2
.

Let
(M,{\mathcal{F}})
be a regular foliation, then it induces a semi-locally simply connected map
p:M\to[M/{\mathcal{F}}]
.

Proof.

Let
x\in X
, then we can pick a open neighborhood
V
of
p(x)
which is contained in a single foliation chart
{\mathbb{R}}^{q}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{n-q}
, where
q=\dim{\mathcal{F}}
and
n=\dim M
.
V\times_{Y}X
equals the union of all leaves lying in
[M/\mathcal{F}]
, i.e.
V\times_{Y}X=\coprod_{x\in[M/\mathcal{F}]}L_{x}
. Note that
V\times_{Y}X\to V
is a submersion hence the lift always exists.
∎

Similarly, we can show any submersions are semi-locally simply connected.

Proposition 23.3
.

Let
\pi:X\to Y
be a submersion, then
\pi
is semi-locally simply connected.

Corollary 23.4
.

A smooth Serre fibration is semi-locally simply connected.

Proof.

This follows from the fact that all smooth Serre fibrations are submersions.
∎

23.2.
The monodromy
\infty
-groupoid
\operatorname{Mon}_{\infty}({\mathcal{F}})

Definition 23.5
.

The
Monodromy
\infty
-groupoid
\operatorname{Mon}_{\infty}({\mathcal{F}})
of a foliation
(M,{\mathcal{F}})
, also denoted by
\Pi_{\infty}({\mathcal{F}})
, is a simplicial space whose
n
-simplices are

\operatorname{Map}(\Delta^{n},\mathcal{F})=\operatorname{Map}_{vert}(\Delta^{n},M)=\{f:\Delta^{n}\to M|f(\Delta^{n})\text{ lies in a single leaf}\}

where
\Delta^{n}
denotes the
geometric
n
-simplex
.

Proposition 23.6
.

\operatorname{Mon}_{\infty}({\mathcal{F}})
is a simplicial space.

Proof.

The topology on
n
-simplices
\operatorname{Map}(\Delta^{n},\mathcal{F})
is inherited from the compact-open topology on
\operatorname{Map}(\Delta^{n},M)
. Given
x\in\operatorname{Map}(\Delta^{n},\mathcal{F})
and
V\subset[M/\mathcal{F}]
, a basic open neighborhood of
x
has the form

<x,U>=\{y\in\operatorname{Map}_{vect}(\Delta^{n},M)\big|\exists h:\Delta^{n}\times\Delta^{1}\to\pi^{-1}(U),h(-,0)=x,h(-,1)=y\}

. Since the degeneracy maps
s^{k}:\Delta^{n}\to\Delta^{n+1}
and face maps
d^{k}:\Delta^{n+1}\to\Delta^{n}
are all continuous, the face maps and degeneracy maps in
\operatorname{Mon}_{\infty}({\mathcal{F}})
are all continuous. Therefore,
\operatorname{Mon}_{\infty}({\mathcal{F}})
is a simplicial topological space.

∎

Next, we want to explore the
\infty
-groupoid structure of
\operatorname{Mon}_{\infty}({\mathcal{F}})
. First, we start with a definition of topological
\infty
-groupoids.

Definition 23.7
.

Let
X_{\bullet}
be a simplicial space, we say
X_{\bullet}
is a
topological
\infty
-groupoid
if all its structure maps are continuous, and diagrams of the following form commutes and the lift exists for
0\leq i\leq k,0\leq k<\infty
.
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lambda^{i}[k]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{\bullet}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta[k]}

Note that here all maps are continuous, and
\Delta[n]
here denotes the
standard
n
-simplex
rather than the geometric
n
-simplex.

We denote the space of
i
-th
k
-horns
\Lambda^{i}[k]\to X_{\bullet}
by
X(\Lambda^{i}[k])
and the
n
-simplices
\Delta[k]\to X_{\bullet}
by
X_{n}
. Since
L_{x}

Proposition 23.8
.

\operatorname{Mon}_{\infty}({\mathcal{F}})
is a topological
\infty
-groupoid.

Proof.

Let
\sigma:\Lambda^{i}[k]\to\operatorname{Mon}_{\infty}({\mathcal{F}})
be a
k
-horn. Note that

X(\Lambda^{i}[k])=\operatorname{Map}(\big|\Lambda^{i}[k]\big|,{\mathcal{F}})

where
\big|\Lambda^{i}[k]\big|
denote the
i
-th geometric
k
-horn. Hence, the image of the standard
k
-horn
\big|\Lambda^{i}[k]\big|
lies completely within a single leaf
L_{x}
for some
x\in M
. Therefore,

\sigma
can be regard as a map from
\Lambda^{i}[k]
to
\Pi_{\infty}(L_{x})
, where
\Pi_{\infty}(L_{x})
denotes the fundamental
\infty
-groupoid of the leaf
L_{x}
. Therefore, the lift exists.
∎

23.3.
Smooth monodromy
\infty
-groupoid
\mathcal{P}_{\infty}({\mathcal{F}})

Next, we consider a smooth refinement of the
\operatorname{Mon}_{\infty}({\mathcal{F}})
.

First, we recall the definition of
A
-path.

Definition 23.9
.

Let
\pi:A\to M
be a Lie algebroid with an anchor map
\rho:A\to T_{M}
. A
C^{1}
curve
a:\Delta^{1}\to A
is called an
A
-path if

\frac{d}{dt}(\pi\circ a(t))=\rho\big(a(t)\big)

.

If, in addition,
a(t)
satisfies the following boundary conditions

a(0)=a(1)=0,\quad\dot{a}(0)=\dot{a}(1)=0

then we say
a
is an
A_{0}
-path.

We want to generalize these to higher dimensions. Recall given a foliation
(M,{\mathcal{F}})
, there is an associated Lie algebroid
{\mathcal{F}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rho}}{{\rightarrow}}T_{M}
where the
\rho
is simply the inclusion.

Definition 23.10
.

Let
\sigma:\Delta^{n}\to{\mathcal{F}}
be a differentiable (
C^{1}
or
C^{\infty}
) map such that

(1)

T_{M}\big|_{\pi\circ\sigma(\Delta^{n})}\subset{\mathcal{F}}\big|_{\pi\circ\sigma(\Delta^{n})}
.

(2)

For any piecewise smooth path
\gamma:\Delta^{1}\to\operatorname{Im}(\sigma)
, we have

\frac{d}{dt}(\pi\circ\gamma)(t)=\gamma(t)

We call such a map
\sigma:\Delta^{n}\to{\mathcal{F}}
a
C^{1}
(
C^{\infty}
) foliated
n
-simplex
.

Proposition 23.11
.

The space of
C^{1}
foliated
n
-simplices
P^{n}_{C^{1}}\mathcal{F}
is a Banach manifold. The space of
C^{\infty}
foliated
n
-simplices
P^{n}_{C^{\infty}}{\mathcal{F}}
is a Frechét manifold.

Proof.

First, consider the
C^{1}
case. Let
(\sigma:\Delta^{n}\to\mathcal{F})\in P^{n}_{C^{1}}{\mathcal{F}}
. Pick up any Riemannian metric on
{\mathcal{F}}
. Let
T_{\epsilon}\subset\gamma^{*}T_{{\mathcal{F}}}
consist of tangent vector of length
\leq\epsilon
. For
\epsilon
small, we have the exponential map
\exp:T_{\epsilon}\to{\mathcal{F}},(x,v)\mapsto\exp_{\sigma(x)}v
. Denote the
C^{1}
section of
T_{\epsilon}
by
PT_{\epsilon}
. Note that
\gamma^{*}T_{{\mathcal{F}}}\simeq\Delta^{1}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{n-q}
is a trivial
(n-q)
bundle, hence any trivialization will give a map
PT_{\epsilon}\to\operatorname{Map}_{C^{1}}(\Delta^{1},{\mathbb{R}}^{n})
. Since
\operatorname{Map}_{C^{1}}(\Delta^{1},{\mathbb{R}}^{n})
is a Banach space,
PT_{\epsilon}
gives a chart for
P^{n}_{C^{1}}A
.

Similarly, we can show the space of
C^{r}
foliated
n
-simplices
P^{n}_{C^{r}}{\mathcal{F}}
is also a Banach manifold for
1<r<\infty
. Hence,
P^{n}_{C^{\infty}}{\mathcal{F}}
is a Frechét manifold.
∎

The chart constructed in above gives a deformation of any smooth path. Given a path
\gamma:\Delta^{1}\to M
,
T_{\epsilon}\simeq I\times D_{\epsilon}
where
D_{\epsilon}
is the
\epsilon
-disk in
{\mathbb{R}}^{n-q}
. For any section
\tilde{\gamma}\in\Gamma T_{\epsilon}
lying above
\gamma
, the exponential map then yield a smooth map
\phi:I\times D_{\epsilon}\to M
by
\phi(t,u)=\exp_{\sigma(t)}\tilde{\gamma}(t,u)
. We call
\phi
the
universal deformation of
\gamma
.

Now we can define the smooth analogue of monodromy
\infty
-groupoid.

Definition 23.12
.

Given a foliation
(M,{\mathcal{F}})
, define a simplicial Frechét manifold
\mathcal{P}_{\infty}({\mathcal{F}})
whose
n
-simplices is
P^{n}_{C^{\infty}}
. Similarly, we define a simplicial Banach manifold

\mathcal{P}_{\infty}^{C^{1}}({\mathcal{F}})
whose
n
-simplices is
P^{n}_{C^{1}}
.

Remark 23.13
.

The definition of
\mathcal{P}_{\infty}({\mathcal{F}})
is similar to
path
\infty
-groupoid
in literature. However, we don’t mod out the thin homotopy classes of path in each level, since we want to keep the manifold structure. For example, the space of morphisms of the path 1-groupoid of a manifold is not a manifold in general.

Proposition 23.14
.

\mathcal{P}_{\infty}({\mathcal{F}})
and
\mathcal{P}_{\infty}^{C^{1}}({\mathcal{F}})
are Lie
\infty
-groupoids.

24.
Integrating derived
L_{\infty}
-algebroids

24.1.
Integrating Lie algebroids

In this section, we consider the integration of Lie algebroids to a Lie
\infty
-groupoids.

Definition 24.1
.

Let
A\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rho}}{{\to}}TM
be a Lie algebroid. Define a simplicial manifold
{\mathcal{G}}_{\bullet}
with

{\mathcal{G}}_{n}=\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathsf{Algd}}(T\Delta_{n}),A).

Proposition 24.2
.

\mathcal{G}_{\bullet}
is a Lie
\infty
-groupoid.

Hence,
{\mathcal{G}}_{\bullet}
presents the
\infty
-stack which globalizes
A
. The 2-truncation of
{\mathcal{G}}_{\bullet}
corresponds to the Lie 2-groupoid integrates
A
, which is equivalent to a Weinstein groupoid or a stacky Lie groupoid. However,
{\mathcal{G}}_{n}
are all infinite dimensional for
n\geq 1
.

Parametrize
n
-simplex
\Delta_{n}
by
\{1\geq t_{1}\geq t_{2}\geq\cdots\geq t_{n}\geq 0\}.

Proposition 24.3
.

Let
\alpha_{1}^{t},\cdots,\alpha_{n}^{t}
be time-dependent sections of
A
, where
t=(t_{1},\cdots,t_{n})\in\Delta_{n}
. Suppose the following equation holds:

(24.1)

[\alpha_{i},\alpha_{j}]=\frac{d\alpha_{i}}{dt_{j}}-\frac{d\alpha_{j}}{dt_{i}}

for all
1\leq i,j\leq n
. Then there exists a family of time-dependent vector fields
X_{i}(x,t)=\rho(\alpha^{t}_{i}(x))+\partial t_{i}
. For any
x_{0}\in M
, there exists a
n
-simplex
\sigma:\Delta_{n}\to M
with

\displaystyle\frac{d\sigma}{dt_{i}}=

\displaystyle X^{t}_{i}(\sigma(t))

\displaystyle\sigma(0)=

\displaystyle x_{0}.

In addition, Let
a_{i}(t)=\alpha_{i}^{t}(\sigma(t))
, then
a=\sum_{i=1}^{n}a_{i}dt_{i}:T\Delta_{n}\to A
defines a Lie algebroid morphism.

Proof.

First, we want to show the existence of the simplex
\sigma
. Applying the anchor map, we have

[\rho(\alpha_{i}),\rho(\alpha_{j})]=

Hence
X_{i}+\partial t_{i}
mutually commutes as vector fields on
M\times\Delta_{n}
. Since we assumed that for any
i\in\{1,\cdots,n\}
, the flow of
X_{i}
exists on
\{t_{i}=t_{i-1}\}
up to time 1. Note that
X_{i}
is a
(t_{1},\cdots,\hat{t_{i}},\cdots,t_{n})
-family of
t_{i}
-time-dependent vector fields. Denote the time-dependent flow of
X_{i}
by
\Phi^{X_{i}}_{s_{i}^{\prime},s_{i}}
. Hence, by previous observation we have

\Phi^{X_{i}^{(t_{1},\cdots,\hat{t_{i}},\cdots,s_{j}^{\prime},\cdots,t_{n})}}_{s_{i}^{\prime},s_{i}}\circ\Phi^{X_{j}^{(t_{1},\cdots,s_{i},\cdots,\hat{t_{j}},\cdots,t_{n})}}_{s_{j}^{\prime},s_{j}}=\Phi^{X_{j}^{(t_{1},\cdots,s_{i}^{\prime},\cdots,\hat{t_{j}},\cdots,t_{n})}}_{s_{j}^{\prime},s_{j}}\circ\Phi^{X_{i}^{(t_{1},\cdots,\hat{t_{i}},\cdots,s_{j},\cdots,t_{n})}}_{s_{i}^{\prime},s_{i}}

Hence, we can construct
\sigma
as

\sigma(t_{1},\cdots,t_{n})=\Phi^{X_{n}^{(t_{1},\cdots,t_{n-1},\hat{t_{n}})}}_{t_{n},0}\circ\cdots\Phi^{X_{i}^{(t_{1},\cdots,\hat{t_{i}},0,\cdots,0)}}_{t_{i},0}\circ\cdots\Phi^{X_{1}^{(t_{1},0,\cdots,0)}}_{t_{1},0}

Next, we want to show
a
defines a Lie algebroid morphism, i.e. the induced map
a^{*}:\operatorname{CE}(A)^{\bullet}\to\Omega^{\bullet}\Delta_{n}
is a dga morphism. Note that it suffices to check on degree 0 and 1, where all higher degree terms follow from Leibniz rules.

Let
\{e_{1},\cdots,e_{m}\}
be a basis of sections of
A
around an open neighborhood
U
of
\sigma(t_{0})=x_{0}
. Let
c^{l}_{p,q}
be the structure constants of
A
on
U
, i.e.
[e_{i},e_{j}]=\sum_{l=1}^{m}c^{l}_{p,q}e_{l}
for
p,q=1,\cdots m
.
Write
\alpha^{t}_{i}=\sum_{p=1}^{m}\alpha^{t}_{i,p}e_{p}
, then let
a_{i}=\alpha_{i}\circ\sigma

a(t)=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{p=1}^{m}a_{i,p}(t)e_{p}(t)\otimes dt_{i}

First let
f\in\operatorname{CE}(A)^{0}={\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}(M)
, we have

\displaystyle<a^{*}\circ d_{A}(f),\partial t_{i}>(t)

\displaystyle=<df(\sigma_{t}),\rho\circ a(\partial t_{i})>

\displaystyle=<df(\sigma_{t}),\rho\circ\alpha^{t}_{i}>

\displaystyle=<df,X^{t}_{i}>(\sigma(t))

\displaystyle=\frac{d}{dt_{i}}(f\circ\sigma(t))

\displaystyle=<d\circ a^{*}(f),\partial t_{t}>(t).

Hence
d_{\sigma}\circ a^{*}=a^{*}\circ d_{A}
for degree 0.
Now let’s verify the case for degree 1. Note that it suffices to check the dual basis
e_{i}^{*}
.

\displaystyle<h^{*}d_{A}(e_{l}),\partial t_{i}\wedge\partial t_{j}>

\displaystyle=<h^{*}\big(\sum_{p,q=1}^{m}c^{l}_{p,q}e^{*}_{p}\wedge e^{*}_{q}\big),\partial t_{i}\wedge\partial t_{j}>

\displaystyle=<\big(\sum_{p,q=1}^{m}c^{l}_{p,q}e^{*}_{p}\wedge e^{*}_{q}\big),h(\partial t_{i})\wedge h(\partial t_{j})>

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{p,q=1}^{m}c^{l}_{i,j}(a_{i,p}a_{j,q}-a_{i,q}a_{j,p})

\displaystyle=\frac{da_{i,l}}{dt_{j}}-\frac{da_{j,l}}{dt_{i}}

where the last equality is due to
[\alpha_{i},\alpha_{j}]=\frac{d\alpha_{i}}{dt_{j}}-\frac{d\alpha_{j}}{dt_{i}}
. Note that

\displaystyle<h^{*}(e_{l}^{*}),\partial t_{i}>=

\displaystyle<e_{l}^{*},\sum_{p=1}^{m}a_{i,p}e_{p}>

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle a_{i,l}

we have

\displaystyle\frac{da_{i,l}}{dt_{j}}-\frac{da_{j,l}}{dt_{i}}=<d_{\sigma}\circ h^{*}(e_{l}^{*}),\partial t_{i}\wedge\partial t_{j}>

Therefore, we have
d_{\sigma}\circ h^{*}=h^{*}\circ d_{A}
.
∎

Next, we want to show that all Lie algebroid morphisms can be obtained in this way.

Lemma 24.4
.

Let
\alpha^{0}_{1},\cdots,\alpha^{0}_{n}
be a family of
t
-time-dependent sections of
A
for
t\in\Delta_{n}
, which satisfy

[\alpha_{i}^{0},\alpha_{j}^{0}]=\frac{d\alpha_{i}^{0}}{dt_{j}}-\frac{d\alpha_{j}^{0}}{dt_{i}}

for
i,j=1,\cdots,n
. Suppose we have another family of sections
\alpha_{n+1}
which depends on
t^{\prime}\in\Delta_{n+1}
and satisfying

\displaystyle[\alpha_{k},\alpha_{n+1}]=

\displaystyle\frac{d\alpha_{k}}{dt_{n+1}}-\frac{d\alpha_{n+1}}{dt_{k}}

\displaystyle\alpha_{k}|_{t_{n+1}=0}=

\displaystyle\alpha_{k}^{0}

then
[\alpha_{i},\alpha_{j}]=\frac{d\alpha_{i}}{dt_{j}}-\frac{d\alpha_{j}}{dt_{i}}
are satisfied for all
i,j=1\cdots,n+1
.

Proof.

We only need to verify the case for
i,j=1,\cdots,n
. Let
\phi_{i,j}=[\alpha_{i},\alpha_{j}]-\frac{d\alpha_{i}}{dt_{j}}-\frac{d\alpha_{j}}{dt_{i}}
. Differentiate
\phi_{i,j}
in
t_{n+1}
we get

\displaystyle\frac{d\phi_{i,j}}{dt_{n+1}}=

\displaystyle[\alpha_{n+1},\phi_{i,j}]

∎

Proposition 24.5
.

Let
a:\sum_{i=1}^{n}a_{i}dt_{i}:T\Delta_{n}\to A
be a Lie algebroid morphism, then there exists a family of time-dependent sections
\alpha^{t}_{1},\cdots,\alpha^{t}_{n}
such that

\displaystyle[\alpha_{i},\alpha_{j}]

\displaystyle=\frac{d\alpha_{i}}{dt_{j}}-\frac{d\alpha_{j}}{dt_{i}}

\displaystyle a_{k}

\displaystyle=\alpha_{k}\circ\sigma

Proof.

The case for
n=1
is obvious. We shall use the previous lemma and prove by induction. Suppose we have shown the case for
n=k
. Let
a:T\Delta_{n+1}\to A
be a Lie algebroid morphism.

First, extend
a_{1}|_{\{0=t_{n}=\cdots=t_{2}\leq t_{1}\leq 1\}}
to a
t_{1}
-time-dependent section
\alpha_{1}
. Next, we extend
a_{2}|_{\{0=t_{n}=\cdots=t_{3}\leq t_{2}\leq t_{1}\leq 1\}}
to a
(t_{1},t_{2})
-time-dependent section
\alpha_{2}
. Then we construct
\alpha_{1}
as solution to

[\alpha_{2},\alpha_{1}]=\frac{d\alpha_{2}}{dt_{1}}-\frac{d\alpha_{1}}{dt_{2}}

with initial condition
\alpha_{1}|_{\{0=t_{n}=\cdots=t_{3}\leq t_{2}\leq t_{1}\leq 1\}}
constructed as in the previous step. Continuing this fashion, we extend
\alpha_{i}
to a
(t_{1},\cdots,t_{i})
-time-dependent section of
A
, which satisfied the equation

[\alpha_{k},\alpha_{i}]=\frac{d\alpha_{k}}{dt_{i}}-\frac{d\alpha_{i}}{dt_{k}}

for
1\leq k<i
, with initial conditions
\alpha_{k}|_{\{0=\cdots=t_{i}\leq\cdots\leq t_{1}\leq 1\}}
.
a_{i}=\alpha_{i}\circ\sigma
is obvious by construction.
∎

24.2.
Homotopy and monodromy

Definition 24.6
.

Let
a_{0},a_{1}:T\Delta^{n}\to A
be two
n
-simplices, we say
a_{0}
and
a_{1}
are
homotopic
if there exists a Lie algebroid morphism
h=\sum_{k=1}^{n+1}h_{k}dt_{k}:T\Delta^{n}\times T\Delta^{1}\to A
.

(1)

a_{\epsilon}=\sum_{k=1}^{n}h_{k}(t_{1},\cdots,t_{n},\epsilon)dt_{k}
for
\epsilon=0,1;

(2)

h_{n+1}
vanishes on the boundary of
\Delta^{n}
.

It is easy to show that homotopies define an equivalence relation on the space of
n
-simplices.

Lemma 24.7
.

A map
T\Delta^{n}\to A
which vanishes on
\partial\Delta^{n}
is homotopic to a map vanishing on
T\partial\Delta^{n}
.

Proof.

First choose a cut-off function
\tau\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}})
with
\tau(0)=0,\tau(1)=1
, and
\tau^{\prime}(t)>0
for
t\in(0,1)
.
Define
h:\Delta^{n}\times\Delta^{1}\to\Delta^{n}
by

h(t_{1},\cdots,t_{n},t_{n+1})=\Big(\big(1-\tau(t_{n+1})\big)t_{1}+\tau(t_{n+1})t_{1},\cdots,\big(1-\tau(t_{n+1})\big)t_{n}+\tau(t_{n+1})t_{n}\Big)

write
\tau(t_{1},\cdots,t_{n})=\big(\tau(t_{1}),\cdots,\tau(t_{n})\big).
Then
a\circ dh
gives a homotopy between
a
and
a\circ d\tau
. Clearly
a\circ d\tau
vanishes on
T\partial\Delta^{n}

∎

Given two simplices
a^{i}:T\Delta^{n}\to A
, we want to define the concatenation of them. The idea is to concatenate in the
t_{n}
-direction, but we have to be careful since the naive concatenation might not be smooth. First, in order to concatenate two simplices, we assume
d_{1}a^{1}=d^{2}a^{2}
. We define the concatenation
a^{1}\odot_{t_{1}}a^{2}
by

a^{1}\odot_{t_{1}}a^{2}=\begin{cases}(dr_{1}^{\tau}*a^{0})\circ dp_{0}\quad t_{1}\in[0,1/2]\\
(dr_{1}^{\tau}*a^{1})\circ dp_{1}\end{cases}

where
dr_{n}^{\tau}:T\Delta^{n}\to T\Delta^{n}
is the tangent map to
r_{1}^{\tau}(t_{1},\cdots,t_{n})\to(\tau(t_{1}),\cdots,t_{n})
, and
p_{i}
are maps which reparametrize the first coordinates

\displaystyle p_{0}(t_{1},\cdots,t_{n})

\displaystyle=\Big(\tilde{p}_{0}^{-1}(t_{1}),t_{2}\cdots,t_{n}\Big)

\displaystyle p_{1}(t_{1},\cdots,t_{n})

\displaystyle=\Big(\tilde{p}_{1}^{-1}(t_{1}),t_{2}\cdots,t_{n}\Big)

where

\displaystyle\tilde{p}_{0}(t_{1})=

\displaystyle\frac{(1-t_{1})t_{1}}{1-t_{2}}+\frac{(t_{1}-t_{2})(1+t_{2})}{2(1-t_{2})}

\displaystyle\tilde{p}_{1}(t_{1})=

\displaystyle\frac{(t_{1}-t_{2})t_{1}}{1-t_{2}}+\frac{(1-t_{1})(1+t_{2})}{2(1-t_{2})}

Based on the property of
\tau
which smoothen the boundary of the simplices, it is easy to get

Lemma 24.8
.

The concatenation map
a^{1}\circ_{t_{1}}a^{2}:T\Delta^{n}\coprod T\Delta^{n}\to A
of
n
-simplices is smooth.

Next, let’s define the homotopy groups of Lie algebroids in terms of simplices.
Let
A
be a Lie algebroid. Its monodromy
\infty
-groupoid is defined as the simplicial manifold
\operatorname{Mon}A
with
n
-simplices

\operatorname{Mon}^{\infty}(A)_{n}=\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathsf{Algd}}(T\Delta^{n},A)

We define the
isotropy
n
-simplices
at
x
or
A
-spheres
based at
x
to be

\operatorname{Mon}^{\infty}_{x}(A)_{n}=\{g\in\operatorname{Mon}_{n}(A):\pi(g|_{\partial\Delta})=x\}

We define
trivial isotropy
n
-simplices
to be those isotropy simplices whose base simplices are contractible

\operatorname{Mon}^{\infty}_{x}(A)_{n}^{0}=\{g\in\operatorname{Mon}^{\infty}_{x}(A)_{n}:\pi(g)=\sim*\}

Note that all isotropy simplices at
x
lie in a single leaf. According to our construction, we have

Lemma 24.9
.

\operatorname{Mon}^{\infty}_{x}(A)_{n}^{0}
is the connected component of the identity of
\operatorname{Mon}^{\infty}_{x}(A)_{n}
. We have a short exact sequence

1\to\operatorname{Mon}^{\infty}_{x}(A)_{n}^{0}\to\operatorname{Mon}^{\infty}_{x}(A)_{n}\to\pi_{n}(L_{x})\to 1

where
L_{x}
denote the leaf of the foliation that
x
sits in.

Recall that, when we restrict to a leaf, we have the following exact sequence of Lie algebroids

0\to\mathfrak{g}_{L_{x}}\to A_{L_{x}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rho}}{{\to}}TL_{x}\to 0

where
\mathfrak{g}_{L_{x}}
is the bundle of isotropy Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}_{x}
.

Proposition 24.10
.

There exists a long exact sequence

(24.2)

\cdots\pi_{i+1}(L_{x})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\partial}}{{\to}}\operatorname{Mon}^{\infty}_{x}(\mathfrak{g}_{L_{x}})_{i}\to\operatorname{Mon}^{\infty}_{x}(A_{L_{x}})_{i}\to\pi_{i}(L_{x})\to\cdots

Proof.

First, we need to construct the boundary map
\partial:\pi_{i+1}(L_{x})\to\operatorname{Mon}^{\infty}_{x}(\mathfrak{g}_{L_{x}})_{i}
. Let
[\sigma]\in\pi_{i+1}(L_{x})
, i.e.
\sigma:\Delta^{n+1}\to L_{x}
such that
\sigma(\partial\Delta^{n})=x
. Let
\sum_{i=1}^{n+1}a_{i}dt_{i}:T\Delta^{n}\to A_{L_{x}}
be any Lie algebroid morphism which lifts
d\sigma:T\Delta^{n}\to TL_{x}
such that
a_{i}|_{\Delta^{n}}=0
for
1\leq i\leq n
and
a_{n+1}|_{\partial\Delta^{n}}=0
, where
\Delta^{n}=\{1\geq t_{1}\geq\cdots\geq t_{n}\geq 0\}=d_{n+1}\Delta^{n}
. Let
\Lambda^{n+1}_{n+1}
be the
(n+1)
-th horn of
\Delta^{n+1}
as usual, then after simple reparametrization,
a(T\Lambda^{n+1}_{n+1})
gives a map
\Delta_{n}\to\mathfrak{g}_{L_{x}}
since
\sigma
is constant on the boundary. Therefore, we define
\partial(\sigma)=[a(T\Lambda^{n+1}_{n+1})]\in\operatorname{Mon}^{\infty}_{x}(\mathfrak{g}_{L_{x}})_{i}
. Now it suffices to show, for two homotopic simplices
\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}
, their image under
\partial
are homotopic as
\mathfrak{g}_{L_{x}}
-paths.

Let
a^{i}:T\Delta^{n}\to A_{L_{x}}
be some lifts of
\sigma^{i}
, we want to show
\partial(a^{i})=a^{i}(T\Lambda^{n+1}_{n+1})
are homotopic. In order to do this, we will construct an explicit homotopy
h:T\Delta^{n}\times T\Delta^{1}\to A_{L_{x}}
. By assumption, there exists a homotopy
\sigma^{s}(t)=\sigma(t,s):\Delta^{n+1}\times\Delta^{1}\to L_{x},s\in\Delta^{1}
of
\sigma^{1}
and
\sigma^{2}
. Choose a homotopy
a^{s}_{n+1}(t)=a_{n+1}(t,s):T\Delta^{n}\times T\Delta^{1}\to A_{L_{x}}
such that,

\rho(a_{n+1}(t,s))=\frac{d\sigma^{s}(t)}{dt_{n+1}},\quad a^{n+1}(t,s)|_{\partial\Delta^{n}}=0

Let
\alpha_{n+1}(t,s,\sigma(t,s))
be the corresponding time-dependent sections of
A_{L_{x}}
. Consider the solutions of the following system

\begin{cases}\frac{d\alpha_{i}}{dt_{n+1}}-\frac{d\alpha_{n+1}}{dt_{i}}=[\alpha_{i},\alpha_{n+1}]\quad 1\leq i\leq n\\
\frac{d\beta}{dt_{n+1}}-\frac{d\alpha_{n+1}}{ds}=[\beta,\alpha_{n+1}]\\
\alpha_{i}|_{\partial\Delta^{n+1}}=0\\
\beta|_{\partial\Delta^{n+1}}=0\par\end{cases}

Note that
\alpha^{i}(t,s,\sigma(t,s))=a^{i}(t,s)
. By similar arguments as in Lemma
24.4
, we have

\frac{d\alpha_{i}}{ds}-\frac{d\beta}{dt_{i}}=[\alpha_{i},\beta]\quad 1\leq i\leq n

Since
\beta|_{\partial\Delta^{n}}=0
, it gives a homotopy between
a^{1}
and
a^{2}
.

Next, we want to show the sequence is exact. It suffices to verify the exactness at
\operatorname{Mon}^{\infty}_{x}(\mathfrak{g}_{L_{x}})_{i}
for all
i\geq 1
. By construction, the image of
\partial
consists of
∎

Now, if we glue everything in the above exact sequence by the leave
L_{x}
, and regard all objects as bundles of groups over
M
, we get

Corollary 24.11
.

There exists a long exact sequence of bundle of groups

(24.3)

\cdots\pi_{i+1}(L_{x})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\partial}}{{\to}}\operatorname{Mon}^{\infty}(\mathfrak{g}_{L_{x}})_{i}\to\operatorname{Mon}^{\infty}(A)_{i}\to\pi_{i}(L_{x})\to\cdots

Now we have a relation between isotropy
n
-simplices,
A
-simplices, and simplices along the foliation, where each of them corresponds to
n
-simplices of
\infty
-groupoids. Once we add back the simplicial structures, we get

Proposition 24.12
.

There exists a fiber sequence of Lie
\infty
-groupoids

(24.4)

\coprod_{L_{x}}\operatorname{Mon}^{\infty}(\mathfrak{g}_{L_{x}})\to\operatorname{Mon}^{\infty}(A)\to\Pi_{\infty}({\mathcal{F}})

We call the boundary map
\partial
to be
monodromy morphism
. As we know for
i=2
, it corresponds to the classical monodromy morphism and its image in
\operatorname{Mon}^{\infty}(\mathfrak{g}_{L_{x}})
controls the integrability of Lie algebroids.

Recall
\operatorname{Mon}^{\infty}(A)_{n}
consists of all
C^{1}

n
-simplices
\tilde{\sigma}:\Delta^{n}\to A
which sits above its projection
\sigma=\pi(\tilde{\sigma})
in
M
, and satisfies

\rho(a(t))=\frac{d(\pi\circ a(t))}{dt}

In order to help us study the smooth structures on
\operatorname{Mon}^{\infty}(A)_{n}
, let’s first look at the larger space
\tilde{P}_{n}(A)
which consists of
C^{1}

n
-simplices
\tilde{\sigma}:\Delta^{n}\to A
over some base
C^{2}
-simplices in
M
. It’s easy to see that
\tilde{P}_{n}(A)
is a Banach manifold, and
\operatorname{Mon}^{\infty}(A)_{n}
is a submanifold of
\tilde{P}_{n}(A)
. The tangent space of a simplex
\tilde{\sigma}(t)
consists of all
C^{0}

A
-sections over
\sigma(t)
. Using a connection
\nabla
on
A
, we can view an element in
T_{\tilde{\sigma}}\tilde{P}_{n}(A)
as a pair
(u,\phi)
, where
u:\Delta^{n}\to A
and
\phi:\Delta^{n}\to TM
are both simplices over the base simplex
\sigma
.

Lemma 24.13
.

\delta\in T_{a}\operatorname{Mon}^{\infty}(A)_{n}
have decomposition
\tilde{\delta}(t)=\big(u(t),\phi(t)\big)
such that

\rho(u)=\overline{\nabla}_{a(t)}\phi(t)

for
t=(t_{1},\cdots,t_{n})\in\{1\geq t_{1}\geq\cdots\geq t_{n}\geq 0\}
.

Proof.

Consider a smooth map
F:\tilde{P}_{n}(A)\to\tilde{P}_{n}(TM)
defined by
F(\tilde{\sigma}(t))=\rho(\sigma(t))-D\sigma(t)
. Here we use
D
denote the gradient
D=\sum_{i}\frac{d}{dt_{i}}
in order to distinguish it from the connection. Let
\tilde{P}_{n}^{0}(TM)
denotes the submanifold of paths with zero value in the fiber in
\tilde{P}_{n}(TM)
. Then it suffices to show
F
is a submersion onto
\tilde{P}_{n}^{0}(TM)
, and
F^{-1}\tilde{P}_{n}^{0}(TM)=\operatorname{Mon}^{\infty}(A)_{n}
.
Let’s restrict to the differential of
F
onto
\tilde{P}_{n}^{0}(TM)

dF:T_{\tilde{\sigma}}\tilde{P}_{n}(A)\to T_{0_{\sigma}}\tilde{P}(TM)

Here
0_{\sigma}
denotes the canonical lift of
\sigma:\Delta^{n}\to M
to
\Delta\to TM
with zero values in the fiber.
Note that
T_{0_{\sigma}}\tilde{P}(TM)
consists of all smooth sections of
TTM
over
\sigma
. The image of
x\in\Delta^{n}
of
dF
is

T_{0_{x}}\tilde{P}(TM)\simeq\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}T_{0_{x}}TM\simeq\bigoplus_{i=1}^{n}T_{x}M\oplus T_{x}M

by the canonical splitting of
T_{0_{x}}T_{M}
. We claim that, for any connection
\nabla
which splits
\tilde{\sigma}
as
(u,\phi)
, the vertical and horizontal components of the splitting
T_{0_{x}}TM\simeq T_{x}M\oplus T_{x}M
are
\rho(u)-\overline{\nabla}_{a}\phi
and
\phi
respectively.
Let
m=\dim M,k=\dim A
. Let
x=\{x_{1},\cdots,x_{m}\}
be a local chart of
M
, and
\{\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{i}}\}
be a local basis of
TM
, then denote the horizontal and vertical basis of
T_{0_{x}}TM
by
\{\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{i}}\}
and
\{\frac{\delta}{\delta x_{i}}\}
respectively. Without loss of generality, we assume
\nabla
is the standard flat connection.
Now let
\tilde{\sigma}(t)=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\tilde{\sigma}_{i}(t)e_{i}

∎

Next, we want to show that the homotopy of
n
-simplices actually induces a (infinite dimensional) foliation on
\operatorname{Mon}^{\infty}(A)_{n}

Proposition 24.14
.

There exists a foliation
{\mathcal{F}}_{n}
on
{\mathcal{G}}_{n}
with finite codimension.

First, let
\sigma
be an
n
-simplex in
M
, consider a subspace
\tilde{P}_{n}^{\sigma}(A)
of
\tilde{P}_{n}(A)
defined by

\tilde{P}_{n}^{\sigma}(A)=\{\gamma\in\tilde{P}_{n}(A):\gamma(0)=0,\gamma(t)\in A_{\sigma(t)}\}

that is,
\tilde{P}_{n}^{\sigma}(A)
consists of sections of
A
over
\sigma
with initial condition
\gamma(0)=0
. Let
\nabla
be a connection on
A
, and
\tilde{\sigma}
be an
A
-simplex over
\sigma
.

Proposition 24.15
.

The
n
-truncation of
{\mathcal{G}}_{\bullet}
is a Lie
n
-groupoid.

24.3.
Integrating
L_{\infty}
-algebroids

Let
\mathfrak{g}
be an
L_{\infty}
algebroids over a smooth manifold
M
which is positively graded. Define a simplicial manifold
\operatorname{Mon}_{\bullet}
whose
n
-simplices are

\operatorname{Mon}_{n}=\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathsf{dgCAlg}}\big(\operatorname{CE}(\mathfrak{g}),\Omega(\Delta_{n})\big)=\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathtt{L_{\infty}Algd}}(T\Delta_{n}),\mathfrak{g})

Note that when
\mathfrak{g}
is a Lie algebroid, then
\operatorname{Mon}_{\bullet}
coincide with the construction in previous section.

Proposition 24.16
.

[
SS19
]

\operatorname{Mon}_{\bullet}
is a Lie
\infty
-groupoid where each
\operatorname{Mon}_{n}
is a Frechét manifold.

In this section, we shall prove an enhancement to the above result, which gives an
n
-truncation of integration of
L_{\infty}
-algebroids.

Proposition 24.17
.

Let
\mathfrak{g}
be an
L_{\infty}
algebroid over a smooth manifold
M
