, with the underlying dg Module being perfect and concentrated in degree
[-n,0]
. Then
\mathfrak{g}
integrates to a Lie
n
-algebroid which is an
n
-truncation of
{\mathcal{G}}_{\bullet}
.

24.4.
Local holonomy
\infty
-groupoid

In this section, we will study the higher holonomy defined by monodromy morphisms. First, we will study the local structures. Let
L
be a singular leaf of
{\mathcal{F}}
.

Recall that a fibration
P:E_{\bullet}\to F_{\bullet}
in the semi-model category
\mathsf{L_{\infty}Algd}^{\operatorname{dg}}_{A^{\bullet}}
is a degree-wise surjection. In particular, if
F_{\bullet}
is a Lie algebroid, the
P
degenerates to a surjection
E_{-1}\to F_{-1}
. Now consider in a fibration in
\mathsf{L_{\infty}Algd}^{\operatorname{dg}}
, we define a fibration
P:E_{\bullet}\to F_{\bullet}
to be a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E_{\bullet}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{\bullet}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}

such that
P
is a degreewise
L_{\infty}
surjection and
p
is a surjective submersion.

Definition 24.18
.

Let
P:E_{\bullet}\to F_{\bullet}
be a
L_{\infty}
-algebroid fibration. An
Ehresmann connection
for
P
is a graded vector sub-bundle
H_{\bullet}\subset E_{\bullet}
such that
H_{\bullet}\oplus\ker(P)=E_{\bullet}
.

Given an Ehresmann connection, we can lift a section of
F_{\bullet}
to a unique section of
E_{\bullet}
, which is called a
horizontal lift
. Moreover,
\rho_{E_{\bullet}}(\sigma(a))
is
p
-related to
\rho_{F_{\bullet}}(a)
.

Example 24.19
.

Let
E_{\bullet}
and
F_{\bullet}
be the tangent Lie algebroids
TM
and
TN
respectively. Then we recover the usual definition of manifold fibrations (surjective submersion). On the other hand, let
E_{\bullet}
and
F_{\bullet}
be ordinary Lie algebras, we recover Lie algebra epimorphisms.

Definition 24.20
.

We say an Ehresmann connection is complete if the for any complete vector field
\rho(\alpha)
,
\rho(\sigma(\alpha))
is complete, where
\sigma:\Gamma(F_{\bullet})\to\Gamma(E_{\bullet})
is a lift induced by the connection.

Let’s look at the fiber of an
L_{\infty}
-fibration. By definition, we have a graded vector bundle
K_{\bullet}=\ker(P)\subset E_{\bullet}
over
M
. We can then restrict the
k
-ary brackets on
E_{\bullet}
to
K_{\bullet}
, i.e.

l_{k}^{K_{\bullet}}(e_{1},\cdots,e_{k})=\pi(l_{k}^{E_{\bullet}}(e_{1},\cdots,e_{k}))

for
e_{i}\in\Gamma(K_{\bullet})
, and
\pi:E_{\bullet}\to K_{\bullet}
is the projection map.

Proposition 24.21
.

Let
P:E_{\bullet}\to F_{\bullet}
be an
L_{\infty}
-algebroid fibration over
p:M\to N
, then
K_{\bullet}=\ker(P)|_{p^{-1}(x)}
inherits an
L_{\infty}
-algebroid structure over an
L_{\infty}
-algebroid fibration for any
x\in N
.

Proof.

Fix
x\in N
. Clearly e
K_{\bullet}
is a graded vector bundle over
p^{-1}(x)
, hence it suffices to show that the brackets
l_{k}^{K_{\bullet}}
is well-defined and satisfies the homotopy Jacobi identities. By an analogue of Frobenius theorem for
L_{\infty}
-algebroids, it suffices to show that

\operatorname{Ann}(\ker P)\simeq\operatorname{Im}:\operatorname{CE}(P)
, where
\operatorname{CE}(P):{\mathcal{O}}(E_{\bullet})\to{\mathcal{O}}(F_{\bullet})
is the induced map on Chevalley-Eilenberg algebras, is
d^{\operatorname{CE}}
-closed. This follows from the fact that

d^{\operatorname{CE}(E_{\bullet})}\circ f({\mathcal{O}}(F_{\bullet}))=f\circ d^{\operatorname{CE}(F_{\bullet})}({\mathcal{O}}(F_{\bullet}))

and the homotopy Jacobi identity follows directly from
E_{\bullet}
.
∎

Next, we will show that
\ker(P)|_{p^{-1}(l)}
can be patched together when we have a complete Ehresmann connection. We will need the following lemma.

Lemma 24.22

(
[
LR19
]
)
.

Let
\mathfrak{g}
be an
L_{\infty}
-algebroid over
A={\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}(M)
.
Let
X
be a degree zero vector field on
{\mathcal{O}}(\mathfrak{g})
, i.e. a degree zero element in the tangent complex
T_{\mathfrak{g}}
, then

(1)

For all fixed
t\in{\mathbb{R}}
,
X
admits a time-
t
flow
\Phi^{X}_{t}:{\mathcal{O}}(\mathfrak{g})\to{\mathcal{O}}(\mathfrak{g})
if and only if the induced vector field
\tilde{X}
on
M
admits a time-
t
flow.

(2)

Assume
X
is
d_{{\mathcal{O}}(\mathfrak{g})}
-closed, i.e.
[d_{{\mathcal{O}}(\mathfrak{g}},X]=0
. Then the flow
\Phi^{X}_{t}:{\mathcal{O}}(\mathfrak{g})\to{\mathcal{O}}(\mathfrak{g})
is an
L_{\infty}
-morphism for any admissible
t
.

(3)

Assume
X
is exact, i.e. there exists a
Y
such that
[d_{{\mathcal{O}}(\mathfrak{g})},Y]=X
, then there exists an
L_{\infty}
-morphism
\Phi^{Y}:{\mathcal{O}}(\mathfrak{g})\otimes{\mathbb{R}}\to{\mathcal{O}}(\mathfrak{g})
defined in a small neighborhood of
{\mathcal{O}}(\mathfrak{g})\otimes\{0\}
, such that the restriction
\Phi^{X}_{t}:{\mathcal{O}}(\mathfrak{g})\to{\mathcal{O}}(\mathfrak{g})
if the flow of
[d_{{\mathcal{O}}(\mathfrak{g})},Y]
at time
t
for all admissible time
t
. Also, we have that all
\Phi^{X}_{t}
’s are homotopic
L_{\infty}
-morphisms.

Proof.

See
[
LR19
]
Lemma 1.6.
∎

Given an
L_{\infty}
-algebroids fibration
P:E_{\bullet}\to F_{\bullet}
, we can regard a section of
E_{0}
as a degree -1 vector field on
{\mathcal{O}}(E_{\bullet})
by left contraction (note the degree shift here in
{\mathcal{O}}(E_{\bullet})
). Let
X\in\Gamma(F_{0})
, then the degree zero vector fields
[\iota_{\sigma(X)},d_{{\mathcal{O}}(E_{\bullet})}]
and
[\iota_{X},d_{{\mathcal{O}}(F_{\bullet})}]
are
P
-related for every Ehresmann connection
K_{\bullet}
.

Lemma 24.23
.

Let
K_{\bullet}
be an Ehresmann connection for an
L_{\infty}
-algebroid fibration
P:E_{\bullet}\to F_{\bullet}
, then
K_{\bullet}
is complete if and only if for any
X\in\Gamma(F_{0})
, the time-
t
flow of
[\iota_{\sigma(X)},d_{{\mathcal{O}}(E_{\bullet})}]
is defined if and only if the time
t
flow of

[\iota_{X},d_{{\mathcal{O}}(F_{\bullet})}]
is defined.

Proof.

By (1) in the previous lemma, the flow of a degree 0 vector field exists if and only if its induced vector fields on the base manifold exists. Note that the induced vector fields of
[\iota_{\sigma(X)},d_{{\mathcal{O}}(E_{\bullet})}]
and
[\iota_{X},d_{{\mathcal{O}}(F_{\bullet})}]
are
\rho_{E_{\bullet}}(\sigma(X))
and
\rho_{F_{\bullet}}(X)
respectively. Therefore, the result follows directly from the definition.
∎

A complete Ehresmann connection allows us to identify different fibers.

Lemma 24.24
.

Let
K_{\bullet}
be an Ehresmann connection for an
L_{\infty}
-algebroid fibration
P:E_{\bullet}\to F_{\bullet}
. Suppose the anchor map of
F_{\bullet}
is surjective, then the fibers
\mathcal{T}_{x}
and
\mathcal{T}_{y}
for
x,y\in N
are isomorphic as
L_{\infty}
-algebroids.

Proof.

By assumption, there exists a vector field
Z
on
N
whose time 1 flow globally, and maps
x
to
y
. By surjectivity of
\rho_{F_{\bullet}}
, we can lift
Z
to a section
X
of
F_{0}
. From previous lemma, we know
[\iota_{\sigma(X)},d_{{\mathcal{O}}(E_{\bullet})}]
is
p
-related to
Z
. Since the time-1 flow of
X
is well-defined, we know the time-1 flow
\Phi_{1}
of
[\iota_{\sigma(X)},d_{{\mathcal{O}}(E_{\bullet})}]
is well-defined and is an
L_{\infty}
-isomorphism. Note that
Phi_{1}
induces a diffeomorphism
\phi_{1}:M\to M
which is over the time-1 flow of
X
, hence it maps the fiber
p^{-1}(x)
to
p^{-1}(y)
. There, we see
\Phi_{1}
restricts to an isomorphism from
\mathcal{T}_{x}
to
\mathcal{T}_{y}
.
∎

For general
\rho_{F_{\bullet}}
, we have the following local result.

Lemma 24.25
.

Let
K_{\bullet}
be an Ehresmann connection for an
L_{\infty}
-algebroid fibration
P:E_{\bullet}\to F_{\bullet}
. Let
x,y
lie in a single leaf in the singular foliation associated to

on

, then the fibers
\mathcal{T}_{x}
and
\mathcal{T}_{y}
for
x,y\in N
are isomorphic as
L_{\infty}
-algebroids.

Proof.

It suffices to consider the anchor map of
F_{\bullet}
is not surjective. Then result follows from the previous lemma by replacing
F_{\bullet}
with
F_{\bullet}|_{L}
, where
L
is the leaf containing both
x
and
y
.
∎

Corollary 24.26
.

\ker(P)_{p^{-1}(x)}
glues to an
L_{\infty}
-algebroid
\mathcal{T}
over
M
.

Proof.

Classical partition of unity type argument.
∎

By construction
dp\circ\rho_{K_{\bullet}}(\Gamma(K_{0}))=\rho_{F_{\bullet}}\circ P(\Gamma(K_{0}))=0\subset\Gamma(TN)
, so
K_{\bullet}
restricts to the fiber of
p
, i.e. given
x\in N
, we have an
L_{\infty}
-algebroid
K_{\bullet}^{l}
over
p^{-1}(x)
.

Example 24.27
.

In
[

]
, Lie algebroid fibrations corresponding to
L_{\infty}
-fibration in our sense for Lie algebroids as
L_{\infty}

Proposition 24.28
.

Let
P:E_{\bullet}\to F_{\bullet}
be an
L_{\infty}
-algebroid fibration over
p:M\to N
. Let
T_{x}
denote the fiber of

over

. Suppose

admits a complete Ehresmann connection. There exists a long exact sequence

(24.5)

\cdots\operatorname{Mon}^{\infty}_{i+1}(F_{\bullet})_{x}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\partial}}{{\to}}\operatorname{Mon}^{\infty}_{i}(T_{x})_{y}\to\operatorname{Mon}^{\infty}_{i}(E_{\bullet})_{y}\to\operatorname{Mon}^{\infty}_{i}(F_{\bullet})_{x}\to\cdots

Note that the boundary map is exactly the monodromy homomorphism

\partial:\operatorname{Mon}^{\infty}_{i+1}(F_{\bullet})_{x}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\Gamma(\operatorname{Mon}^{\infty}_{i}(T_{x}))

.

Now consider
E_{\bullet}
to be the universal
L_{\infty}
-algebroid associated to
{\mathcal{F}}
, and let
L
be a locally closed leaf.

Definition 24.29
.

Let
L
be a locally closed singular leaf of
{\mathcal{F}}
. An
Ehresmann
{\mathcal{F}}
-connection
consists of a triple
(
an Ehresmann connection
H
of a projection
p:M_{L}\to L
of a neighborhood
M_{L}\subset M
of
L
. We say an Ehresmann
{\mathcal{F}}
-connection
(M_{L},p,H)
is complete near
L
is
H
is complete.

Recall that we have the following local splitting property of singular foliations

Theorem 24.30

(local splitting)
.

Let
(M,{\mathcal{F}})
be a singular foliation. Let
x\in M
be arbitrary,
k=\dim(F_{x})
, and
\hat{S}
a slice at
x
, i.e. an embedded submanifold of
M
such that
T_{x}\hat{S}\oplus F_{x}=T_{x}M
. Then there exists an open neighborhood
U
of
x
in
M
and a foliated diffeomorphism
(U,{\mathcal{F}}|_{U})\simeq(I^{k},TI^{k})\times(S,{\mathcal{F}}_{S}
, where
S=\hat{S}\cap U
,
{\mathcal{F}}|_{U}
is the restriction of
{\mathcal{F}}
to
U
,
I=(-1,1)
, and
{\mathcal{F}}_{S}={\mathcal{F}}|_{U}\cap\Gamma(TS)
.

We have an analog for the Ehresmann
{\mathcal{F}}
-connection

Proposition 24.31

(local splitting by Ehresmann
{\mathcal{F}}
-connection)
.

Let
(M_{L},p,H)
be a complete Ehresmann
{\mathcal{F}}
-connection for a locally closed leaf
L
. For every
x\in L
, there exists a neighborhood
U\subset L
and a foliated diffeomorphism
p^{-1}(U)\simeq U\times p^{-1}(L)
which intertwining
{\mathcal{F}}|_{p^{-1}(U)}
and the product foliation
\Gamma(TU)\times{\mathcal{F}}_{p^{-1}(l)}
.

Proof.

Without loss of generality,
L\simeq I^{k}
where
k=\dim L
. It suffices to show that given a complete Ehresmann
{\mathcal{F}}
-connection
(M_{L},p,H)
for
L
, then there actually exists a flat complete Ehresmann
{\mathcal{F}}
-connection.

We shall proceed by induction. The case for
k=1
is trivial since any dimension 1 distribution is integrable. Suppose we have proved the result for some
k\in{\mathbb{N}}
. Let consider a complete Ehresmann
{\mathcal{F}}
-connection over
L=I^{k+1}
. Let
(t_{1},\cdots,t_{k},t_{k+1})
be a coordinate for
I^{k+1}
. Since
H
is complete, the horizontal lift of
\frac{\partial}{\partial t_{k+1}}
is complete, and its flow
\Psi_{t}:p^{-1}(I^{k}\times\{s\})\to p^{-1}(I^{k}\times\{s+t\})
preserves
{\mathcal{F}}
, where
t,s,s+t\in I
. Hence
(\Psi_{t})_{*}:{\mathcal{F}}|_{p^{-1}(I^{k}\times\{0\})}\simeq{\mathcal{F}}|_{p^{-1}(I^{k}\times\{t\})}
. By induction hypothesis, the projection
p^{-1}(I^{k}\times\{0\})\to(I^{k}\times\{0\})
admits a flat complete Ehresmann
{\mathcal{F}}
-connection
(M_{L},p,H^{\prime})
. Now we can use
\Psi_{t}
to transport
H^{\prime}
to get a flat complete Ehresmann
{\mathcal{F}}
-connection for all
p^{-1}(I^{k}\times\{t\})\to(I^{k}\times\{t\})
. Therefore, we have a new distribution

H^{\prime\prime}=<H(\frac{\partial}{\partial t_{k+1}})>\oplus H^{\prime}

Then it’s easy to verify that
H^{\prime\prime}
is actually the flat complete Ehresmann
{\mathcal{F}}
-connection we need.
∎

Consider the tangent Lie algebroid
TL
of a locally closed singular leaf
L
.

Lemma 24.32
.

Let
E_{\bullet}
be the
L_{\infty}
-algebroid resolving
{\mathcal{F}}
. Suppose
L
admits an Ehresmann
{\mathcal{F}}
connection
(M_{L},p,H)
, then the induced map
E_{\bullet}\to TL
is an
L_{\infty}
-algebroid fibration.

Proof.

P:E_{\bullet}\to TL
is the composition of the anchor map
\rho:E_{\bullet}\to TM
and the surjection
dp:TM\to TL
, which is clearly an
L_{\infty}
-morphism.
∎

Lemma 24.33
.

Suppose
L
admits an Ehresmann
{\mathcal{F}}
-connection
(M_{L},p,H)
, then there exists an Ehresmann connection for
P:E_{\bullet}\to TL
such that the only non-trivial term is
H_{0}\subset E_{0}
and
\rho(H_{0})=H
. Moreover,
H_{0}
is complete if and only if
H
is complete.

Proof.

We will do the construction locally and then the result follows from the standard partition of unity argument. Let
x\in L
, by assumption there exist a neighborhood
U_{x}
and
k
vector fields
X_{1},\cdots,X_{k}
generate
H
, where
k=\dim L
. Let
e_{i}\in\Gamma(E_{0})
be a lift of
X_{i}
for
1\leq i\leq k
, i.e.
dp\circ\rho(e_{i})=X_{i}
. Then
\{\mathcal{E}_{i}\}
generate the desired
H
on
U_{x}
.
∎

Suppose
L
admits a complete Ehresmann
{\mathcal{F}}
-connection
(M_{L},p,H)
, and denote the fiber over
x
to be
\mathcal{T}_{x}
, then we have an exact sequence

\mathcal{T}_{x}\to E_{\bullet}\to TL

Applying the previous result, we have

Proposition 24.34
.

Let
L
be a locally closed singular leaf of
{\mathcal{F}}
which admits a complete Ehresmann
{\mathcal{F}}
-connection
(M_{L},p,H)
, then
P:E_{\bullet}\to TL
is an
L_{\infty}
-algebroid fibration over
p:M_{L}\to L
. Let
\mathcal{T}_{x}
denote the fiber of
P
over
x\in L
, which corresponds to
L_{\infty}
-algebroid of the transversal foliation at
x
. There exists a long exact sequence

(24.6)

\cdots\pi_{i+1}(L,x)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\partial}}{{\to}}\operatorname{Mon}^{\infty}_{i}(\mathcal{T}_{x})_{y}\to\operatorname{Mon}^{\infty}_{i}(E_{\bullet})_{y}\to\pi_{i}(L,x)\to\cdots

Note that the boundary map is exactly the monodromy homomorphism

\partial:\pi_{i+1}(L,x)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}\Gamma(\operatorname{Mon}^{\infty}_{i}(\mathcal{T}_{x})_{y})

.

Recall that,
\operatorname{Mon}_{0}(E_{\bullet})=M/E_{\bullet}
which corresponds to the leaf space of
{\mathcal{F}}
.
When
i=0
,
\operatorname{Mon}^{\infty}_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{x})=p^{-1}(x)/{\mathcal{F}}|_{\mathcal{T}_{x}}
, and we have an identification
\Gamma(\operatorname{Mon}^{\infty}_{0}(\mathcal{T}_{x})=\operatorname{Diff}(p^{-1}(x)/{\mathcal{F}}|_{\mathcal{T}_{x}})
which is the bijections of the leaf space induced by diffeomorphisms. Hence, the image of
\partial_{1}
is the holonomy group
\operatorname{Hol}({\mathcal{F}})_{x}
.

Definition 24.35
.

Define the
n
-th holonomy
of
L
to be the image of the
n
-th monodromy morphism
\operatorname{Hol}_{n}({\mathcal{F}},L)=\partial(\operatorname{Mon}_{n+1}(TL))
.

Proposition 24.36
.

There is a natural simplicial structure on
\operatorname{Hol}_{n}({\mathcal{F}},L)
, which assembles to a Lie
\infty
-groupoid
\operatorname{Hol}_{\bullet}({\mathcal{F}},L)
. We call
\operatorname{Hol}_{\bullet}({\mathcal{F}},L)
the holonomy
\infty
-groupoid of
{\mathcal{F}}
at
L
.

Example 24.37

(embedded submanifold)
.

Let’s consider
L
to be a simply connected embedded submanifold of
M
. Consider
{\mathcal{F}}
to be a singular foliation generated by all the vector field tangent to
L
. Let
U
be a tubular neighborhood of
L
in
M
, and
NL
the normal bundle of
NL
. Let
f:{\mathcal{F}}|_{U}\to NL
be a foliated diffeomorphism which send
L
to the zero section of
L
. Then the Atiyah Lie algebroid
\operatorname{At}(NL)
of
NL
is a Lie algebroid of minimal rank of
{\mathcal{F}}
. Recall the
\operatorname{At}(NL)
consists of covariant differential operators on
\Gamma(NL)
.

Now let’s look at the long exact sequence of holonomy. Take some
x\in L
and
y\in p^{-1}(x)
. There are two cases:

(1)

First consider
y\not=0
. The transverse foliation consists of a fiber
V\simeq{\mathbb{R}}^{q}
of
NL
, where
q
is the codimension of
L
, with a regular leaf
V-\{0\}
and a singular leaf
\{0\}
. Hence, we have

\operatorname{Mon}_{n}(\mathcal{T}_{x},y)=\pi_{n}(V-\{0\},y)\simeq\pi_{n}(S^{q-1},y)

Therefore, the
i
-th monodromy morphism reduces to

\partial:\pi_{i}(L,x)\to\Gamma(\pi_{n}(S^{q-1}))

which corresponds to the exact sequence of the fibration

(V-\{0\})\to(NL-L)\to L.

(2)

Next, let’s look at
y=0
. Note that
\operatorname{At}(NL)
restricts to
\mathfrak{gl}(V)
on
V
, hence by classical Lie integration theory, we know that

\operatorname{Mon}_{n}(\mathcal{T}_{x},y)=\begin{cases}\widetilde{\operatorname{GL}(V)}\quad&n=1\\
\pi_{n}(\widetilde{\operatorname{GL}(V)})&n>1\end{cases}

25.
Higher foliations

25.1.
Tangent
\infty
-stack

Assigning a manifold
M
its tangent bundle
TM
gives a functor
T:\mathsf{Mfd}\to\mathsf{Mfd}
. Associated to
T
there is a natural projection
\pi:T\to\operatorname{Id}
given by
TM\to M
. Precompose
T
with the Yoneda embedding
\mathsf{y}:\mathsf{Mfd}^{\operatorname{op}}\to\mathsf{PSh_{\infty}}(\mathsf{Mfd})
gives a
\infty
-functor
T^{*}:\mathsf{PSh_{\infty}}(\mathsf{Mfd})\to\mathsf{PSh_{\infty}}(\mathsf{Mfd})
, i.e.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{\bullet}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{\bullet}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{\bullet}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{\bullet}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d}

Lemma 25.1
.

There exists an
\infty
-functor
T^{*}:\mathsf{PSh_{\infty}}(\mathsf{Mfd})\to\mathsf{PSh_{\infty}}(\mathsf{Mfd})
canonically associated to
T
.

Lemma 25.2
.

T^{*}
restricts to an
\infty
-functor
T^{*}:\mathsf{Sh}_{\infty}(\mathsf{Mfd})\to\mathsf{Sh}_{\infty}(\mathsf{Mfd})
.

Proof.

If follows from
T
preserves open covers and pullbacks of covers are covers.
∎

Recall that two
\infty
-functors
F:{\mathsf{C}}\to{\mathsf{D}}
and
G:{\mathsf{D}}\to{\mathsf{C}}
if there exist a unit
\infty
-transformation
\epsilon:\operatorname{Id}_{{\mathsf{D}}}\to F\circ G
such that the composition

\operatorname{Hom}_{{\mathsf{C}}}(F(x),y)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hom_{{\mathsf{C}}}(G,G)}}{{\longrightarrow}}\operatorname{Hom}_{{\mathsf{D}}}(G\circ F(x),G(y))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hom_{{\mathsf{C}}}(G\epsilon,\operatorname{Id})}}{{\longrightarrow}}\operatorname{Hom}_{{\mathsf{D}}}(x,G(y))

is an equivalence of Kan complex.

Proposition 25.3
.

T^{*}:\mathsf{Sh}_{\infty}(\mathsf{Mfd})\to\mathsf{Sh}_{\infty}(\mathsf{Mfd})
admits a left
\infty
-adjoint
T:\mathsf{Sh}_{\infty}(\mathsf{Mfd})\to\mathsf{Sh}_{\infty}(\mathsf{Mfd})
.

Proof.

The
\infty
-category of Kan complexes
\mathsf{Grpd_{\infty}}
is homotopically complete, hence we can form the left
\infty
-adjoint
T^{pre}:\mathsf{PSh_{\infty}}(\mathsf{Mfd})\to\mathsf{PSh_{\infty}}(\mathsf{Mfd})
on
\infty
-presheaves. The inclusion
i:\mathsf{Sh}_{\infty}(\mathsf{Mfd})\to\mathsf{PSh_{\infty}}(\mathsf{Mfd})
admits the left adjoint of the stackification functor
p:\mathsf{Sh}_{\infty}(\mathsf{Mfd})\to\mathsf{Sh}_{\infty}(\mathsf{Mfd})
. Now we define
T=p\circ T^{pre}\circ i
.
∎

We call
T
the
tangent
\infty
-stack functor
. We also want to extend
\pi:T\to\operatorname{Id}_{\mathsf{Mfd}}
to
\infty
-stacks. Precomposing Yoneda embedding with
\pi
determines a natural transformation
\pi^{*}:\operatorname{Id}_{\mathsf{PSh_{\infty}}(\mathsf{Mfd})}\to T^{*}
. Take the left adjoint of
\pi^{*}
to be
\pi^{pre}
. Now define
\pi:T\to\operatorname{Id}_{\mathsf{Sh}_{\infty}(\mathsf{Mfd})}
to be

\operatorname{Hom}_{[\mathsf{Sh}_{\infty}(\mathsf{Mfd}),\mathsf{Sh}_{\infty}(\mathsf{Mfd})]}(p,p)\circ\pi^{pre}\circ\operatorname{Hom}_{[\mathsf{PSh_{\infty}}(\mathsf{Mfd}),\mathsf{PSh_{\infty}}(\mathsf{Mfd})]}(i,i)

.

Proposition 25.4
.

There exist an
\infty
-natural equivalence
\epsilon:T\circ\mathsf{y}\Rightarrow\mathsf{y}\circ T
.

Proof.

Follows from the construction.

∎

We have a fully faithfully embedding
|-|:\mathsf{Lie_{\infty}Grpd}\to\mathsf{Sh}_{\infty}(\mathsf{Mfd})
from Lie
\infty
-groupoids to
\infty
-stacks. We can form the tangent
\infty
-groupoid functor
T^{gpd}:\mathsf{Lie_{\infty}Grpd}\to\mathsf{Lie_{\infty}Grpd}
by taking degreewise tangent bundle along with differentials.

Definition 25.5
.

Let
X_{\bullet}
be a Lie
\infty
-groupoid. Define the tangent groupoid functor
T^{gpd}
to be the unique functor which sends
X_{\bullet}
to
TX_{\bullet}
, where
TX_{i}=T(X_{i})
and all structure maps are getting by taking differentials.

Proposition 25.6
.

Let
X_{\bullet},Y_{\bullet}\in\mathsf{Lie_{\infty}Grpd}
, and
|X_{\bullet}|,|XY_{\bullet}|
denote their associated stacks.
We have a commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{\bullet}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{\bullet}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{\bullet}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{\bullet}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d}

Proof.

∎

25.2.
\infty
-vector fields on
\infty
-stack

Definition 25.7
.

Let
\mathfrak{X}\in\mathsf{Sh}_{\infty}(\mathsf{Mfd})
, we define an (
\infty
-)
vector field
on
X
to be a pair (X,
\epsilon_{X}
), where
X
is a morphism
X:\mathfrak{X}\to T\mathfrak{X}
, and
\epsilon_{X}:\pi_{\mathfrak{X}}\circ X\Rightarrow\operatorname{Id}_{\mathfrak{X}}
is an equivalence.

Similarly, we can define vector fields on a Lie
\infty
-groupoid.

Definition 25.8
.

Let
G_{\bullet}\in\mathsf{Lie_{\infty}Grpd}
. An (
\infty
-)vector field on
G_{\bullet}
is a morphism
X:G_{\bullet}\to TG_{\bullet}
such that
\pi_{G_{\bullet}}\circ X=\operatorname{Id}_{{G_{\bullet}}}
.

Proposition 25.9
.

Given a Lie
\infty
-groupoid
G_{\bullet}
, and denote
G
the associated
\infty
-stack, then we have an equivalence of category
\mathsf{Vect}(G_{\bullet})\simeq\mathsf{Vect}(G)

25.3.
Higher foliations

25.3.1.
Foliations on stacks

Let
M
be a smooth manifold. A regular foliation is defined as an involutive sub-bundle of the tangent bundle
TM
. This easily generalized to Lie
\infty
-groupoids.

Definition 25.10
.

Let
X_{\bullet}
be a Lie
\infty
-groupoid. We define a
\infty
-foliation

{\mathcal{F}}
on
X_{\bullet}
to be a sub-Lie
\infty
-groupoid
A_{\bullet}
of the tangent
\infty
-groupoid
TX_{\bullet}
, where at each level,
A_{i}\subset TX_{i}
is an involutive sub-bundle of
TX_{i}
.

Recall that a singular foliation
{\mathcal{F}}
on a smooth manifold
M
is defined as a subsheaf of the tangent
T_{M}
which is involutive and locally finitely generated as a
{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}(X)
-module. Replace the
\infty
-foliation degreewise by a singular foliation, we get a higher notion of singular foliation.

Definition 25.11
.

Let
X_{\bullet}
be a Lie
\infty
-groupoid. We define a singular (
\infty
-)foliation
{\mathcal{F}}
on
X_{\bullet}
to be a simplicial set
{\mathcal{F}}_{\bullet}
, where at each level,
{\mathcal{F}}_{i}
is a subsheaf of the tangent
T_{M}
which is involutive and locally finitely generated as a
{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}(X)
-module.

Here the simplicial set
{\mathcal{F}}
is actually a simplicial sheaf of
{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}(X)
-modules, by applying the forgetful functor from sheaf of
{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}(X)
-modules to sheaf of sets, we can regard
{\mathcal{F}}
as an element of
s\mathsf{Sh}(\mathsf{Mfd})
.

Similarly, we can consider foliations on
\infty
-stacks.

25.3.2.
Foliation on 1-stack

Part VII
Higher Riemann-Hilbert correspondence for foliations

In this chapter, we study more in depth about the foliation dga (algebroid). Recall that, for a smooth manifold, we have the
de Rham theorem
: given a manifold
M
, the singular cohomology groups
H^{\bullet}(M,{\mathbb{R}})
and the de Rham cohomology groupoids
H^{\bullet}_{\mathsf{dR}}(M,{\mathbb{R}})
are isomorphic, i.e.

H^{\bullet}(M,{\mathbb{R}})\simeq H^{\bullet}_{\mathsf{dR}}(M,{\mathbb{R}})

In other words, the singular cochain dga
C^{\bullet}(M,{\mathbb{R}})
and de Rham dga
\mathcal{A}^{\bullet}(M,{\mathbb{R}})
are
quasi-isomorphic
.

However, this quasi-isomorphism is not an dga quasi-isomorphism, since the product structure is not preserved. However, Guggenheim
[
Gug77
]
proved that this quasi-isomorphism lifts to an
A_{\infty}
-quasi-isomorphism, where the product structure is preserved up to a higher homotopy coherence. We first study foliated dga’s and prove an
A_{\infty}
de Rham theorem for foliations.

On the other hand, the similar method can be applied to modules over foliated dga’s (algebroids). Recall that the classical
Riemann-Hilbert correspondence
(for manifolds) established the following equivalences:

(1)

Local systems over
M
.

(2)

Vector bundles with flat connections over
M
.

(3)

Representations of the fundamental group of
M
.

Following Chen’s iterated integrals
[
Che77
]
[
Gug77
]
and Igusa’s integration of superconnections
[
Igu09
]
, Block-Smith
[
BS14
]
proves a higher Riemann-Hilbert correspondence for compact manifolds: the dg category of cohesive modules over the de Rham dga is
A_{\infty}
-quasi-equivalent to the dg-category of
\infty
-local systems over
M
:

\mathsf{Mod}^{{\operatorname{coh}}}_{\mathcal{A}}\simeq_{A_{\infty}}\mathsf{Loc}^{{\operatorname{dg}}}_{\operatorname{ch}_{k}}(M)

where the left-hand side is equivalent to the dg category of
\infty
-representations of the tangent Lie algebroid
TM
, and the right-hand side is equivalent to the dg category of the
\infty
-representations of the fundamental
\infty
-groupoid
\Pi^{\infty}(M)
. Notice that
\Pi^{\infty}(M)
is equivalent to the integration of
TM
by the Lie integration functor we mentioned before. Thus, we have the following homotopy-commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{T_{M}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\Pi^{\infty}(M)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{Mod}^{{\operatorname{coh}}}_{\mathcal{A}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{Loc}^{{\operatorname{dg}}}_{\operatorname{ch}_{k}}(M)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Rep}^{\infty}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\int}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Rep}^{\infty}}

Hence we can really understand the Riemann-Hilbert Correspondence as an equivalence between
\infty
-representations of
L_{\infty}
-algebroids and
\infty
-representations of the integration of
L_{\infty}
-algebroids, i.e. Lie
\infty
-groupoids.
We apply this idea to the case of foliations and prove a higher Riemann-Hilbert correspondence for foliation, and construct the integration functor from the
\infty
-representations of
L_{\infty}
-algebroids and
\infty
-representations Lie
\infty
-groupoids.

26.
Algebras and modules of foliations

26.1.
\mathcal{D}
-module and foliations

Let
X
be a smooth manifold and
{\mathcal{F}}
a regular foliation on
X
. We consider a
{\mathcal{D}}_{X}
-module associated
{\mathcal{D}}_{{\mathcal{F}}}={\mathcal{D}}_{X}/{\mathcal{D}}_{X}\cdot{\mathcal{F}}
, which stands for linear differential operator normal to
{\mathcal{F}}
.

First, consider
{\mathcal{F}}=T_{X}
, then
{\mathcal{D}}_{{\mathcal{F}}}/{\mathcal{D}}_{X}\cdot T_{X}\simeq{\mathcal{O}}_{X}
.

Next, consider a general regular foliation
{\mathcal{F}}
. Let
{A}^{\bullet}={A}^{\bullet}(M,\widehat{\operatorname{Sym}}({\mathcal{F}}^{\perp}))=\Omega^{\bullet}(X)\otimes\widehat{\operatorname{Sym}}({\mathcal{F}}^{\perp})
. Let
q
be the codimension of
{\mathcal{F}}
. Let
\{x_{i}\}_{i},1\leq i\leq n
coordinates for
U_{i}
and
\hat{x}_{i}=dx_{i},1\leq i\leq q
be a basis of
{\mathcal{F}}^{\perp}
.

Lemma 26.1
.

\nabla=d-\sum_{i=1}^{q}dx_{i}\wedge\frac{\partial}{\partial\hat{x}_{i}}
is a flat connection on
{A}^{\bullet}={A}^{\bullet}(M,\widehat{\operatorname{Sym}}({\mathcal{F}}^{\perp}))
.

Proof.

First, we show
\nabla^{2}=0
. Let
\sum_{\alpha}f_{\alpha}\hat{x}^{\alpha}\in\widehat{\operatorname{Sym}}({\mathcal{F}}^{\perp})
, where
\alpha
’s are multi-indices, and
f_{\alpha}\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}(M)

\displaystyle\nabla\sum_{\alpha}f_{\alpha}\hat{x}^{\alpha})=

\displaystyle(d-\sum_{j=1}^{q}dx_{j}\wedge\frac{\partial}{\partial\hat{x}_{j}})(\sum_{\alpha}f_{\alpha}\hat{x}^{\alpha})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha}\bigg(\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{n}dx_{i}\wedge\frac{\partial f_{\alpha}}{\partial x_{i}}\hat{x}^{\alpha}\Big)-\sum_{i=1}^{q}\Big(dx_{i}\wedge f_{\alpha}\frac{\partial\hat{x}^{\alpha}}{\partial\hat{x}_{i}}\Big)\bigg)

Let’s look at these two terms in the summand independently. Note that
d
on the first term is just 0, hence

\displaystyle\nabla\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{n}dx_{i}\wedge\frac{\partial f_{\alpha}}{\partial x_{i}}\hat{x}^{\alpha}\Big)=

\displaystyle-\sum_{j=1}^{q}\Big(dx_{j}\wedge\frac{\partial}{\partial\hat{x_{j}}}\Big)\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{n}dx_{i}\wedge\frac{\partial f_{\alpha}}{\partial x_{i}}\hat{x}^{\alpha}\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{q}dx_{i}\wedge dx_{j}\wedge\frac{\partial f_{\alpha}}{\partial x_{i}}\frac{\partial\hat{x}^{\alpha}}{\partial\hat{x}_{j}}

Next, for the second term,

\displaystyle\nabla\Big(-\sum_{j=1}^{q}dx_{i}\wedge f_{\alpha}\frac{\partial\hat{x}^{\alpha}}{\partial\hat{x}_{i}}\Big)=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{q}dx_{i}\wedge\Big(\sum_{k=1}^{n}dx_{k}\wedge\frac{\partial f_{\alpha}}{\partial x_{k}}\Big)\frac{\partial\hat{x}^{\alpha}}{\partial\hat{x}_{i}}-\sum_{i=1}^{q}dx_{i}\wedge\Big(\sum_{l=1}^{q}dx_{l}\wedge f_{\alpha}\frac{\partial}{\partial\hat{x_{l}}}\frac{\partial\hat{x}^{\alpha}}{\partial\hat{x}_{i}}\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{q}\sum_{k=1}^{n}dx_{i}\wedge dx_{k}\wedge\frac{\partial f_{\alpha}}{\partial x_{k}}\frac{\partial\hat{x}^{\alpha}}{\partial\hat{x}_{i}}-\sum_{i=1}^{q}\sum_{l=1}^{q}dx_{i}\wedge dx_{l}\wedge f_{\alpha}\frac{\partial}{\partial\hat{x_{l}}}\frac{\partial\hat{x}^{\alpha}}{\partial\hat{x}_{i}}

The first term cancels the term in the precious equation and the second is clearly vanished. Hence, we see
\nabla
is flat.

Next, we want to show
\nabla
is well-defined. Let
U
and
V
be two foliated neighborhoods with nonempty intersection. Let
\{x_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
and
\{y_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
be coordinates on
U
and
V
respectively. consider
\phi:U_{i}\to U_{j}
be a transition function between foliated neighborhoods. Note that
\phi:U\simeq{\mathbb{R}}^{q}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{n-q}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{q}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{n-q}\simeq V
has the following form

(1)

\phi_{i}(x)=\phi_{i}(x_{1},\cdots,x_{q})
for
1\leq i\leq q
.

(2)

\phi_{i}(x)=\phi_{i}(x_{1},\cdots,x_{n})
for
q+1\leq i\leq n
.

By our assumption,
\phi_{i}(x)=y_{i}
.

\displaystyle d-\sum_{i=1}^{q}dy_{i}\wedge\frac{\partial}{\partial\hat{y}_{i}}=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}dy_{i}\wedge\frac{\partial}{\partial y_{i}}-\sum_{i=1}^{q}dy_{i}\wedge\frac{\partial}{\partial\hat{y}_{i}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big(\sum_{j=1}^{n}dx_{j}\wedge\frac{\partial\phi_{i}}{\partial x_{j}}\Big)\wedge\frac{\partial}{\partial y_{i}}-\sum_{i=1}^{q}\Big(\sum_{l=1}^{n}dx_{j}\wedge\frac{\partial\phi_{i}}{\partial x_{j}}\Big)\wedge\Big(\sum_{k=1}^{q}\frac{\partial\hat{x}_{k}}{\partial\hat{y}_{i}}\wedge\frac{\partial}{\partial\hat{x}_{k}}\Big)

Note that
\frac{\partial\phi_{i}}{\partial x_{j}}=0
for
1\leq i\leq q
and
q+1\leq j\leq n
. Hence, the above equation becomes

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Big(\sum_{j=1}^{q}dx_{j}\wedge\frac{\partial\phi_{i}}{\partial x_{j}}\Big)\wedge\frac{\partial}{\partial y_{i}}-\sum_{i=1}^{q}\Big(\sum_{l=1}^{q}dx_{l}\wedge\frac{\partial\phi_{i}}{\partial x_{l}}\Big)\wedge\Big(\sum_{k=1}^{q}\frac{\partial\hat{x}_{k}}{\partial\hat{y}_{i}}\wedge\frac{\partial}{\partial\hat{x}_{k}}\Big)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{q}dx_{j}\wedge\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\partial\phi_{i}}{\partial x_{j}}\wedge\frac{\partial}{\partial y_{i}}\Big)-\sum_{l=1}^{q}\sum_{k=1}^{q}dx_{l}\wedge\frac{\partial}{\partial\hat{x}_{k}}\wedge\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{q}\frac{\partial\phi_{i}}{\partial x_{l}}\wedge\frac{\partial\hat{x}_{k}}{\partial\hat{y}_{i}}\Big)

Note that
{\partial\hat{x}_{k}}/{\partial\hat{y}_{i}}={\partial(dx_{k})}/{\partial(dy_{i})}=\partial x_{k}/\partial y_{i}
, and then
\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{q}\frac{\partial\phi_{i}}{\partial x_{l}}\wedge\frac{\partial\hat{x}_{k}}{\partial\hat{y}_{i}}\Big)
equals a diagonal matrix which restricts to
I_{q}
on the top left
q\times q
submatrix and all the other entries are 0. Hence, we get

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{q}dx_{j}\wedge\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\partial\phi_{i}}{\partial x_{j}}\wedge\frac{\partial}{\partial y_{i}}\Big)-\sum_{l=1}^{q}\sum_{k=1}^{q}\delta_{lk}dx_{l}\wedge\frac{\partial}{\partial\hat{x}_{k}}=\sum_{j=1}^{q}dx_{j}\wedge\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{j}}-\sum_{m=1}^{q}dx_{m}\wedge\frac{\partial}{\partial\hat{x}_{m}}

∎

Next, let us look at the cohomology of the dga
{A}^{\bullet}={A}^{\bullet}(M,\widehat{\operatorname{Sym}}({\mathcal{F}}^{\perp}))

Lemma 26.2
.

The 0-th cohomology of
{A}^{\bullet}
equal
{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}
functions on
M
which is constant on leaves, i.e.

H^{0}({A}^{\bullet}(M,\widehat{\operatorname{Sym}}({\mathcal{F}}^{\perp}))\simeq{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{F}}}

Proof.

Let
\sum_{\alpha}f_{\alpha}\hat{x}^{\alpha}\in\widehat{\operatorname{Sym}}({\mathcal{F}}^{\perp})
, where
\alpha
’s are multi-indices and
f_{\alpha}\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}(M)
, then from the previous proof

\displaystyle\nabla(\sum_{\alpha}f_{\alpha}\hat{x}^{\alpha})=

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha}\bigg(\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{n}dx_{i}\wedge\frac{\partial f_{\alpha}}{\partial x_{i}}\hat{x}^{\alpha}\Big)-\sum_{i=1}^{q}\Big(dx_{i}\wedge f_{\alpha}\frac{\partial\hat{x}^{\alpha}}{\partial\hat{x}_{i}}\Big)\bigg)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha}\bigg(\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{n}dx_{i}\wedge\frac{\partial f_{\alpha}}{\partial x_{i}}\hat{x}^{\alpha}\Big)-\sum_{i=1}^{q}\Big(dx_{i}\wedge f_{\alpha}\alpha_{i}\hat{x}^{\alpha-1_{i}}\bigg)

Here
\alpha=(\alpha_{1},\cdots,\alpha_{i},\cdots,\alpha_{n})
, and
1_{i}
denotes the multi-index with 1 at the
i
-th entry and 0’s elsewhere. Now we can group the coefficients of
dx_{i}\wedge\hat{x}^{\alpha}
, so we get

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{q}dx_{i}\wedge\sum_{\alpha}\Big(\frac{\partial f_{\alpha}}{\partial x_{i}}-(\alpha_{i}+1)f_{\alpha+1_{i}}\hat{x}^{\alpha}\Big)-\sum_{i=q+1}^{n}dx_{i}\wedge\Big(\sum_{\alpha}\frac{\partial f_{\alpha}}{\partial x_{i}}\hat{x}^{\alpha}\Big)

Hence
\ker\nabla
consists of sections
\sum_{\alpha}f_{\alpha}\hat{x}^{\alpha}
of
\widehat{\operatorname{Sym}}({\mathcal{F}}^{\perp})
where
f_{\alpha}
satisfies
\frac{\partial f_{\alpha}}{\partial x_{i}}-(\alpha_{i}+1)f_{\alpha+1_{i}}=0
for
1\leq i\leq q
and
\frac{\partial f_{\alpha}}{\partial x_{i}}=0
for
q+1\leq i\leq n
. The first condition implies
\sum_{\alpha}f_{\alpha}\hat{x}^{\alpha}
is holonomic and the second condition implies that
f_{\alpha}
’s are constant along leaves.

∎

Corollary 26.3
.

The cohomology of
{A}^{\bullet}
is isomorphic to
\Omega^{\bullet}_{M}\otimes_{{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}(M)}{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{F}}}
.

26.2.
Sheaf of constant functions along leaves

We denote the sheaf of (smooth) functions on
M
which are constant along leaves of
{\mathcal{F}}
by
\underline{{\mathbb{R}}}_{{\mathcal{F}}}
. Regard
(M,\underline{{\mathbb{R}}}_{{\mathcal{F}}})
as a ringed space, then the sheaf of
{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}
-functions
{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}_{M}
on
M
is a sheave of
\underline{{\mathbb{R}}}_{{\mathcal{F}}}
-module. We have the following conjecture:

Conjecture

Given a foliation
(M,{\mathcal{F}})
,
{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}_{M}
is flat over
\underline{{\mathbb{R}}}_{{\mathcal{F}}}
.

This is a proposition encoding differential geometric properties into a simple algebraic form, which will be useful in proving many results later. We won’t prove it in this paper, and we shall use other method (
{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}
-rings or topological algebras) to get rid of our issues. In this chapter, we will prove a partial result on this conjecture.

The problem is local. It suffices to show
{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}_{M,x}
is flat over
\underline{{\mathbb{R}}}_{{\mathcal{F}},x}
for all
x\in M
. Picking a foliation chart and a foliated neighborhood
U\simeq{\mathbb{R}}^{q}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{n-q}
, then
{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}_{M}(U)\simeq{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})
and
\underline{{\mathbb{R}}}_{{\mathcal{F}}}\simeq{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{n-q})
. Hence, it suffices to show the following lemma

Lemma 26.4
.

{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})
is a flat
{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{n-q})
module for
q\geq 0
.

The module structure is induced by the projection
p:{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{n-q}
. Given
a_{1},\cdots,a_{k}\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{n-q})
and
b_{1},\cdots,b_{k}\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})
such that
\sum_{i}a_{i}b_{i}=0
, we want to show that there exist functions
G_{1},\cdots,G_{r}\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}
and
c_{ij}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n-q}
, such that
\sum_{j=1}c_{ij}G_{j}
for all
i
and
\sum_{i}a_{i}c_{ij}=0
for all
j
.

Let’s first consider the simple case
n=2,q=1
. We start by the following lemma, which is a special case of flatness when
k=1
.

Lemma 26.5
.

Let
h\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}})
to be strictly positive for
x<0
and 0 for
x\geq 0
, and
g\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})
. Let
{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})
as a
{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}})
-module induced by the projection
{\mathbb{R}}^{2}\to{\mathbb{R}}
. Suppose
hg=0
, then
g(x,y)=c(x)G(x,y)
, where
c(x)\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}})
vanishes on
x\leq 0
and
G(x,y)\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})
.

Proof.

Consider two sets
I=\{f\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}})|\text{$f=0$ when $x\leq 0$ and $f>0$ when $x>0$}\}
,
J=\{f\in\operatorname{Map}({\mathbb{R}}^{+},{\mathbb{R}})|\text{$f(x)/x^{n}\to 0$ as $x\to 0$ for all $n>0$}\}
.

Lemma 26.6
.

For any
f\in J
, there exists a
g\in I
such that
f/g\to 0
as
x\to 0
.

Proof.

Consider a bump function
r\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}})
such that
r=1
for
x\leq 0
and
r=0
for
x\geq 1
. Let
\{a_{k}\}
be a monotonically decreasing sequence such that
\sum_{i}a_{k}<\infty
and
a_{k}>0
for all
k
. Define
\theta(x)=\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}r(x/a_{i})
. Let

(26.1)

g(x)=\begin{cases}x^{\theta(x)}\quad&x>0\\
0&x\leq 0\end{cases}.

We claim that
g(x)
satisfied the requirement in lemma. Outside any open neighborhood of
0
, there will be only finitely many non-zero summands in
\theta
, hence
g(x)
is smooth and bounded outside any open neighborhood of
0
. Clearly
g(x)/x^{n}\to 0
as
x\to 0
. We just need to check all derivative of
g
. Let
\theta_{n}(x)=\sum_{i=1}^{n}a_{k}
and
g_{n}=x^{\theta_{n}}
, we have

\frac{d^{l}}{dx^{l}}g_{n}=\frac{d^{l}}{dx^{l}}x^{\theta_{n}}=\theta_{n}(x)\cdots(\theta_{n}(x)-l+1)x^{\theta_{n}(x)-l}

for
x>0
. For each
n
, there exists an
\epsilon_{n}
such that for
0<x<\epsilon_{n}
,
\frac{d^{l}}{dx^{l}}x^{\theta_{n+1}}<\frac{d^{l}}{dx^{l}}x^{\theta_{n}}
. For each
l
, if we pick
n
large enough, e.g.
l>n
, then
\frac{d^{l}}{dx^{l}}x^{\theta_{n}}=0
. Let
\epsilon>0
be arbitrary, we want to show that there exists
x_{0}>0
and
N\in{\mathbb{N}}
such that for all
n>N,0<x<x_{0}
,
|\frac{d^{l}}{dx^{l}}x^{\theta_{n}(x)}-\frac{d^{l}}{dx^{l}}x^{\theta(x)}|<\epsilon
, that is
x^{\theta_{n}}\to x^{\theta}
uniformly on
[0,x_{0}]
. Consider
n>l
to be sufficiently large, then there exist
x_{1}>0
such that
\frac{d^{l}}{dx^{l}}x^{\theta_{n}}<\epsilon
for
0\leq x\leq x_{1}
. Now

\displaystyle\frac{d^{l}}{dx^{l}}x^{\theta_{n+1}}

\displaystyle=\theta_{n+1}(x)\cdots(\theta_{n+1}(x)-l+1)x^{\theta_{n+1}(x)-l}

\displaystyle=\frac{\theta_{n+1}(x)\cdots(\theta_{n+1}(x)-l+1)}{\theta_{n}(x)\cdots(\theta_{n}(x)-l+1)}\theta_{n}(x)\cdots(\theta_{n}(x)-l+1)x^{\theta_{n}(x)-l}x^{r(x/a_{n+1})}

\displaystyle\leq x^{\theta_{n+1}(x)-l}\Bigg(\frac{\theta_{n+1}(x)-l+1}{\theta_{n}(x)-l+1}\Bigg)^{l}\frac{d^{l}}{dx^{l}}x^{\theta_{n}}

Note that
\frac{\theta_{n+1}(x)-l+1}{\theta_{n}(x)-l+1}
is monotonically decreasing as
n
increases and as
x
decreases. Let
\delta>0
such that
(\frac{\theta_{n+1}(x)-l+1}{\theta_{n}(x)-l+1})^{l}<1+\delta
for all
0\leq 0\leq x_{1}
, then we can find an
x_{2}<x_{1}
such that
x^{r(x/a_{n+1})}<1/(1+\delta)
. Therefore,
\frac{d^{l}}{dx^{l}}x^{\theta_{n+1}}<\epsilon
on
[0,x_{2}]
. By induction, we get
\frac{d^{l}}{dx^{l}}x^{\theta_{k}}<\epsilon
for all
k\geq n
and
0\leq x\leq x_{2}
.

Now picking a sequence
\{x_{m}\}
such that all
x_{m}\leq x_{2}
and
x_{m}\to 0
monotonically, then
\lim_{m\to 0}g(x_{m})=0
by continuity, and

\lim_{n\to\infty}\lim_{m\to 0}\frac{d^{l}}{dx^{l}}g_{n}(x_{m})=\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{d^{l}}{dx^{l}}g_{n}(0)=0

,
then by the uniform convergence, we can change the order of limits and get

\lim_{m\to 0}\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{d^{l}}{dx^{l}}g_{n}(x_{m})=\lim_{m\to 0}\frac{d^{l}}{dx^{l}}g(x_{m})=0

Now we have shown that
g\in J
. Since
f=0
for all
x\leq 0
, all derivatives of
f
must vanish at infinite order at
0
, hence for each
n>0
, there exists a decreasing sequence
\{\epsilon_{n}\}
and
\epsilon_{k}<1
for all
k
such that
|f|\leq x^{n}
for all
x\in[0,\epsilon_{n}]
. Now we just need to pick
a_{k}<\epsilon_{n+1}
for all
k\geq n
which ensure that
g(x)/x^{n-1}>1
for
x\in(\epsilon_{n+1},\epsilon_{n})
. Hence,
|f(x)/g(x)|\leq x
on
(\epsilon_{n+1},\epsilon_{n})
for all
n
, which implies
f/g\to 0
as
x\to 0
.

∎

Corollary 26.7
.

Given a sequence
\{f_{i}\}
in
J
, there exists a
g\in I
such that
f_{k}/g\to 0
as
x\to 0
for all
k
.

Corollary 26.8
.

Given a sequence
\{f_{i}\}
in
J
, there exists a
g\in I
such that
f_{k}/g^{n}\to 0
as
x\to 0
for all
k,n\in{\mathbb{N}}
.

Now consider
f_{ijk}(x)=\sup_{|y|\leq k}\big(\frac{d^{i+j}}{dx^{i}dy^{j}}g(x,y)\big)
. Then by previous lemma, we can find an
a(x)
such that
f_{ijk}/a^{n}\to 0
as
x\to 0^{+}
for all
i,j,k,n
. Now, take

G(x,y)=\begin{cases}g(x,y)/a(x)\quad&x>0\\
0&x\leq 0\end{cases}

It suffices to verify
G^{(n)}(x,y)\to 0
as
x\to 0^{+}
. Expand by quotient rules, we have

\bigg(\frac{g(x,y)}{a(x)}\bigg)^{(n)}=\frac{1}{a}\left(g^{(n)}-\sum_{j=1}^{n}\binom{n}{j}\bigg(\frac{g}{a}\bigg)^{(n-1-j)}a^{(j)}\right).

which goes to
0
by induction. Hence,
g=aG
is the desired factorization.

∎

Proposition 26.9
.

Let
p:{\mathbb{R}}^{2}\to{\mathbb{R}}
be a submersion. Let
h\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}})
and
g\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}{({\mathbb{R}}^{2})}
such that
hg=0
, then there exist an
a\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}})
and
G\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})
such that
h=aG
and
ah=0
.

Proof.

It suffices to consider the case
p
is the projection. Without loss of generality, we restrict the domain on an open neighborhood
U
of the origin on
{\mathbb{R}}
. Let
V=h^{-1}(0)\subset U
. If
V
is nowhere dense, then
g
must vanish on
p^{-1}(U\setminus V)\simeq(U\setminus V)\times{\mathbb{R}}
, which have to vanish on
\overline{U\setminus V}\times{\mathbb{R}}=\overline{U}\times{\mathbb{R}}
. In this case,
g
vanishes on
\overline{U}\times{\mathbb{R}}
, then we just take
a
be
a(x)=0
and
G
arbitrary. Now suppose
x_{0}\in U
is contained in a closed interval
V^{\prime}
. Let
U^{\prime}\supset V^{\prime}
be a open neighborhood of
V^{\prime}
such that
g
vanishes on
\overline{U^{\prime}\setminus V^{\prime}}
. Note that
h
can still vanish on a nowhere dense set on
U^{\prime}\setminus V^{\prime}
. Then
g
vanishes at infinite order at
\partial V^{\prime}
. By previous lemma, there exist an
a\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}})
vanishes on
x\in\overline{U^{\prime}\setminus V^{\prime}}
and
g=aG
for some
G\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})
.
∎

Lemma 26.10
.

Let
h_{1},\cdots,h_{k}\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}})
to be strictly positive for
x<0
and 0 for
x\geq 0
, and
g_{1},\cdots,g_{k}\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})
. Let
{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})
as a
{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}})
-module induced by the projection
{\mathbb{R}}^{2}\to{\mathbb{R}}
. Suppose
\sum_{i}h_{i}g_{i}=0
, then
g_{i}(x,y)=c_{ij}(x)G_{j}(x,y)
, where
c_{ij}(x)\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}})
vanishes on
x\leq 0
and
G_{j}(x,y)\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})
.

27.
A_{\infty}
de Rham theorem for foliations

27.1.
de Rham theorem for foliations

Theorem 27.1

(de Rham theorem for foliations)
.

Given a foliation
(M,{\mathcal{F}})
, there exists a isomorphism

(27.1)

H^{\bullet}(M,\mathop{\bigwedge\nolimits^{\!\bullet}}{\mathcal{F}}^{\vee})\simeq H^{\bullet}(M,C^{\bullet}({\mathcal{F}}))

Consider the codimension
q
product foliation
{\mathbb{R}}^{n-q}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{q}
on
{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
, we can build two new product foliations
{\mathbb{R}}^{n-q+1}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{q}
and
{\mathbb{R}}^{n-q}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{q+1}
out of it. Let
(x_{1},\cdots,x_{n-q},x_{n-q+1},\cdots,x_{n})
be the canonical coordinates on
{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
, then
{\mathcal{F}}=\{\partial_{x_{1}},\cdots,\partial_{{n-q}}\}
. Therefore we have
\mathop{\bigwedge\nolimits^{\!\bullet}}{\mathcal{F}}^{\vee}\simeq(\mathop{\bigwedge\nolimits^{\!\bullet}}T^{\vee}{\mathbb{R}}^{n-q})\times{\mathbb{R}}^{q}
, which yields
H^{\bullet}({\mathbb{R}}^{n},{\mathcal{F}})\simeq\Omega^{\bullet}({\mathbb{R}}^{n-q})
. Therefore,
H^{\bullet}({\mathbb{R}}^{n+1},{\mathbb{R}}^{n-q}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{q+1})\simeq H^{\bullet}({\mathbb{R}}^{n+1},{\mathbb{R}}^{n-q+1}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{q})
. On the other hand, we also have
H^{\bullet}({\mathbb{R}}^{n+1},{\mathbb{R}}^{n-q+1}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{q})\simeq H^{\bullet}({\mathbb{R}}^{n},{\mathbb{R}}^{n-q}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{q})
by Poincare lemma for
{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
.

Lemma 27.2

(Poincare lemma for foliations)
.

Consider the codimension
q
product foliation
({\mathbb{R}}^{n},{\mathcal{F}})=({\mathbb{R}}^{n},{\mathbb{R}}^{n-q}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{q})
, then

(27.2)

H^{i}({\mathbb{R}}^{n},\mathop{\bigwedge\nolimits^{\!\bullet}}{\mathcal{F}}))\simeq\begin{cases}{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{q})&\quad i=0\\
0&i\not=0\end{cases}

.

Proof.

By previous observation,
H^{i}({\mathbb{R}}^{n},\mathop{\bigwedge\nolimits^{\!\bullet}}{\mathcal{F}}))\simeq H^{i}(\Omega^{\bullet}({\mathbb{R}}^{n-q}))
, then the result follows from Poincare lemma for
{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
.
∎

Definition 27.3
.

We define the (smooth)
{\mathcal{F}}
-foliated singular
n
-chain
C_{n}({\mathcal{F}},G)
of a foliation
(M,{\mathcal{F}})
to be the free Abelian group generated by (smooth) foliated
n
-simplices
\sigma:\Delta^{n}\to{\mathcal{F}}
with coefficient in some Abelian group
G
. Define the differential
d_{n}:C_{n}({\mathcal{F}},G)\to C_{n-1}({\mathcal{F}},G)
by
d_{n}=\sum_{i=0}^{n}(-1)^{i}\delta_{i}
, where
\delta_{i}
is the
i
-th face map. We call
\big(C_{*}({\mathcal{F}},G),d\big)
the foliated singular chain complex.

Definition 27.4
.

We define the (smooth) foliated singular cochains
C^{\bullet}({\mathcal{F}})
to be
{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}
function on the monodromy
\infty
-groupoid
\operatorname{Mon}_{\infty}{\mathcal{F}}
associated to
{\mathcal{F}}
, i.e.
C^{n}({\mathcal{F}})={\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}(\operatorname{Mon}_{n}{\mathcal{F}},{\mathbb{R}})
.

Lemma 27.5
.

Consider the codimension
q
product foliation
({\mathbb{R}}^{n},{\mathcal{F}})=({\mathbb{R}}^{n},{\mathbb{R}}^{n-q}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{q})
, then

(27.3)

H^{i}({\mathbb{R}}^{n},C^{\bullet}{\mathcal{F}}))\simeq\begin{cases}{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{q})&\quad i=0\\
0&i\not=0\end{cases}

.

Proof.

Given a
k
-simplex
\sigma:\Delta^{n}\to{\mathcal{F}}
(
0\leq k\leq n-q-1
), define
K:C_{k}\to C_{k+1}
by
K\sigma(\sum_{j=0}^{k+1}t_{j}x_{j})=(1-t_{q+1})\sigma(\sum_{j=0}^{q}\frac{t_{j}}{1-t_{q+1}}x_{j})
which sends a foliated
k
-simplex to
k+1
simplex, then by standard calculation we have
\partial K-K\partial=(-1)^{q+1}
. Let
L
be the adjoint of
K
, then
(-1)^{k+1}(dL-Ld)=1
, which gives the result.
∎

On the other hand,
C^{n}{\mathcal{F}}
are soft since
C^{n}{\mathcal{F}}
are sheaves of
C^{0}{{\mathcal{F}}}\simeq{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}(M)
-modules.

Proof of de Rham theorem.

By Poincare lemma, we have
0\to{\mathbb{R}}_{{\mathcal{F}}}\to\Gamma(\mathop{\bigwedge\nolimits^{\!\bullet}}{\mathcal{F}})
which is a resolution of
\underline{{\mathbb{R}}}_{{\mathcal{F}}}
by fine sheaves. Note that
C^{n}({\mathcal{F}})
’s are sheaves of
C^{0}({\mathcal{F}})\simeq{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}(M)
-modules, which are soft since
{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}(M)
is. By lemma ,
C^{\bullet}({\mathcal{F}})
is a soft resolution of
\underline{{\mathbb{R}}}_{{\mathcal{F}}}
. Then integration over chains gives the desired quasi-isomorphism.
∎

Next, we are going to show the quasi-isomorphism between the dga of
{\mathcal{F}}
-foliated forms and the dga of smooth singular
{\mathcal{F}}
-cochains actually lifts to an
A_{\infty}
-quasi-isomorphism

\phi:(\mathop{\bigwedge\nolimits^{\!\bullet}}{\mathcal{F}}^{\vee},-d,\wedge)\to(C^{\bullet}({\mathcal{F}}),\delta,\cup)

The
\phi
is defined as a composition of two maps

\mathsf{B}\big((\mathop{\bigwedge\nolimits^{\!\bullet}}{\mathcal{F}}^{\vee})[1]\big)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle F}}{{\to}}\Omega^{\bullet}(P{\mathcal{F}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle G}}{{\to}}C^{\bullet}({\mathcal{F}})[1]

here
\mathsf{B}
is the bar construction. The first map is similar to Chen’s iterated integral map, and the second map is similar to Igusa’s construction in
[
Igu09
]
.

27.2.
Riemann-Hilbert correspondence

Theorem 27.6

(Riemann-Hilbert correspondance for foliation)
.

Let
(M,{\mathcal{F}})
be a manifold with foliation
{\mathcal{F}}
, then the following categories are equivalent

(1)

The category of foliated local systems
\mathsf{Loc}({\mathcal{F}})
.

(2)

The category of vector bundles with flat
{\mathcal{F}}
-connection.

(3)

The category of the representations of the fundamental groupoid.

Let
P{\mathcal{F}}
denote the Frechét manifold
P^{1}_{C^{\infty}}{\mathcal{F}}
which consists of smooth path along leaves. We parametrize geometric
k
-simplex
\Delta^{n}
by
t=(1\geq t_{1}\geq t_{2}\cdots\geq t_{k}\geq 0)
. First we have a map of evaluation on a path

\operatorname{ev}_{k}:P{\mathcal{F}}\times\Delta^{k}\to M^{k}:(\gamma,(t_{1},\cdots,t_{k}))\mapsto\big(\gamma(t_{1}),\cdots,\gamma(t_{k})\big)

The image of
\operatorname{ev}_{k}
fixing
\gamma
lies in a single leaf. Along with the natural inclusion
P{\mathcal{F}}\subset PM
, the following diagrams commutes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{P{\mathcal{F}}\times\Delta^{k}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\coprod_{x\in M}L_{x}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{PM\times\Delta^{k}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{M^{k}}}

Definition 27.7
.

We define
T_{{\mathcal{F}}}P{\mathcal{F}}
to be a vector bundle whose fiber at
\gamma\in P{\mathcal{F}}
is the vector space of all
{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}
-sections
I\to{\mathcal{F}}
along
\gamma
. We define the dual bundle
T^{\vee}_{{\mathcal{F}}}P{\mathcal{F}}
of
T_{{\mathcal{F}}}P{\mathcal{F}}
to be the vector bundle whose fiber at
\gamma
is the space of all bounded linear functionals, i.e.
T^{\vee}_{{\mathcal{F}},\gamma}P{\mathcal{F}}=\operatorname{Hom}(T_{{\mathcal{F}},\gamma}P{\mathcal{F}},{\mathbb{R}})
.

We denote the
{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}
-section of
T^{\vee}_{{\mathcal{F}},\gamma}P{\mathcal{F}}
by
\Omega_{{\mathcal{F}}}^{1}P{\mathcal{F}}
, and the exterior algebra of
\Omega_{{\mathcal{F}}}^{1}P{\mathcal{F}}
by
\Omega_{{\mathcal{F}}}^{\bullet}P{\mathcal{F}}
.

Lemma 27.8
.

Let
f\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}(PM)
and
\gamma_{0}\in M
, there exists a unique section
Df\in\Omega_{{\mathcal{F}}}^{1}P{\mathcal{F}}.

Proof.

Let
\eta\in T_{{\mathcal{F}},\gamma_{0}}P{\mathcal{F}}
. Take an one-parameter deformation
\gamma_{s}
of
\gamma_{0}
such that
\frac{\partial}{\partial s}\gamma_{s}=\eta
, then we can define
Df\big|_{\gamma_{0}}(\eta)=\frac{\partial}{\partial s}\big|_{s=0}(f\circ\gamma_{s})
. We want to show this gives a unique bounded linear functional on
T_{{\mathcal{F}},\gamma_{0}}P{\mathcal{F}}
. The boundedness and linearity is obvious.
∎

Corollary 27.9
.

For any smooth deformation
\gamma_{s}
of
\gamma_{0}
, we have the following chain role

Df\big|_{\gamma_{0}}\big(\frac{\partial}{\partial s}\bigg|_{s=0}\gamma_{s}\big)=\frac{\partial}{\partial s}\bigg|_{s=0}(f\circ\gamma_{s})

.

Next, we want to define higher differentials on
\Omega_{{\mathcal{F}}}^{\bullet}P{\mathcal{F}}
. A key observation is that
T_{{\mathcal{F}}}P{\mathcal{F}}
is involutive. Given two elements
\eta,\zeta\in\Gamma(T_{{\mathcal{F}},\gamma_{0}}P{\mathcal{F}})
, we can regard them as sections
I\to{\mathcal{F}}
along
\gamma_{0}
. Then, by involutivity of
{\mathcal{F}}
,
[\gamma,\eta]
is still a section
I\to{\mathcal{F}}
. Using this fact, we can define all higher differential on
\Omega_{{\mathcal{F}}}^{\bullet}P{\mathcal{F}}
simply by Chevalley-Eilenberg formula.

\operatorname{ev}_{1}
induces a smooth map
T\operatorname{ev}_{1}\big|_{\gamma,t}:T_{{\mathcal{F}},\gamma}P{\mathcal{F}}\to{\mathcal{F}}_{\gamma(t)}
. Given a vector bundle
V
on
M
, we can get a pullback bundle
W_{t}
along
\operatorname{ev}_{1}
at time
t
, i.e.
W_{t}=\operatorname{ev}_{1}^{*}V_{\gamma(t)}
. Hence,
W
is a vector bundle on
P{\mathcal{F}}\times\Delta_{1}
.

Lemma 27.10
.

\operatorname{ev}_{1}^{*}\Gamma({\mathcal{F}}^{\vee})
lies in
\Gamma(T^{\vee}_{{\mathcal{F}}}P{\mathcal{F}})
.

Proof.

∎

27.3.
Chen’s iterated integral

Let
\pi:{\mathcal{F}}\times\Delta^{k}\to{\mathcal{F}}
be the projection on the first factor.
Define the push forward map

\pi_{*}:\mathop{\bigwedge\nolimits^{\!\bullet}}\big(({\mathcal{F}}\times\Delta^{k})^{\vee}\big)\to\mathop{\bigwedge\nolimits^{\!\bullet}}({\mathcal{F}}^{\vee})

by

\pi_{*}\big(f(x,t)dt_{i_{1}}\cdots dt_{i_{k}}dx_{j_{1}}\cdots dx_{j_{s}}\big)=\Big(\int_{\Delta_{k}}f(x,t)dt_{i_{1}}\cdots dt_{i_{k}}\Big)dx_{j_{1}}\cdots dx_{j_{s}}

Note that here
{\mathcal{F}}\times\Delta^{k}
is a foliation on
M\times\Delta^{k}
which extends
{\mathcal{F}}
trivial along the
\Delta^{k}
direction, i.e.
{\mathcal{F}}\times\Delta^{k}:={\mathcal{F}}\times T\Delta^{k}
.

If
M
is compact, we have

\int_{M}\pi_{*}(\alpha)=\int_{M\times\Delta^{k}}\alpha

for all
\alpha\in\mathop{\bigwedge\nolimits^{\!\bullet}}({\mathcal{F}}{\vee}\otimes\Delta^{k})
.

Lemma 27.11
.

\pi_{*}
is a morphism of left
\mathop{\bigwedge\nolimits^{\!\bullet}}{\mathcal{F}}^{\vee}
-modules of degree
-k
, i.e for every
alpha\in\mathop{\bigwedge\nolimits^{\!\bullet}}({\mathcal{F}}^{\vee})
and
\beta\in\mathop{\bigwedge\nolimits^{\!\bullet}}\big(({\mathcal{F}}\times\Delta^{k})^{\vee}\big)
, we have

(27.4)

\pi_{*}(\pi^{*}\alpha\wedge\beta)=(-1)^{|\alpha|k}\alpha\wedge\pi_{*}\beta

In addition, let
\partial\pi
be the composition

{\mathcal{F}}\otimes\partial\Delta^{k}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\operatorname{Id}\otimes\iota}}{{\longrightarrow}}{\mathcal{F}}\otimes\Delta^{k}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\pi}}{{\longrightarrow}}M

Then we have

\pi_{*}\circ d-(-1)^{k}d\circ\pi_{*}=(\partial\pi)_{*}\circ(\operatorname{Id}\times\iota)^{*}

Proof.

.
Similar to
[
AS12
, Lemma 3.5]
. Note that we just need to restrict to integration along leaves.
∎

Next, we shall construct Chen’s iterated integral map. Let
a_{1}[1]\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{n}[1]
be an element of
\mathsf{B}\big((\mathop{\bigwedge\nolimits^{\!\bullet}}{\mathcal{F}}^{\vee})[1]\big)
. Given a path
\gamma:I\to{\mathcal{F}}\in P{\mathcal{F}}
, we define a differential form on
P{\mathcal{F}}
by

(1)

Pull back each
a_{i}
to
M^{k}
via the
i
-th projection map
p_{i}:M^{k}\to M
, then we get a wedge product
p_{1}^{*}a_{1}\wedge\cdots p_{k}^{*}a_{k}
.

(2)

Pullback
p_{1}^{*}a_{1}\wedge\cdots p_{k}^{*}a_{k}
to a form on
P{\mathcal{F}}\times\Delta^{k}
via
\operatorname{ev}_{k}
.

(3)

Push forward through
\pi
to get a form on
P{\mathcal{F}}
.

(4)

Finally, correct the sign by multiplying
\spadesuit=\sum_{1\leq i<k}(T(a_{i})-1)(k-i)
where
T(a_{i})
denotes the total degree of
a_{i}
.

In summary,

Definition 27.12

(Chen’s iterated integrals on foliated manifold)
.

Let
(M,{\mathcal{F}})
be a foliated manifold, define Chen’s iterated integral map from the bar complex of the suspension of foliation algebra to the foliated path space by

(27.5)

\mathsf{C}(a_{1}[1]\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{k}[1])=(-1)^{\spadesuit}\pi_{*}\big(\operatorname{ev}_{k}^{*}(p_{1}^{*}a_{1}\wedge\cdots p_{k}^{*}a_{k})\big)

Remark 27.13
.

Note that if any of the
a_{i}
’s is of degree 0, then the iterated integral vanishes. This follows from the observation that the form
\operatorname{ev}_{k}^{*}(p_{1}^{*}a_{1}\wedge\cdots p_{k}^{*}a_{k})\in\Omega^{\bullet}({\mathcal{F}}\otimes\Delta^{k})
is annihilated by vector fields
\frac{\partial}{\partial t_{i}}
’s,
1\leq i\leq k
, which forces the push forward along
\pi:P{\mathcal{F}}\times\Delta_{k}\to{\mathcal{F}}
vanishing.

Lemma 27.14
.

\mathsf{C}
is natural, i.e. for any foliated map
f:(M,{\mathcal{F}}_{1})\to(N,{\mathcal{F}}_{2})
, the diagram
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{B}\big((\mathop{\bigwedge\nolimits^{\!\bullet}}{\mathcal{F}}_{1}^{\vee})[1]\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega^{\bullet}(P{\mathcal{F}}_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{B}\big((\mathop{\bigwedge\nolimits^{\!\bullet}}{\mathcal{F}}_{2}^{\vee})[1]\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega^{\bullet}(P{\mathcal{F}}_{2})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{C}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{C}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{B}f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(Pf)^{*}}

Proof.

Since
f
is foliated,

\displaystyle(Pf)^{*}\operatorname{ev}_{k}^{*}(p_{1}^{*}a_{1}\wedge\cdots p_{k}^{*}a_{k})

\displaystyle=((f\otimes\operatorname{Id})\circ\operatorname{ev}_{k})^{*}(p_{1}^{*}a_{1}\wedge\cdots p_{k}^{*}a_{k})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{ev}_{k}^{*}f^{*}(p_{1}^{*}a_{1}\wedge\cdots p_{k}^{*}a_{k})

\displaystyle=\operatorname{ev}_{k}^{*}((p_{1}\circ f)^{*}a_{1}\wedge\cdots(p_{k}\circ f)^{*}a_{k})

∎

Lemma 27.15
.

Let
a_{1}[1]\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{k}[1]\in\mathsf{B}\big((\mathop{\bigwedge\nolimits^{\!\bullet}}{\mathcal{F}}^{\vee})[1]\big)
be an element of the bar complex, then we have

(27.6)

\displaystyle d(\mathsf{C}(a_{1}[1]\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{k}[1]))=

\displaystyle\mathsf{C}\big(\overline{D}(a_{1}[1]\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{k}])\big)+\operatorname{ev}_{1}^{*}(a_{1})\wedge\mathsf{C}(a_{2}[1]\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{k}[1])

(27.7)

\displaystyle-(-1)^{|a_{1}|+\cdots|a_{k-1}|}\mathsf{C}(a_{1}[1]\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{k-1}[1])\wedge\operatorname{ev}_{0}^{*}(a_{n})

here
\overline{D}
is the differential of the foliation dga
(\mathop{\bigwedge\nolimits^{\!\bullet}}{\mathcal{F}}^{\vee},-d,\wedge)
.

Proof.

Note that by lemma,

\displaystyle d(\mathsf{C}(a_{1}[1]\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{k}[1]))=

\displaystyle(-1)^{\spadesuit}\Big((-1)^{k}(\pi_{*}d\big(\operatorname{ev}_{k}^{*}(p_{1}^{*}a_{1}\wedge\cdots p_{k}^{*}a_{k})\big)

\displaystyle+(-1)^{k+1}((\partial\pi)_{*}(\operatorname{Id}\otimes\iota)^{*}\big(\operatorname{ev}_{k}^{*}(p_{1}^{*}a_{1}\wedge\cdots p_{k}^{*}a_{k})\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{k}(-1)^{|a_{1}|+\cdots|a_{i-1}|}\mathsf{C}(a_{1}[1]\otimes\cdots\otimes(-da_{i})[1]\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{k}[1])

\displaystyle+\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{k-1}(-1)^{|a_{1}|+\cdots|a_{i}|}\mathsf{C}(a_{1}[1]\otimes\cdots\otimes(a_{i}\wedge a_{i+1})[1]\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{k}[1])

\displaystyle+\operatorname{ev}_{1}^{*}(a_{1})\wedge\mathsf{C}(a_{2}[1]\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{k}[1])

\displaystyle-(-1)^{|a_{1}|+\cdots|a_{k-1}|}\mathsf{C}(a_{1}[1]\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{k-1}[1])\wedge\operatorname{ev}_{0}^{*}(a_{n})\Big)

∎

Let
{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}_{+,\partial I}(I)
be the space of differentiable maps from
I\to I
which are monotonically increasing and fixing the boundary
\partial I
.

Definition 27.16
.

We call a differential form
\alpha\in\Omega^{\bullet}(P{\mathcal{F}})
is
reparametrization invariant
if
\alpha
is invariant under any reparametrization
\phi\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}_{+,\partial I}(I)
, i.e.

\phi^{*}\alpha=\alpha

Denote the subcomplex of invariant forms by
\Omega^{\bullet}_{\operatorname{Inv}}(P{\mathcal{F}})

Lemma 27.17
.

The image’s of Chen’s map on foliation

\mathsf{C}:\mathsf{B}\big((\mathop{\bigwedge\nolimits^{\!\bullet}}{\mathcal{F}}_{1}^{\vee})[1]\big)\to\Omega^{\bullet}(P{\mathcal{F}}_{1})

lies in
\Omega^{\bullet}_{\operatorname{Inv}}(P{\mathcal{F}})

Proof.

∎

27.4.
Cube’s to simplices

In this section, we shall construct a map

(27.8)

\mathsf{S}:\Omega^{\bullet}(P{\mathcal{F}})\to C^{\bullet}({\mathcal{F}})[1]

which is based on Igusa’s construction from cubes to simplices
[
Igu09
]
.
Recall that in this chapter, we parametrize the
k
-simplex by

\Delta^{k}=\{(t_{1},\cdots,t_{k})\in{\mathbb{R}}^{k}|1\geq t_{1}\geq t_{2}\cdots\geq t_{k}\geq 0\}\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{k}

The coface maps
\partial_{i}:\Delta^{k}\to\Delta^{k+1}
are given by

(27.9)

(t_{1},\cdots,t_{k})\mapsto\begin{cases}(1,t_{1},\cdots,t_{k})&\text{for $i=0$}\\
(t_{1},\cdots,t_{i-1},t_{i},t_{i},t_{i+1},\cdots,t_{k})&\text{for $0<i<k+1$}\\
(t_{1},\cdots,t_{k},0)&\text{for $i=k+1$}\end{cases}

The codegeneracy maps
\epsilon_{i}:\Delta^{k}\to\Delta^{k-1}
are given by

(27.10)

(t_{1},\cdots,t_{k})\mapsto(t_{1},\cdots,\hat{t_{i}},\cdots,t_{k})

The
i
-th vertex of
\Delta^{k}
is the point

(\underbrace{1,\cdots,1}_{\text{ $i$-times}},\underbrace{0,\cdots,0}_{\text{ $k-i$-times}})

Recall the smooth singular
{\mathcal{F}}
-chains
C_{\bullet}({\mathcal{F}})
is given by
C_{k}({\mathcal{F}})={\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}(\Delta^{k},M)
. With structure map
d_{i}=\partial_{i}^{*},s_{i}=\epsilon_{i}^{*}
, we equip
C_{\bullet}({\mathcal{F}})
a simplicial set structure, which is equivalent to the monodromy
\infty
-groupoids
\operatorname{Mon}^{\infty}({\mathcal{F}})
of
{\mathcal{F}}

We define maps
P_{i}
and
Q_{i}
which send element of
\operatorname{Mon}^{\infty}({\mathcal{F}})
to its back-face and front-face respectively, i.t.
P_{i}
and
Q_{i}
are pullbacks of

\displaystyle U_{i}:

\displaystyle\Delta^{i}\to\Delta^{k},(t_{1},\cdots,t_{i})\mapsto(1,\cdots,1,t_{1},\cdots,t_{i})

\displaystyle V_{i}:

\displaystyle\Delta^{i}\to\Delta^{k},(t_{1},\cdots,t_{i})\mapsto(t_{1},\cdots,t_{i},0,\cdots,0)

respectively.

Definition 27.18
.

Let
(M,{\mathcal{F}})
be a foliated manifold, we define the dga of (smooth) singular
{\mathcal{F}}
-cochains
(C^{\bullet}(M),\delta,\cup)
consisting of the following data:

(1)

The grade vector space
C^{\bullet}(M)
of linear functional on the vector space generated by
\operatorname{Mon}^{\infty}({\mathcal{F}})
.

(2)

The differential
\delta
is given by

(\delta\phi)(\sigma)=\sum_{i=0}^{k}(d^{*}_{i}\phi)(\sigma)=\sum_{i=0}^{k}(\phi)(\partial^{*}_{i}\sigma)

(3)

The product
\cup
is given by the usual cup product

(\phi\cup\psi)(\sigma)=\phi(V_{i}^{*}\sigma)\psi(U_{j}^{*}\sigma)

Define
\pi_{k}:I^{k}\to\Delta_{k}
by the order preserving retraction, i.e
\pi_{k}(x_{1},\cdots,x_{k})=(t_{1},\cdots,t_{k})
with
t_{i}=\max\{x_{i},\cdots,x_{k}\}
for each
k
.

Consider an element
\lambda_{w}:I\to I^{k}
of
PI^{k}
which is parametrized by a
w\in I^{k}
. In detail, if
w=(w_{1},\cdots,w_{k-1})
, then
\lambda_{k}
travels backwards through the
k+1
points

0\leftarrow w_{1}x_{1}\leftarrow w_{1}x_{1}+w_{2}x_{2}\leftarrow\cdots\leftarrow\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}e_{i}

For more details, see
[
Igu09
, Proposition 4.6]
.
Set
\lambda_{(k-1)}:I^{k-1}\to PI^{k}
by sending
w
to
\lambda_{w}
.

Finally, we define
\theta_{(k)}
to be the composition

\theta_{(k)}=P\pi_{k}\circ\lambda_{(k-1)}:I^{k-1}\to P\Delta^{k}

We denote the adjoint of
\theta_{(k)}
to be
\theta_{k}:I^{k}\to\Delta^{k}
.

Remark 27.19
.

By construction,
\theta_{(k)}
are piecewise linear but not smooth. We can correct it by reparametrization, for example, let the derivative vanish near the vertices. Since the image of Chen’s map
\mathsf{C}
is invariant, our construction for
\theta_{(k)}
is well-defined.

Remark 27.20
.

It is easy to verify that

\int_{I^{k}}\theta^{*}_{k}\alpha=(-1)^{k}\int_{\Delta^{k}}\alpha

for any form
\alpha\in\Omega^{\bullet}(\Delta^{k})
.

For each
i
, define
\widehat{\partial_{i}^{-}}
to be the map which inserts a 0 between the
(i-1)
-th and
i
-th coordinates. Note that the
i
-th negative face operator is given by
\partial_{i}^{-}(\theta_{(k)})=\theta_{(k)}\circ\widehat{\partial_{i}^{-}}

Lemma 27.21

(
[
Igu09
]
)
.

For each
1\leq i\leq k-1
, we have the following commutative diagram
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I^{k-2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I^{k-1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P(\Delta^{k},v_{k},v_{0})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P(\Delta^{k-1},v_{k-1},v_{0})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P(\Delta^{k-1},v_{k-1},v_{0})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\partial_{i}^{-}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta_{(k-1)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta_{(k)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\omega_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P\partial_{i}}

that is,

\partial_{i}^{-}(\theta_{(k)})=\theta_{(k)}\circ\widehat{\partial_{i}^{-}}=P\widehat{\partial_{i}^{-}}\circ\omega_{i}\circ\theta_{(k-1)}

Here
w_{i}
is given by the following reparametrization:
for each
\gamma\in P(\Delta^{k-1},v_{k-1},v_{0})
,
w_{i}(\gamma)
is defined by

\omega_{i}(\gamma)(t)=\begin{cases}\gamma(\frac{kt}{k-1})&\text{if $t\leq\frac{j-1}{k}$}\\
\gamma(\frac{j-1}{k-1})&\text{if $\frac{j-1}{k}\leq k\leq\frac{k}{k}$}\\
\gamma(\frac{kt-1}{k-1})&\text{if $t\geq\frac{j}{k}$}\end{cases}

Proof.

See
[
Igu09
, Lemma 4.7]
.
∎

Set
\widehat{\partial_{i}^{+}}:I^{k-1}\to I^{k-1}
to be the map which inserts 1 between the
(i-1)
-th and
i
-th places.

Lemma 27.22

(
[
Igu09
]
)
.

For each
1\leq i\leq k-1
, we have the following commutative diagram
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I^{k-2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I^{k-1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P(\Delta^{k},v_{k},v_{0})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I^{i-1}\times I^{k-i-1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P(\Delta^{i},v_{i},v_{0})\times P(\Delta^{k-i},v_{k-i},v_{0})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\partial_{i}^{+}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta_{(k)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta_{(i)}\times\theta_{(k-i)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu_{i}}

that is,

\partial_{i}^{+}(\theta_{(k)})=\theta_{(k)}\circ\widehat{\partial_{i}^{+}}=\mu_{i}\circ(\theta_{(i)}\times\theta_{(j)})

where
\mu_{i,j}
is the path composition map

\mu_{i}(\alpha,\beta)(t)=\begin{cases}U_{k-i}\big(\beta(\frac{kt}{k-i})\big)&\text{if $t\leq\frac{k-i}{k}$}\\
V_{i}\Big(\alpha\big(\frac{k}{i}(t-\frac{k-i}{k})\big)\Big)&\text{if $k\geq\frac{k-i}{k}$}\end{cases}

Proof.

See
[
Igu09
, Lemma 4.8]
.
∎

Lemma 27.23

(
[
AS12
]
)
.

Let
a_{1},\cdots,a_{n}
be forms on
\Delta^{k}
, then we have the following factorization

\displaystyle\int_{P(\Delta^{i},v_{i},v_{0})\times P(\Delta^{k-i},v_{k-i},v_{0})}(\mu_{i})^{*}\mathsf{C}(a_{1}[1]\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{n})

\displaystyle=\sum_{l=1}^{n}\Big(\int_{P(\Delta^{i},v_{i},v_{0})}\mathsf{C}(V_{i}^{*}a_{1}[1]\otimes\cdots\otimes V_{i}^{*}a_{l}[1])\Big)

\displaystyle\times\Big(\int_{P(\Delta^{k-i},v_{k-i},v_{0})}\mathsf{C}(U_{k-i}^{*}a_{l+1}1[1]\otimes\cdots\otimes U_{k-i}^{*}a_{n}[1])\Big)

Proof.

See
[
AS12
, Lemma 3.19]
.
∎

We define the map
\mathsf{S}:\Omega^{\bullet}(P{\mathcal{F}})\to C^{\bullet}({\mathcal{F}})[1]
to be

\mathsf{S}(\alpha)=\int_{I^{k-1}}(\theta_{(k)})^{*}P\sigma^{*}\alpha

for
\alpha\in\Omega^{\bullet}(P{\mathcal{F}})
.

27.5.
A_{\infty}
de Rham theorem for foliation

Next, we will prove the
A_{\infty}
-enhancement of the de Rham theorem for foliations.

Theorem 27.24

(
A_{\infty}
de Rham theorem for foliation)
.

Let
(M,{\mathcal{F}})
be a foliated manifold, there exists an
A_{\infty}
-quasi-isomorphism between
\big(\Omega^{\bullet}({\mathcal{F}}),-d,\wedge\big)
and
\big(C^{\bullet}({\mathcal{F}}),\delta,\cup\big)

We have already constructed the map

\mathsf{S}\circ\mathsf{C}:\mathsf{B}\big((\mathop{\bigwedge\nolimits^{\!\bullet}}{\mathcal{F}}_{1}^{\vee})[1]\big)\to\Omega^{\bullet}(P{\mathcal{F}}_{1})\to C^{\bullet}({\mathcal{F}})[1]

Lemma 27.25
.

Let
a_{1},\cdots,a_{n}
be
{\mathcal{F}}
-foliated forms, then we have the following identity

\displaystyle\mathsf{S}(d(\mathsf{C}(a_{1}[1]\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{n}[1])))=

\displaystyle\delta^{\prime}(\mathsf{S}(\mathsf{C}(a_{1}[1]\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{n}[1])))+

\displaystyle\sum_{l=1}^{n-1}\mathsf{S}((\mathsf{C}(a_{1}[1]\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{l}[1])))\cup^{\prime}\mathsf{S}((\mathsf{C}(a_{l+1}[1]\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{n}[1])))

Here
\delta^{\prime}
and
\cup^{\prime}
are differential and product of the dga of singular
{\mathcal{F}}
-cochains at the level of suspensions.

Proof.

We follow
[
AS12
, Proposition 3.22]
. Consider
\alpha=\mathsf{C}(a_{1}[1]\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{n}[1])\in\Omega^{\bullet}(P{\mathcal{F}}_{1})
, and
\sigma\in\operatorname{Mon}^{\infty}({\mathcal{F}})^{k}
a simplex.
We want to compute

\int_{I^{k-1}}d(\theta_{(k)})^{*}P\sigma^{*}\alpha=\int_{\partial I^{k-1}}\iota^{*}(\theta_{(k)})^{*}P\sigma^{*}\alpha

Recall
\widehat{\partial^{\pm}_{i}}
are the canonical embeddings of
I^{k-2}
into
I^{k-1}
as top and bottom faces. Then the right-hand side of the above equation breaks to

\sum_{i=1}^{k-1}(-1)^{i}\int_{I^{k-2}}(\partial_{i}^{-})^{*}(\theta_{(k)})^{*}P\sigma^{*}\alpha-\sum_{i=1}^{k-1}(-1)^{i}\int_{I^{k-2}}(\partial_{i}^{+})^{*}(\theta_{(k)})^{*}P\sigma^{*}\alpha

By Lemma
27.21
and properties of Chen’s map, we have

\displaystyle\int_{I^{k-2}}(\partial_{i}^{-})^{*}(\theta_{(k)})^{*}P\sigma^{*}\alpha=

\displaystyle\int_{I^{k-2}}(\theta_{(k-1)})^{*}(P\partial_{i}^{*}\sigma)^{*}\alpha

On the other hand, by Lemma
27.22
and Lemma
27.23
,

\displaystyle\int_{I^{k-2}}

\displaystyle(\partial_{i}^{+})^{*}(\theta_{(k)})^{*}P\sigma^{*}\alpha=

\displaystyle\sum_{l=0}^{n}\mathsf{S}((\mathsf{C}(a_{1}[1]\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{l}[1])))(V_{i}^{*}\sigma)\mathsf{S}((\mathsf{C}(a_{l+1}[1]\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{n}[1])))(U^{*}_{k-i\sigma})

Summing up all the items yields the desired result.
∎

Now we describe our proposed
A_{\infty}
-map. Let
(M,{\mathcal{F}})
be a foliated manifold, we define a series of maps
\phi_{n}:(\Omega^{\bullet}({\mathcal{F}})[1])^{\otimes n}\to C^{\bullet}({\mathcal{F}})[1]
by

(1)

For
n=1
,

(\phi_{1}(a[1])(\sigma)=(-1)^{k}\int_{\Delta^{k}}\sigma^{*}\alpha

(2)

For
n>1
,

\phi_{n}(a_{1}[1]\otimes\cdots a_{n}[1])=(\mathsf{S}\circ\mathsf{C})(a_{1}[1]\otimes\cdots a_{n}[1])

Next we shall prove that
\phi_{n}
’s form an
A_{\infty}
-morphism. The case for
{\mathcal{F}}=TM
is proved by Guggenheim in
[
Gug77
]
. We will follow the proof in
[
AS12
, Theorem 3.25]
.

Proposition 27.26
.

\phi_{n}
’s form an
A_{\infty}
-morphism from
\Omega^{\bullet}({\mathcal{F}})
to
C^{\bullet}({\mathcal{F}})
which induces a quasi-isomorphism. Moreover, this map is natural with respect to pullbacks along
{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}
-maps.

Proof.

Let
a_{1}[1]\otimes\cdots a_{n}[1]\in\mathsf{B}\big((\mathop{\bigwedge\nolimits^{\!\bullet}}{\mathcal{F}}_{1}^{\vee})[1]\big)
.

First consider the case
n\not=2
. By lemma
27.15
,

\displaystyle d(\mathsf{C}(a_{1}[1]\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{k}[1]))=

\displaystyle\mathsf{C}\big(\overline{D}(a_{1}[1]\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{k}])\big)+\operatorname{ev}_{1}^{*}(a_{1})\wedge\mathsf{C}(a_{2}[1]\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{k}[1])

\displaystyle-(-1)^{|a_{1}|+\cdots|a_{k-1}|}\mathsf{C}(a_{1}[1]\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{k-1}[1])\wedge\operatorname{ev}_{0}^{*}(a_{n})

By lemma
27.25
,

\displaystyle\mathsf{S}(d(\mathsf{C}(a_{1}[1]\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{n}[1])))=

\displaystyle\delta^{\prime}(\mathsf{S}(\mathsf{C}(a_{1}[1]\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{n}[1])))+

\displaystyle\sum_{l=1}^{n-1}\mathsf{S}((\mathsf{C}(a_{1}[1]\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{l}[1])))\cup^{\prime}\mathsf{S}((\mathsf{C}(a_{l+1}[1]\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{n}[1])))

Combining these two equations gives

\displaystyle(\mathsf{S}\circ\mathsf{C})\big(\overline{D}(a_{1}[1]\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{n}])\big)=

\displaystyle\delta^{\prime}(\mathsf{S}(\mathsf{C}(a_{1}[1]\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{n}[1])))

\displaystyle+\sum_{l=1}^{n-1}\mathsf{S}((\mathsf{C}(a_{1}[1]\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{l}[1])))\cup^{\prime}\mathsf{S}((\mathsf{C}(a_{l+1}[1]\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{n}[1])))

\displaystyle-\mathsf{S}\big(\operatorname{ev}_{1}^{*}(a_{1})\wedge\mathsf{C}(a_{2}[1]\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{n}[1])\big)

\displaystyle+(-1)^{|a_{1}|+\cdots|a_{n-1}|}\mathsf{S}\big(\mathsf{C}(a_{1}[1]\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{n-1}[1])\wedge\operatorname{ev}_{0}^{*}(a_{n})\big)

The third term

\mathsf{S}\big(\operatorname{ev}_{1}^{*}(a_{1})\wedge\mathsf{C}(a_{2}[1]\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{n}[1])\big)=-\phi_{1}(a_{1}[1])\cup^{\prime}(\mathsf{S}\circ\mathsf{C})(a_{2}[1]\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{n}[1])

for
|a_{1}|=0
.
The fourth term

\displaystyle\mathsf{S}

\displaystyle\big(\mathsf{C}(a_{1}[1]\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{n-1}[1])\wedge\operatorname{ev}_{0}^{*}(a_{n})\big)=

\displaystyle(-1)^{|a_{1}|+\cdots|a_{k-1}|}(\mathsf{S}\circ\mathsf{C})(a_{1}[1]\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{n-1}[1])\cup^{\prime}\phi_{1}(a_{n}[1])

for
|a_{n}|=0
. These two terms vanish for
|a_{1}|>0
and
|a_{n}|>0
respectively.

Therefore, putting everything together, we have

\displaystyle(\mathsf{S}\circ\mathsf{C})\big(\overline{D}(a_{1}[1]\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{n}])\big)=

\displaystyle\delta^{\prime}(\phi_{n}(a_{1}[1]\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{n}[1]))

\displaystyle+\sum_{l=1}^{n-1}\phi_{l}(a_{1}[1]\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{l}[1]))\cup^{\prime}\phi_{n-l}(a_{l+1}[1]\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{n}[1])

On the other hand, by definition

\displaystyle(\mathsf{S}

\displaystyle\circ\mathsf{C})\big(\overline{D}(a_{1}[1]\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{n}])\big)=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}(-1)^{|a_{1}|+\cdots|a_{i-1}|}\phi_{n}(a_{1}[1]\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{i-1}[1]\otimes(-da_{i})[1]\otimes a_{i+1}[1]\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{n}[1])

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}(-1)^{|a_{1}|+\cdots|a_{i}|}\phi_{n-1}(a_{1}[1]\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{i-1}[1]\otimes(a_{i}\wedge a_{i+1})[1]\otimes a_{i+2}[1]\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{n}[1])

Combining these two equations yields the desired
A_{\infty}
-structure maps.

For
n=2
and
|a_{1}|=|a_{2}|=0
. Just noted that for two foliated functions, for
\phi
to be an
A_{\infty}
-map, we only need to check
(a_{1}a_{2})(x)=a_{1}(x)a_{2}(x)
.

The quasi-isomorphism follows from the ordinary de Rham theorem for foliations (Theorem
27.1
). The naturality follows from the naturality of the maps
\mathsf{S}
and
\mathsf{C}
.

∎

Remark 27.27
.

It is easy to verified that, according to the construction,

\phi_{1}(f[1])=f[1]
for any
|f|=0
, and
\phi_{n}(a_{1}[1]\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{n}[1])
vanishes if any of the
a_{i}[1]
in the argument is of degree 0.

Lemma 27.28
.

The image of
\phi_{n}
’s lies in the dga of normalized
{\mathcal{F}}
-cochains.

Proof.

Follows from
[
AS12
, Proposition 3.26]
. Note that by our construction of
\phi_{n}
, we just need to restricted to leaves.
∎

28.
Riemann-Hilbert correspondence for
\infty
-foliated local systems

28.1.
Iterated integrals on vector bundles

In this section, we shall generalize iterated integrals in the previous section to the case of graded vector bundles (or dg modules).
Let
V
be a graded vector bundle on
M
, denote

\displaystyle\iota:\big(\ \Gamma(\operatorname{End}(V)\otimes\bigwedge\nolimits^{\bullet}{\mathcal{F}}^{\vee})\big)^{\otimes_{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k}}

\displaystyle\to\Gamma\big(\operatorname{End}(V)^{\boxtimes^{k}}\otimes(\mathop{\bigwedge\nolimits^{\!\bullet}}{\mathcal{F}}^{\vee})^{\boxtimes^{k}}\big)

\displaystyle a_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{n}

\displaystyle\mapsto a_{1}\boxtimes\cdots\boxtimes a_{n}

the canonical embedding.

The pull back of
\operatorname{ev}_{k}
induces

\operatorname{ev}_{k}^{*}:\Gamma\big(\operatorname{End}(V)^{\boxtimes^{k}}\otimes(\mathop{\bigwedge\nolimits^{\!\bullet}}{\mathcal{F}}^{\vee})^{\boxtimes^{k}}\big)\to\Gamma\big(\boxtimes_{i}\operatorname{ev}_{1}^{*}\operatorname{End}(V)_{t_{i}}\otimes(\mathop{\bigwedge\nolimits^{\!\bullet}}T^{\vee}_{{\mathcal{F}}}P{\mathcal{F}})\times\Delta^{k}\big)

Let
\mu
denote the multiplication map on
\operatorname{ev}_{1}^{*}\operatorname{End}(V)_{t_{i}}
, i.e.

\mu:\operatorname{ev}_{k}^{*}\Gamma\big(\operatorname{End}(V)^{\boxtimes^{k}}\otimes(\mathop{\bigwedge\nolimits^{\!\bullet}}{\mathcal{F}}^{\vee})^{\boxtimes^{k}}\big)\to\Gamma\big(p_{0}^{*}\operatorname{End}(V)\otimes(\mathop{\bigwedge\nolimits^{\!\bullet}}T^{\vee}_{{\mathcal{F}}}P{\mathcal{F}})\times\Delta^{k}\big)

where
p_{0}:P{\mathcal{F}}\to M
is the evaluation map at
t=0
. Denote
\pi
the projection map
\pi:P{\mathcal{F}}\times\Delta^{k}\to P{\mathcal{F}}
.

Definition 28.1
.

We define the iterative integral

\int:\big(\ \Gamma(\operatorname{End}(V)\otimes\bigwedge\nolimits^{\bullet}{\mathcal{F}}^{\vee})\big)^{\otimes_{{\mathbb{R}}}^{k}}\to\Gamma\big(p_{0}^{*}\operatorname{End}(V)\otimes(\mathop{\bigwedge\nolimits^{\!\bullet}}T^{\vee}_{{\mathcal{F}}}P{\mathcal{F}})\big)

on graded vector bundles
V
over a foliation
{\mathcal{F}}
to be the composition

(28.1)

\int a_{1}\otimes a_{2}\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{k}=(-1)^{\spadesuit}\pi_{\star}\circ\mu\circ\operatorname{ev}_{l}^{*}\circ\iota(a_{1}\otimes a_{2}\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{k})

with
\spadesuit=\sum_{1\leq i<k}(T(a_{i})-1)(k-i)
where
T(a_{i})
denotes the total degree of
a_{i}
.

Lemma 28.2
.

On
\Gamma(\operatorname{End}(V)\otimes\bigwedge\nolimits^{\bullet}{\mathcal{F}}^{\vee})\big)
, we have

\pi_{*}\circ d-(-1)^{k}d\circ\pi_{*}=(\partial\pi)_{*}\circ(\operatorname{Id}\times\iota)^{*}

Let
\alpha\in\Gamma(\operatorname{End}(V)\otimes\mathop{\bigwedge\nolimits^{\!\bullet}}T^{\vee}_{{\mathcal{F}}}P{\mathcal{F}})\big)
,
\beta\in\Gamma\big(\operatorname{End}(V)\otimes(\mathop{\bigwedge\nolimits^{\!\bullet}}T^{\vee}_{{\mathcal{F}}}P{\mathcal{F}})\times\Delta^{k}\big)
,

\displaystyle\pi_{*}(\pi^{*}\alpha\circ\beta)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{kT(\alpha)}\alpha\circ\pi_{*}\beta

\displaystyle\pi_{*}(\circ\beta\pi^{*}\alpha)

\displaystyle=\pi_{*}\beta\circ\alpha

Proof.

Similar to Lemma
27.11
.
∎

Lemma 28.3

(Stoke’s theorem)
.

(28.2)

\displaystyle d\int\omega_{1}\cdots\omega_{r}=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{r}(-1)^{i}T\omega_{1}\cdots d\omega_{i}\omega_{i+1}\cdots\omega_{r}+\sum_{i=1}^{r-1}(-1)^{i}T\omega_{1}\cdots(T\omega_{i}\circ\omega_{i+1})\cdots\omega_{r}

(28.3)

\displaystyle+p_{1}^{*}\omega_{1}\circ\int\omega_{2}\cdots\omega_{r}-T(\int\omega_{1}\cdots\omega_{r-1})\circ p_{0}^{*}\omega_{r}

Proof.

Similar to Lemma
27.15
. See also
[
BS14
, Proposition 3.3]
.
∎

28.2.
\infty
-holonomy of
{\mathbb{Z}}
-connection over
{\mathcal{F}}

Let
V
be a
{\mathbb{Z}}
-graded vector bundle with a
{\mathbb{Z}}
-connection
\nabla
over
{A}^{\bullet}=\mathop{\bigwedge\nolimits^{\!\bullet}}{\mathcal{F}}^{\vee}
. Locally,
\nabla=d-\sum_{i=0}^{m}A_{i}
, where
A_{i}\in\operatorname{End}^{1-i}(V)\otimes_{{A}_{0}}{A}^{i}
. Let
\omega=\sum_{i=0}^{m}A_{i}
. We define
p
-th holonomy of
\nabla
to be the iterative integral

(28.4)

\Psi_{p}=\int\omega^{\otimes p}\in\Gamma\big((\mathop{\bigwedge\nolimits^{\!k}}T^{\vee}_{{\mathcal{F}}}P{\mathcal{F}})\otimes\operatorname{End}^{-k}(V)\big)

and
\Psi_{0}=\operatorname{Id}
for
p=0
.

Definition 28.4
.

Define the
\infty
-holonomy
associated to
\nabla
to be
\Psi=\sum_{p=0}^{\infty}\Psi_{p}
.

Since
\omega
has total degree 1,

\displaystyle\Psi_{p}=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1,j=p-i}^{p}(-1)^{i+1}\int(\omega^{\otimes i})d\omega(\omega^{\otimes j})+\sum_{i=1,j=p-i-1}^{p-1}(-1)^{i}\int(\omega^{\otimes i})(\omega_{i}\circ\omega_{i+1})(\omega^{\otimes j})

\displaystyle+p_{1}^{*}\omega\circ\int\omega^{\otimes(r-1)}-\big(\int\omega^{\otimes r-1}\big)\circ p_{0}^{*}\omega

Summing in
p
, we get

\displaystyle d\Psi=\bigg(\int\kappa+\big(\int\kappa\omega+-\int\omega\kappa\big)+\cdots+\sum_{i+j=p-1}(-1)^{i}\int\omega^{i}\kappa\omega^{j}+\cdots\bigg)+p_{1}^{*}\omega\circ\Psi-\Psi\circ p_{0}^{*}\omega

If
\nabla
is flat, then locally
\nabla^{2}=(d-\omega)^{2}=-d\omega-T\omega\circ\omega=-d\omega+\omega\circ\omega
.

Let
\sigma:\Delta^{k}\to{\mathcal{F}}
be a foliated simplex. We can regard it as a
k-1
-family of paths into
{\mathcal{F}}
. We can break this into two parts. First we have a map
