\theta_{(k-1)}:I_{k-1}\to P\Delta^{k}_{(v_{k},v_{0})}
, then there is a canonical map
P\sigma:P\Delta^{k}_{(v_{k},v_{0})}\to P{\mathcal{F}}_{(x_{k},x_{0})}
. We define a series of map
\psi_{k}\in\operatorname{End}^{1-k}(V)
by

\psi_{k}(\sigma)=\begin{cases}\int_{I^{k-1}}(-1)^{(k-1)(K\Psi)}\theta^{*}_{(k-1)}(P\sigma)^{*}\Psi&\quad k\geq 1\\
(V_{x},\nabla^{0}_{x})&\quad k=0\end{cases}

which is essentially the integral of
I^{k-1}
of the pullback holonomy of the
{\mathbb{Z}}
-connection
\nabla
.

Now we define the Riemann-Hilbert functor
\operatorname{RH}:\mathcal{P}_{{A}}\to\mathtt{Rep}(\operatorname{Mon}_{\infty}{\mathcal{F}})
. On objects we define
\operatorname{RH}_{0}:\operatorname{Obj}(\mathcal{P}_{{A}})\to\operatorname{Obj}(\mathtt{Rep}(\operatorname{Mon}_{\infty}{\mathcal{F}}))
by
\operatorname{RH}_{0}\big((E^{\bullet},\nabla)\big)(\sigma_{k})=\psi_{k}(\sigma_{k})
. We claim that the image of this functor are
\infty
-local systems. Note that, by our construction

\displaystyle\operatorname{RH}_{0}\big((E^{\bullet},\nabla)\big)_{x}=

\displaystyle E_{x}

\displaystyle\operatorname{RH}_{0}\big((E^{\bullet},\nabla)\big)(x)=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}^{0}_{x}

\displaystyle\operatorname{RH}_{0}\big((E^{\bullet},\nabla)\big)(\sigma_{k>0})=

\displaystyle\int_{I^{k-1}}(-1)^{(k-1)(K\Psi)}\theta^{*}_{(k-1)}(P\sigma)^{*}\Psi

Write
F
the image of
\operatorname{RH}_{0}\big((E^{\bullet})),\nabla)\big)
for simplicity, i.e.
F(\sigma_{k})=\operatorname{RH}_{0}\big((E^{\bullet}))(\sigma_{k})
. Since
\mathbb{E}
is flat, we have

d\Psi=-p_{0}^{*}A^{0}\circ\Psi+\Psi\circ p_{1}^{*}A^{0}

Integrate the left side and apply the Stoke’s formula we get

-\hat{\delta}F-\sum_{i=1}^{k-1}(-1)^{i}F(\sigma_{0\cdots i})F(\sigma_{i\cdots k})

Plug in the integration of right side, we get

\mathbb{E}^{0}\circ F(\sigma_{k})-(-1)^{k}F(\sigma_{k})-\sum_{i=1}^{k-1}(-1)^{i}F(\sigma_{(0\cdots\hat{i}\cdots k)})+\sum_{i=1}^{k-1}(-1)^{i}F(\sigma_{0\cdots i})F(\sigma_{i\cdots k})=0

which is the
k
-th level of the Maurer-Cartan equations for
\infty
-local system condition. Therefore,
\operatorname{RH}_{0}
is a well-defined map on objects.

Theorem 28.5

(
[
Igu09
]
)
.

The image of an object under the functor
\operatorname{RH}
is an
\infty
-representations of
\operatorname{Mon}_{\infty}({\mathcal{F}})
if and only if
\nabla
is flat.

Proof.

By Theorem 4.10 in
[
Igu09
]
, we have

\psi_{0}(x_{0})\phi_{k}(\sigma)+(-1)^{k}\psi_{k}(\sigma)\psi(x_{k})=\sum_{i=1}^{k-1}(-1)^{i}\big(\psi_{k-1}(\sigma_{(0\cdots\hat{i}\cdots k)})-\psi_{i}(\sigma_{(0\dots i)})\psi_{k-i}(\sigma_{(i\cdots k)})\big)

which is equivalent to

\sum_{i=1}^{k-1}(-1)^{i}\psi_{k-1}(\sigma_{(0\cdots\hat{i}\cdots k)})-\sum_{i=0}^{k}(-1)^{i}\psi_{i}(\sigma_{(0\dots i)})\psi_{k-i}(\sigma_{(i\cdots k)})

i.e.
\hat{\delta}\psi+\psi\cup\psi=0
.

For the other direction, we just go back from the definition of
\psi
, and found that
A_{0}\Psi_{k}-\Psi_{k}A_{0}=d\Psi_{k-1}
must be equal for all
k
, which is equivalent to the flatness of
\nabla
.
∎

Now we proceed to
\operatorname{RH}
on higher simplices

\operatorname{RH}_{n}:\mathcal{P}_{{A}}(E_{n-1}^{\bullet},E_{n}^{\bullet})\otimes\cdots\otimes\mathcal{P}_{{A}}(E_{0}^{\bullet},E_{1}^{\bullet})\to\mathtt{Rep}(\operatorname{Mon}_{\infty}{\mathcal{F}})(\operatorname{RH}_{0}(E_{0}^{\bullet}),\operatorname{RH}_{0}(E_{n}^{\bullet}))[1-n]

by

(28.5)

\operatorname{RH}_{n}(\phi_{n}\otimes\cdots\otimes\phi_{1})(\sigma_{k})=\operatorname{RH}_{0}\big(C_{(\phi_{n}\otimes\cdots\otimes\phi_{1})}(\sigma_{k})\big)_{n+1,1}

Next, we will need
\infty
-holonomy with respect to the pre-triangulated structure of
\mathcal{P}_{{\mathcal{A}}}
. We follow the calculation in
[
BS14
, Section 3.5]

of the following

•

\infty
-holonomy with respect to the shift.

•

\infty
-holonomy with the cone

Proposition 28.6
.

\operatorname{RH}
is an
A_{\infty}
-functor.

Proof.

We follow
[
BS14
, Theorem 4.2]
. Let
\phi=\phi_{n}\otimes\cdots\otimes\phi_{1}\in\mathcal{P}_{{A}}(E_{n-1},E_{n})\otimes\cdots\otimes\mathcal{P}_{{A}}(E_{0},E_{1})
be a tuple of morphisms, denote the holonomy of the associated to the generalized homological cone
C_{\phi}
by
\Psi^{\phi_{n}\otimes\cdots\otimes\phi_{1}}
. Locally, we can write
D^{\phi}=d-\omega
. By …, we have that on
P{\mathcal{F}}(x_{0},x_{1})
, where
x_{0},x_{1}
lie in some leaf. By the
\infty
-holonomy for cones, we have

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle d\Psi^{\phi_{n}\otimes\cdots\otimes\phi_{1}}_{n+1,1}-p_{0}^{*}\omega^{0}_{n+1,1}\circ\Psi^{\phi_{n}\otimes\cdots\otimes\phi_{1}}_{n+1,1}+\Psi^{\phi_{n}\otimes\cdots\otimes\phi_{1}}_{n+1,1}\circ p_{1}^{*}\omega^{0}_{n+1,1}=

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{n-1}(-1)^{n-k-1-|\phi_{n}\otimes\cdots\otimes\phi_{k+2}|}\Psi^{\phi_{n}\otimes\cdots\otimes\phi_{k+1}\circ\phi_{k}\otimes\cdots\otimes\phi_{1}}_{n+1,1}+

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{n}(-1)^{n-k-|\phi_{n}\otimes\cdots\otimes\phi_{k+1}|}\Psi^{\phi_{n}\otimes\cdots\otimes d\phi_{k}\otimes\cdots\otimes\phi_{1}}_{n+1,1}

Now applying
\int(-1)^{K(\Psi J(-)}\theta^{*}(P[-])^{*}(\Psi)
to both sides of the equation. For simplicity, we denote
\phi_{k}\otimes\cdots\otimes\phi_{l}
by
\phi_{k,l}
. We have

\displaystyle[

\displaystyle RH_{0}(C_{\phi})\cup RH_{0}(C_{\phi})+\hat{\delta}\operatorname{RH}_{0}(C_{\phi})]_{n+1,1}=

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{n-1}(-1)^{n-k-1-|\phi_{n,k+2}|}\operatorname{RH}_{n-1}(\phi_{n}\otimes\cdots\otimes\phi_{k+1}\circ\phi_{k}\otimes\cdots\otimes\phi_{1})

\displaystyle+\sum_{k=1}^{n-1}(-1)^{n-k-|\phi_{n,k+1}|}\operatorname{RH}_{n}(\phi_{n}\otimes\cdots\otimes d\phi_{k}\otimes\cdots\otimes\phi_{1})

By the matrix decomposition formulas in calculating the holonomy of cones in
[
AS14
, Section 3.5.2]
, we get

\displaystyle[RH_{0}(C_{\phi})\cup RH_{0}(C_{\phi})]_{n+1,1}=

\displaystyle\sum_{i+j=n}\operatorname{RH}_{0}(C_{\phi_{n,i+1}})_{j+1,1}\cup\operatorname{RH}_{0}(C_{\phi_{i,1}})_{i+1,1}[(j-\sum_{k=i+1}^{n}p_{k})]

\displaystyle+\operatorname{RH}_{0}(E_{n})\cup\operatorname{RH}_{0}(C_{\phi})_{n+1,1}+\operatorname{RH}_{0}(C_{\phi})_{n+1,1}\cup\operatorname{RH}_{0}(E_{0}[n-|\phi|])

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i+j=n}(-1)^{(j-\sum_{k=i+1}^{n}p_{k})}\operatorname{RH}_{j}(C_{\phi_{n,i+1}})\cup\operatorname{RH}_{i}(C_{\phi_{i,1}})

\displaystyle+\operatorname{RH}_{0}(E_{n})\cup\operatorname{RH}_{0}(C_{\phi})_{n+1,1}+(-1)^{n-|\phi|}\operatorname{RH}_{0}(C_{\phi})_{n+1,1}\cup\operatorname{RH}_{0}(E_{0})

By our construction,

\displaystyle D_{\mathsf{Loc}^{{\operatorname{dg}}}_{{\mathsf{C}}}(K_{\bullet})}\big(\operatorname{RH}_{n}(\phi)\big)=

\displaystyle\hat{\delta}\big(\operatorname{RH}_{n}(\phi)\big)+\operatorname{RH}_{0}(E_{n})\cup\operatorname{RH}_{n}(\phi)

\displaystyle+(-1)^{n-|\phi|}\operatorname{RH}_{n}(\phi)\cup\operatorname{RH}_{0}(E_{0})

Put the last two equations into the one above, we get

\displaystyle\Big(\sum_{i+j=n}(-1)^{(j-\sum_{k=i+1}^{n}p_{k})}\operatorname{RH}_{j}(C_{\phi_{n,i+1}})\cup\operatorname{RH}_{i}(C_{\phi_{i,1}})\Big)+D\big(\operatorname{RH}_{n}(\phi_{n,1})\big)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{n-1}(-1)^{n-k-1-|\phi_{n,k+2}|}\operatorname{RH}_{n-1}(\phi_{n}\otimes\cdots\otimes\phi_{k+1}\circ\phi_{k}\otimes\cdots\otimes\phi_{1})

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{k=1}^{n-1}(-1)^{n-k-|\phi_{n,k+1}|}\operatorname{RH}_{n}(\phi_{n}\otimes\cdots\otimes d\phi_{k}\otimes\cdots\otimes\phi_{1})

which is the
A_{\infty}
-relation for an
A_{\infty}
-functor between two dg-categories. Therefore,
\operatorname{RH}
is an
A_{\infty}
-functor.
∎

28.3.
Riemann-Hilbert correspondence

Theorem 28.7
.

The functor
\operatorname{RH}
is an
A_{\infty}
-quasi-equivalence.

First, we want to show
\operatorname{RH}
is
A_{\infty}
-quasi-fully-faithful
. Consider two objects
(E_{1}{\bullet},\mathbb{E}_{1})
,
(E_{1}{\bullet},\mathbb{E}_{1})\in\mathcal{P}_{{A}}
. The chain map

\operatorname{RH}_{1}:\mathcal{P}_{{A}}(E_{1},E_{2})\to\mathsf{Loc}^{\infty}_{\mathsf{Ch}_{k}}({\mathcal{F}})(\operatorname{RH}_{0}(E_{1}),\operatorname{RH}_{0}(E_{2}))

induces a map on the spectral sequence. In
E_{1}
-page, on
\mathcal{P}_{{A}}
side,
H^{\bullet}\big((E_{i},\mathbb{E}_{i}^{0})\big)
are vector bundles with flat connections, while on the other side
H^{\bullet}\big((\operatorname{RH}(E_{i}),\mathbb{E}_{i}^{0})\big)
are
{\mathcal{F}}
-local systems. In
E_{2}
-page, the map is

\displaystyle H^{\bullet}\bigg(M,\operatorname{Hom}\Big(H^{\bullet}\big((E_{1},\mathbb{E}_{1}^{0})\big),H^{\bullet}\big((E_{2},\mathbb{E}_{2}^{0})\big)\Big)\bigg)\to

\displaystyle H^{\bullet}\bigg(M,\operatorname{Hom}\Big(H^{\bullet}\big((\operatorname{RH}(E_{1}),\mathbb{E}_{1}^{0})\big),H^{\bullet}\big((\operatorname{RH}(E_{2}),\mathbb{E}_{2}^{0})\big)\Big)\bigg)

which is an isomorphism by the de Rham theorem for foliated local systems.
Next, we shall prove that
\operatorname{RH}
is
A_{\infty}
-essentially surjective
.

Let
F\in\mathsf{Loc}^{{\operatorname{dg}}}_{{\mathsf{C}}}({\mathcal{F}})
, we want to construct an object
(E^{\bullet},\nabla)\in\mathcal{P}_{{A}}
whose image under
\operatorname{RH}_{0}
is quasi-isomorphic to
F
. First notice that
\underline{{\mathbb{R}}}_{{\mathcal{F}}}
defines a representation of
\operatorname{Mon}_{\infty}({\mathcal{F}})
by previous section, which can be viewed as
\infty
-local system over
{\mathcal{F}}
. Regard
(M,\underline{{\mathbb{R}}}_{{\mathcal{F}}})
as a ringed space. Construct a complex of sheaves
(\underline{C}_{F}^{\bullet},D)
by

\big(\underline{C}_{F}(U)^{\bullet},D(U)\big)=\mathsf{Loc}^{\infty}_{\mathsf{Ch}_{k}}({\mathcal{F}})\big(\underline{{\mathbb{R}}}_{{\mathcal{F}}}|_{U},F|_{U}\big)^{\bullet}

We claim that
\underline{C}_{F}^{\bullet}
is soft. First notice that for
i>0
,
\underline{C}_{F}^{i}
is a
\underline{C}_{F}^{0}
-module by cup products on open sets. By definition,
\underline{C}_{F}^{0}=\{\phi:(\mathsf{Loc}^{\infty}_{\mathsf{Ch}_{k}}({\mathcal{F}}))_{0}\to\mathsf{Ch}_{{\mathbb{R}}}^{0}|\phi(x)\in\mathsf{Ch}_{{\mathbb{R}}}^{0}\big(\underline{{\mathbb{R}}}_{F}(x),F(x)\big)\}
which is a sheaf of discontinuous sections, hence soft. Therefore, all
\underline{C}_{F}^{i}
’s are soft. Recall that for two
\infty
-representation of a Lie
\infty
-groupoid, the
E_{1}
term of the spectral sequence is an ordinary representation. Hence,
\underline{C}_{F}^{\bullet}
is a perfect complex of sheaves. Let
\underline{{A}}^{\bullet}
be the sheaf of
{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}
sections of
{A}^{\bullet}=\mathop{\bigwedge\nolimits^{\!\bullet}}{\mathcal{F}}^{\vee}
.
\underline{{A}}^{0}={{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}(M)}
is flat over
\underline{{\mathbb{R}}}_{{\mathcal{F}}}
as
{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}
-rings since locally the module of smooth functions on
M
are
{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})
and the foliated functions are
{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{n-q})
where
q=\operatorname{codim}{\mathcal{F}}
, and
{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{n-q})\otimes_{\infty}{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{q})\simeq{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})
where
\otimes_{\infty}
is the tensor product for
{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}
-rings. Therefore,
\underline{C}_{F}^{\infty}=\underline{C}_{F}^{\bullet}\otimes_{\underline{{\mathbb{R}}}_{{\mathcal{F}}}}\underline{{A}}^{0}
is a sheaf of perfect
{A}^{0}
-modules.

Again by the flatness of
\underline{{A}}^{0}
is flat over
\underline{{\mathbb{R}}}_{{\mathcal{F}}}
. We have a quasi-isomorphism
(\underline{C}_{F}^{\bullet},D)\simeq(\underline{C}_{F}^{\infty}\otimes_{\underline{{A}}^{0}}\underline{{A}}^{\bullet},D\otimes 1+1\otimes d)
. We need the following proposition from Proposition 2.3.2, Exposè II, SGA6,
[
Ber+06
]
.

Remark 28.8
.

One of the core tool in previous proof is the flatness of
\underline{{A}}^{0}
over
\underline{{\mathbb{R}}}_{{\mathcal{F}}}
, or, in other words,
the flatness of
\underline{{A}}^{0}
over
H^{0}(\underline{{A}})
. We then expect a natural extension of our results to arbitrary
L_{\infty}
-algebroids
\mathfrak{g}
with associated foliation dga
A
, and
A^{0}
is flat over
H^{0}(A)
. A natural question will be, given any dga
A
(which presents some geometric object), when is
A^{0}
flat over
H^{0}
? Or we can consider an even more generalization, given a map of sheaves of algebras

d:A^{0}\to A^{1}

,
when
A^{0}
is flat over
H^{0}(A)=\ker(d)
?
We believe that this question is related to a more general phenomenon in noncommutative geometry.

Proposition 28.9
.

Let
(X,\underline{\mathcal{S}}_{X})
be a ringed space, where
X
is compact and
\underline{\mathcal{S}}_{X}
is a soft sheaf of rings. Then

(1)

The global section functor

\Gamma:\mathsf{Mod}_{\underline{\mathcal{S}}_{X}}\to\mathsf{Mod}_{\underline{\mathcal{S}}_{X}(X)}

is exact and establishes an equivalence of categories between the category of sheaves of right
\underline{\mathcal{S}}_{X}
-modules and the category of right modules over the global sections
\underline{\mathcal{S}}_{X}(X)
of
\underline{\mathcal{S}}_{X}
.

(2)

If
\underline{F}\in\mathsf{Mod}_{\underline{\mathcal{S}}_{X}}
locally has finite resolutions by finitely generated free
\underline{\mathcal{S}}_{X}
-modules, then
\Gamma(X,\underline{F})
has a finite resolution by finitely generated projective modules.

(3)

The derived category of perfect complexes of sheaves
\mathsf{D_{Perf}}(\mathsf{Mod}_{\underline{\mathcal{S}}_{X}})
is equivalent to the derived category of perfect complexes of modules
\mathsf{D_{Perf}}(\mathsf{Mod}_{\underline{\mathcal{S}}_{X}(X)})
.

By this theorem, there is a (strict) perfect complex of
{A}^{0}
-modules
(E,\mathbb{E}^{0})
and a quasi-isomorphism
e^{0}:(E^{\bullet},\mathbb{E}^{0})\to(F^{\bullet},\mathbb{F}^{0})=(\Gamma(M,\underline{C}_{F}^{\infty}),D)
. We shall follow the argument of Theorem 3.2.7 of
[
Blo05
]
to construct the higher components
\mathbb{E}^{i}
of
{\mathbb{Z}}
-connection along with the higher components of a morphism
e^{i}
.

On
F^{\bullet}
, we have a
{\mathbb{Z}}
-connection

\mathbb{F}=D\otimes 1+1\otimes d:F^{\bullet}\to F^{\bullet}\otimes_{{A}^{0}}{A}^{\bullet}

. The idea is to transfer this
{\mathbb{Z}}
-connection to
E^{\bullet}
which is compatible with the quasi-isomorphism on
H^{0}
’s. Note that we have an induced connection

\mathbb{H}^{k}:H^{k}(F^{\bullet},\mathbb{F}^{0})\to H^{k}(F^{\bullet},\mathbb{F}^{0})\otimes_{{A}^{0}}{A}^{1}

for each
k
. First we will transfer this connection to a connection on
H^{k}(E^{\bullet},\mathbb{E}^{0})
, and we have the following commutative diagram
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{k}(E^{\bullet},\mathbb{E}^{0})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{k}(E^{\bullet},\mathbb{E}^{0})\otimes_{{A}^{0}}{A}^{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{k}(F^{\bullet},\mathbb{F}^{0})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{k}(F^{\bullet},\mathbb{F}^{0})\otimes_{{A}^{0}}{A}^{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{H}^{k}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e^{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e^{0}\otimes 1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{H}^{k}}

Note that
e^{0}\otimes 1
is a quasi-isomorphism since
{A}^{\bullet}
is flat over
{A}^{0}
. We need the following lemma.

Lemma 28.10
.

Given a bounded complex of finitely generated projective
{A}^{0}
-modules
(E^{\bullet},\mathbb{E}^{0})
with connections
\mathbb{H}^{k}:H^{k}(F^{\bullet},\mathbb{F}^{0})\to H^{k}(F^{\bullet},\mathbb{F}^{0})\otimes_{{A}^{0}}{A}^{1}
for each
k
, there exists connections

\tilde{\mathbb{H}}^{k}:E^{k}\to E^{k}\otimes_{{A}^{0}}{A}^{1}

lifting
\mathbb{H}^{k}
, i.e.

\tilde{\mathbb{H}}^{k}\mathbb{E}^{0}=(\mathbb{E}^{0}\otimes 1)\tilde{\mathbb{H}}^{k}

for each
k

and the connection induced on the cohomology is
\mathbb{H}^{k}
.

Proof.

This is Lemma 3.2.8 in
[
Blo05
]
and Lemma 4.6 in
[
BS14
]
. Since
E^{\bullet}
is bounded, let
[N,M]
be its magnitude. Pick some arbitrary connection
\nabla
on
E^{M}
. Consider the following diagram whose rows are exact

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E^{M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{M}(E^{\bullet},\mathbb{E}^{0})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E^{M}\otimes_{{A}^{0}}{A}^{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{M}(E^{\bullet},\mathbb{E}^{0})\otimes_{{A}^{0}}{A}^{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nabla}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{H}^{M}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j\otimes 1}

Here
\theta=\mathbb{H}^{M}\circ j-(j\otimes 1)\circ\nabla
is
{A}^{0}
-linear and
j\otimes 1
is surjective. Now, by the projectivity of
E^{M}
, we can lift
\theta
to a
\tilde{\theta}:E^{M}\to E^{M}\otimes_{{A}^{0}}{A}^{1}
such that
(j\otimes 1)\tilde{\theta}=\theta
. Let
\tilde{\mathbb{H}}^{k}=\nabla+\tilde{\theta}
. Now replace
\nabla
by
\tilde{\mathbb{H}}^{k}
and the above diagram still commutes.

Now pick some arbitrary connection
\nabla_{M-1}
on
E^{M-1}
. Note that
\mathbb{E}^{0}\nabla_{M-1}={\mathbb{H}}^{M-1}\mathbb{E}^{0}=0
does not necessarily hold. Set
\mu={\mathbb{H}}^{M-1}\mathbb{E}^{0}-\mathbb{E}^{0}\nabla_{M-1}
, then
\mu
is
{A}^{0}
-linear.
\operatorname{Im}\mu\subset\operatorname{Im}\mathbb{E}^{0}\otimes 1
since
\operatorname{Im}{\mathbb{H}}^{M-1}\mathbb{E}^{0}\subset\operatorname{Im}\mathbb{E}^{0}\otimes 1
as
{\mathbb{H}}
is a lift of
\mathbb{H}
. Now by the projectivity we can lift this map to a
\tilde{\mu}:E^{M-1}\to E^{M-1}\otimes_{{A}^{0}}{A}^{1}
such that
(\mathbb{E}^{0}\otimes 1)\circ\tilde{\mu}=\mu
. Now set
\tilde{\mathbb{H}}^{M-1}=\nabla_{M-1}+\tilde{\mu}
, then
(\mathbb{E}^{0}\otimes 1)\tilde{\mathbb{H}}^{M-1}=\tilde{\mathbb{H}}^{M-1}\mathbb{E}^{0}
. We have the following diagram
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E^{N}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E^{N+1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E^{M-1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E^{M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E^{N}\otimes_{{A}^{0}}{A}^{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E^{N+1}\otimes_{{A}^{0}}{A}^{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E^{M-1}\otimes_{{A}^{0}}{A}^{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E^{M}\otimes_{{A}^{0}}{A}^{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{E}^{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{E}^{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{E}^{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{E}^{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nabla_{M-1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mu}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{\mathbb{H}}_{M}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{E}^{0}\otimes 1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{E}^{0}\otimes 1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{E}^{0}\otimes 1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{E}^{0}\otimes 1}

Now we continue in the same fashion and construct all
\tilde{\mathbb{H}}^{k}
with
(\mathbb{E}^{0}\otimes 1)\tilde{\mathbb{H}}^{k}=\tilde{\mathbb{H}}^{k}\mathbb{E}^{0}
for all
k
.
∎

Now let’s continue the proof of the main theorem. Set
\tilde{\mathbb{E}}^{1}=(-1)^{k}\tilde{\mathbb{H}}_{k}
on
E^{k}
for each
k
. By our construction

\mathbb{E}^{0}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}^{1}+\tilde{\mathbb{E}}^{1}\mathbb{E}^{0}=0

but
e^{0}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}^{1}=\mathbb{F}^{1}e^{0}
might not hold. We will correct this by modifying
\tilde{\mathbb{E}}^{1}
. Consider the map
\psi=e^{0}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}^{1}-\mathbb{F}^{1}e^{0}:E^{\bullet}\to F^{\bullet}\otimes_{{A}^{0}}{A}^{1}
. It is easy to verify that
\psi
is
{A}^{0}
-linear and a map of chain complexes. Now we have the following diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(E^{\bullet}\otimes_{{A}^{0}}{A}^{1},\mathbb{E}^{0}\otimes 1)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E^{\bullet}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(F^{\bullet}\otimes_{{A}^{0}}{A}^{1},\mathbb{F}^{0}\otimes 1)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e^{0}\otimes 1}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{\psi}}

here
e^{0}\otimes 1
is a quasi-isomorphism since
e^{0}
is a homotopy equivalence.
\tilde{\psi}
is a lift of
\psi
and there exists a homotopy
e^{1}:E^{\bullet}\to F^{\bullet-1}\otimes_{{A}^{0}}{A}^{1}

\phi-(e^{0}\otimes 1)\tilde{\psi}=(e^{1}\mathbb{E}^{0}+\mathbb{F}^{0}e^{1})

Now let
\mathbb{E}^{1}=\tilde{\mathbb{E}}
. We have

\mathbb{E}^{0}\mathbb{E}^{1}+\mathbb{E}^{1}\mathbb{E}^{0}=0

and

e^{1}\mathbb{E}^{0}+\mathbb{F}^{0}e^{1}=e^{0}\mathbb{E}^{1}+\mathbb{F}^{1}e^{0}

Now we have constructed the first two components
\mathbb{E}^{0}
and
\mathbb{E}^{1}
of the
{\mathbb{Z}}
-connection, and the first two components
e^{0}
and
e^{1}
of the quasi-isomorphism
E^{\bullet}\otimes_{{A}^{0}}{A}^{\bullet}\to F^{\bullet}\otimes_{{A}^{0}}{A}^{\bullet}
.

Now let’s proceed to construct the rest components. Consider the mapping cone
C_{e^{0}}^{\bullet}
of
e^{0}
, i.e.
C_{e^{0}}^{\bullet}=E[1]^{\bullet}\oplus F^{\bullet}
. Now let
\mathbb{L}^{0}
be defined as the matrix

\begin{pmatrix}\mathbb{E}^{0}[1]&0\\
e^{0}[1]&\mathbb{F}^{0}\end{pmatrix}

Define
\mathbb{L}^{1}
as the matrix

\begin{pmatrix}\mathbb{E}^{1}[1]&0\\
e^{1}[1]&\mathbb{F}^{1}\end{pmatrix}

Now
\mathbb{L}^{0}\mathbb{L}^{0}=0
and
[\mathbb{L}^{0},\mathbb{L}^{1}]=0
by construction. Let

D=\mathbb{L}^{1}\mathbb{L}^{1}+\begin{pmatrix}0&0\\
\mathbb{F}^{2}e^{0}&[\mathbb{F}^{0},\mathbb{F}^{2}]\end{pmatrix}

It is easy to check that
D
is
{A}
-linear,

(1)

[\mathbb{L}^{0},D]=0
,

(2)

D|_{0\oplus F^{\bullet}}
.

Note that
(C_{e^{0}}^{\bullet},\mathbb{L}^{0})
is acyclic since it is a mapping cone of a quasi-isomorphism. By flatness of
{A}^{\bullet}
over
{A}^{0}
,
(C_{e^{0}}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{A}^{0}}{A}^{2},\mathbb{L}^{0}\otimes 1)
is also acyclic. In addition,

\operatorname{Hom}^{\bullet}_{{A}^{0}}\big((E^{\bullet},\mathbb{E}^{0}),(C_{e^{0}}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{A}^{0}}{A}^{2},\mathbb{L}^{0}\otimes 1)\big)

is a subcomplex of

\operatorname{Hom}^{\bullet}_{{A}^{0}}\big(C_{e^{0}}^{\bullet},(C_{e^{0}}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{A}^{0}}{A}^{2},[\mathbb{L}^{0},-])\big)

How
D\in\operatorname{Hom}^{\bullet}_{{A}^{0}}\big((E^{\bullet},\mathbb{E}^{0}),(C_{e^{0}}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{A}^{0}}{A}^{2},\mathbb{L}^{0}\otimes 1)\big)
is a cycle, so there exists some
\tilde{\mathbb{L}}^{2}\in\operatorname{Hom}^{\bullet}_{{A}^{0}}\big((E^{\bullet},\mathbb{E}^{0}),(C_{e^{0}}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{A}^{0}}{A}^{2},\mathbb{L}^{0}\otimes 1)\big)
such that
-D=[\mathbb{L}^{0},\tilde{\mathbb{L}}^{2}]
. Define
\mathbb{L}^{2}
by

\mathbb{L}^{2}=\tilde{\mathbb{L}}^{2}+\begin{pmatrix}0&0\\
0&\mathbb{F}^{2}\end{pmatrix}

We have

\displaystyle[\mathbb{L}^{0},\mathbb{L}^{2}]=

\displaystyle\Big[\mathbb{L}^{0},\tilde{\mathbb{L}}^{2}+\begin{pmatrix}0&0\\
0&\mathbb{F}^{2}\end{pmatrix}\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-D+\Big[\mathbb{L}^{0},\begin{pmatrix}0&0\\
0&\mathbb{F}^{2}\end{pmatrix}\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\mathbb{L}^{1}\mathbb{L}^{1}

Therefore we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{L}^{0}\mathbb{L}^{2}+\mathbb{L}^{1}\mathbb{L}^{1}+\mathbb{L}^{2}\mathbb{L}^{0}=0

Following this pattern, we continue by setting

D=\mathbb{L}^{1}\mathbb{L}^{2}+\mathbb{L}^{2}\mathbb{L}^{1}+\begin{pmatrix}0&0\\
\mathbb{F}^{3}e^{0}&[\mathbb{F}^{0},\mathbb{F}^{3}]\end{pmatrix}

Again it is easy to verify that
D
is
{A}^{0}
-linear, and

(1)

[\mathbb{L}^{0},D]=0
,

(2)

D|_{0\oplus F^{\bullet}}
.

By the same reasoning as before, so there exists some
\tilde{\mathbb{L}}^{3}\in\operatorname{Hom}^{\bullet}_{{A}^{0}}\big((E^{\bullet},\mathbb{E}^{0}),(C_{e^{0}}^{\bullet}\otimes_{{A}^{0}}{A}^{3},\mathbb{L}^{0}\otimes 1)\big)
such that
-D=[\mathbb{L}^{0},\tilde{\mathbb{L}}^{3}]
. Define
\mathbb{L}^{3}
by

\mathbb{L}^{3}=\tilde{\mathbb{L}}^{3}+\begin{pmatrix}0&0\\
0&\mathbb{F}^{3}\end{pmatrix}

By easy verification we get
\sum_{i=0}^{3}\mathbb{L}^{i}\mathbb{L}^{3-i}=0
.

Now suppose we have constructed
\mathbb{L}^{0},\cdots,\mathbb{L}^{n}
which satisfy

\sum_{i=0}^{k}\mathbb{L}^{i}\mathbb{L}^{k-i}=0

for
k=0,\cdots,n
.
Then we define

D=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{L}^{i}\mathbb{L}^{n+1-i}+\begin{pmatrix}0&0\\
\mathbb{F}^{n+1}e^{0}&[\mathbb{F}^{0},\mathbb{F}^{n+1}]\end{pmatrix}

Again we have
D
is
{A}^{0}
-linear, and

(1)

[\mathbb{L}^{0},D]=0
,

(2)

D|_{0\oplus F^{\bullet}}
.

We can continue the inductive construction of
\mathbb{L}
to get a
{\mathbb{Z}}
-connection satisfying
\mathbb{L}\mathbb{L}=0
. Then we have constructed both components of the
{\mathbb{Z}}
-connection and the morphism from
(E^{\bullet},\mathbb{E})
to
(F^{\bullet},\mathbb{F})
.

Now we have shown that
\operatorname{RH}
is
A_{\infty}
-essentially surjective. Therefore,
\operatorname{RH}
is an
A_{\infty}
-quasi-equivalence.

Corollary 28.11
.

The
\infty
-category
\mathtt{Loc}^{\infty}_{\mathsf{Ch}_{k}}{\mathcal{F}}
is equivalent to the
\infty
-category
\mathtt{Mod}_{{A}}^{{\operatorname{coh}}}
, for
{A}=\operatorname{CE}({\mathcal{F}})
.

28.4.
Integrate
\infty
-representations of
L_{\infty}
-algebroids

\operatorname{RH}
is a functor from cohesive modules over the foliation dga
A
, which can also be regarded as cohesive modules over the foliation Lie algebroid
T{\mathcal{F}}
.
It is not hard to generalize the
\operatorname{RH}
as a functor from cohesive modules over any
L_{\infty}
-algebroids, where we only need to refine the iterated integrals to the corresponding vector bundles over the foliations, i.e. we only integrate along leaves of the (singular) foliations generated by
L_{\infty}
-algebroids. On the other hand, the monodromy
\infty
-groupoid of a perfect singular foliation
{\mathcal{F}}
is the truncation of the integration of the
L_{\infty}
-algebroid
\mathfrak{g}
associated
{\mathcal{F}}
. Therefore, given a perfect singular foliation
{\mathcal{F}}
with its associated
L_{\infty}
-algebroid
\mathfrak{g}
, we get the following commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathfrak{g}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Mon}(\mathfrak{g})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\operatorname{Mon}({\mathcal{F}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{Mod}^{{\operatorname{coh}}}_{\mathfrak{g}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{Loc}^{\infty}(\mathfrak{g})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathsf{Loc}^{\infty}({\mathcal{F}})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\int}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Rep}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Rep}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Rep}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{RH}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau}

where
\tau
denotes the truncation functor.
A natural question to ask is when
\operatorname{RH}
will be an
A_{\infty}
-quasi-equivalence, or induce an
\infty
-equivalence at the
\infty
-category level. This will be studied in a future paper.
